# Empirical Tensions

Places where sources in the wiki disagree, or where a claim is asserted more strongly than its evidence supports. One row per tension. Updated at every ingest that creates or resolves one.

**Status key:** `LIVE` — unresolved · `LEANING` — evidence favours one side · `RESOLVED` — settled, kept for the record.

> **Note (lint pass, 212 ingests).** **T210, T213 and T214 are one defect, discovered three times.** Each was logged as a disagreement about a benchmark's human number; none of them is. A human baseline is a protocol — pool, attempt budget, aggregation order, exclusion rule, item-pool identity — and each of the three is a different one of those parameters left unstated. The specification, and the wiki's reporting convention, are at [[wiki/concepts/human-baseline.md]]. Rows that are instances of one missing specification should be marked as such rather than re-argued per benchmark.

| # | Tension | Position A | Position B | Where it bites | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | **Does the implementation level carry computational content?** | Only Marr levels 1–2 (computation, algorithm) need transfer; biophysics is substrate detail, deliberately excluded (Hassabis et al. 2017). Lake et al. 2017 push the same position further: understand the "software" before the "hardware", because what we know about the brain is not clear-cut enough to constrain a cognitive theory in either direction — and a hypothetical biological mechanism should be doubted if it is *cognitively* implausible | Spiking timing, energy constraints and one-shot synaptic change are computational, not incidental — the position of neuromorphic and biophysical modelling, which the same paper sets itself against. Schmidgall et al. 2023 argue it directly: spikes are theoretically shown to carry *more* information than rate codes despite being binary and sparse; energy is a first-order barrier at scale and on edge devices; the i.i.d. timeless data assumption "does not reflect physical reality" ([[wiki/entities/spiking-neural-networks.md]]) | Decides whether a reasoning architecture may be specified purely functionally, or whether the substrate constrains what is expressible | `LEANING B` — B now has a controlled test rather than an argument: Kawahara & Fujisawa 2025 rebuild an architecture the wiki already holds at the computational level ([[wiki/entities/tolman-eichenbaum-machine.md]]) in spiking neurons, and three mechanisms *inexpressible* in its functional specification turn out to be necessary for its central representation — grid cells fall from 59.6% to 0.00% without a neuromodulatory input gain, to 10.1% without STDP, to 25.6% without theta-phase inhibition — while the spiking version additionally produces phase precession and phase locking, which the rate model has no vocabulary for ([[wiki/entities/spiking-tem.md]]). B also has the sharpest *quantitative* case in the wiki, and it is older than the debate: an integrate-and-fire model of the barn-owl laminar nucleus fires with **20–25 µs** precision from 250 µs postsynaptic potentials, `τ_m ≈ 100 µs` and 40 µs input jitter, with the precision almost independent of `τ_m` — so temporal content is not merely present, it is *not bounded by* the speed of the components, and the variable carrying the answer (arrival-time difference) has no rate-level description (Gerstner et al. 1996; [[wiki/concepts/temporal-coding.md]]). The residual defence of A: the rate TEM obtains the same code *without* any of these, so they may be repairs for costs the substrate itself imposes rather than computational content it adds. **A now has two further data, both from inside the neuromorphic literature.** (i) Whole trained rate networks — multilayer perceptrons, convolutional nets, deep belief networks, an LSTM — are routinely *converted* to spikes by copying the weights and reading activations as firing rates, with accuracy preserved up to CIFAR-10 and ImageNet; on those tasks the functional specification was evidently complete, and spiking bought energy, not content (T231). (ii) subLSTM replaces the LSTM's multiplicative gates with subtractive ones implementable by cortical lateral inhibition and matches — never beats — the original on sequential MNIST and a language benchmark, so at least one piece of the substrate is confirmed detail. The two sides are reconcilable only if the implementation level matters for **what is represented** (grid codes, phase precession, temporal difference variables) and not for **how a gate or a unit is computed** — which is a sharper form of the question than either position currently states (Tavanaei et al. 2019) |
| T2 | **Is the fast level a separate store or recurrent activity?** | Complementary learning systems: a second anatomical system with a sparse conjunctive code, coupled by replay | Meta-RL: the fast learner is the *activity dynamics* of a network whose weights are fixed — no second store required. Reinforced by Schmidgall et al. 2023: in-context learning changes activations not weights, yet parameter-sharing in a meta-learner yields the *interpretation of activations as weights*, and self-attention's outer/inner products can be cast as learned weight updates that implement gradient descent | Determines what fast **M** is built from, and whether one-shot binding and fast policy adaptation are the same mechanism | `LIVE` — but possibly **dissolving**: if activations and weights are interconvertible under parameter sharing, the question is a change of basis, and the real variables are the fast level's degrees of freedom and decay timescale ([[wiki/concepts/meta-optimized-plasticity.md]]) |
| T3 | **Is backpropagation biologically realizable?** | Not as stated: it needs weight transport and non-local error signals, neither of which cortex provides | Feedback alignment, predictive-coding and energy-based nets approximate it with fixed random feedback and purely local updates, with correspondences to spike-timing-dependent plasticity | Decides whether the slow-**W** meta-graph learner can exist in a neural substrate, and whether "biologically plausible" still rules anything out | `LEANING` — toward realizable approximations, but **narrowed** by Schmidgall et al. 2023: plain feedback alignment is suboptimal at ImageNet scale, and only sign-symmetry — which still transports sign information — matches backpropagation on large data. The surviving approximation is the least biologically defensible one. **And the two objections must now be scored separately** (Whittington & Bogacz 2019): the four models that most convincingly answer *non-locality* — contrastive learning, continuous update, predictive coding, dendritic error, all reaching ~1.7–3% MNIST error with purely Hebbian updates — every one of them **assumes symmetric feedforward/feedback weights** and relies on its plasticity rule to maintain the symmetry. So the family that solves locality does not touch weight transport, and the family that solves weight transport (sign-symmetry) transports the load-bearing part. No model currently answers both. **A partial escape, asserted not measured:** in predictive coding the reciprocal prediction and error synapses obey the same pre×post rule with the roles swapped, so approximate symmetry is a fixed point the two synapses reach independently rather than an assumption — which, if it holds under nonlinearity at depth, converts the symmetry assumption into a *consequence* of the locality solution (talk-nd-brain-learning-algorithm, **(tentative)**) **And the question may be malformed** (Whittington & Bogacz 2017): the predictive-coding equivalence is not approximate-with-unknown-error but exact in the limit `Σ^(0) → ∞`, which is the limit that drives error-node activity to zero and so *removes* the spiking implementation. Realizability and equivalence trade off along a single parameter, so "is backpropagation realizable" has no fixed answer — see T119. |
| T4 | **Do abstract structural codes exist in the brain?** | Grid-like periodic codes appear during abstract categorization in humans; periodic encoding may be a general hallmark of knowledge organization Epstein et al. 2017 adds detail rather than a second report: the same morphed-stimulus result also shows the sixfold signal in ventromedial prefrontal cortex, with its strength tracking task performance | The same review states we *lack direct evidence* for codes that are invariant to objects and code relational information, and flags the grid-like claim as requiring much further work. And the nearest independent non-spatial map is *not* periodic — a social power × affiliation space is coded as hippocampal vector *angle* plus posterior-cingulate *magnitude* — so the general claim that survives is "maps outside space", not "grids outside space" ([[wiki/concepts/cognitive-map.md]]) | The wiki's `g` (structural code) has one contested biological anchor; if it fails, the factorization must be justified computationally alone | `LIVE` |
| T5 | **Was neuroscience causally responsible for the transferred mechanisms?** | Attention, replay, dropout, external memory and elastic weight consolidation are documented imports; TD learning and PDP have explicit historical lineage | Several were plausibly reachable by engineering alone and the inspiration was acknowledged only retrospectively — the paper itself says inspiration is often drawn "without explicit acknowledgment" | Sets how much predictive weight the biology deserves when choosing what to build next; if the record is retrofitted, the "look at the brain" prior is weaker than claimed | `LIVE` |
| T6 | **What is out-of-distribution failure a failure *of*?** | A flat learner fits the mixture `E_θ[p(obs\|θ)]`, a distribution no instance follows — o.o.d. failure is a missing level of hierarchy ([[wiki/concepts/latent-graph-discovery.md]]) | Networks generalise o.o.d. *prodigiously* — abstract patterns confidently classified as guitars, vast image sets mapped to object categories. The failure is directional: chosen features create insensitivity to all others, so the model extrapolates along the wrong axes (Geirhos et al. 2020) | Prescription differs: *add hierarchy* (fit the right object) vs. *add bias* (choose the right invariances). If B is right, a correctly hierarchical model with the wrong feature preference still fails | `LIVE` — likely complementary; A explains why the fitted object is wrong, B why the extrapolation is confident |
| T7 | **Are backpropagation-derived local rules worth pursuing at all?** | Yes — locality is what makes deployment-time learning, spiking substrates and neuromorphic hardware possible, and each new constraint has generated a new algorithm (feedback alignment → e-prop → neuromodulated credit assignment, each improving on the last) | No — measured against backpropagation through time they show **worse and more variable generalization** that does not close by scaling the step size, because the gradient approximation is poorly aligned with the true gradient. A local *approximation* of an optimization process is fundamentally sub-par, and Schmidgall et al. 2023 pose the abandonment question themselves. Bartunov et al. 2018 add the scaling half of the case: on ImageNet with locally-connected (weight-sharing-free) nets, **every** target-propagation variant sits at chance (98–99% top-1) and feedback alignment reaches 93.08% against backpropagation's 71.43% — the family's validation had all been done on a dataset (MNIST) that does not discriminate between the methods | Decides whether the wiki's slow-**W** learner should pursue local gradient approximations at all, or restrict local rules to the jobs backpropagation cannot do (deployment-time adaptation, delayed sparse reward, spiking substrates) | `LEANING` — toward B *for the approximation programme specifically*. Rules that never approximated a gradient ([[wiki/concepts/synaptic-plasticity.md]]) are untouched by the result, and no experiment separates "intrinsic to approximation" from "artefact of the approximations tested" |
| T8 | **Is compositional machinery innate, or acquired from an external symbolic system?** | **Acquired.** Infants are endowed only with language-*learning* capacities (sensitivity to speech and prosody, an abstract content-word/function-word distinction); the syntax and compositional semantics of the acquired natural language are what break the modularity of core systems. An innate language of thought is rejected because it overgenerates — the infant could not know which concepts are useful, which apply, or which propositions are true (Spelke 2022) | **Innate.** Core-system outputs are already in a language-of-thought format and a general composer sits on top. The language route undergenerates in both directions: core concepts are taken for granted and so rarely uttered, while logical operators, tense/aspect and modals have no core-system counterpart. And the conceded escape hatch — that home-signing deaf children reinvent the needed features of language — makes those features indistinguishable from a pre-linguistic language of thought (Revencu & Csibra 2023) | Decides whether a reasoning architecture must *contain* a composer or may expect one to emerge from training on a symbolic corpus — i.e. whether a large language model is a candidate answer to gap G21 or only to G22 | `LIVE` — neither side has a criterion that separates productive composition from associative combination, so no evidence currently discriminates them |
| T9 | **Are installed priors unitary modules or bundles of independently violable constraints?** | **Unitary / all-or-none.** An entity that violates cohesion loses the continuity and persistence expectations too — infants stop expecting a non-cohesive entity to move continuously or survive occlusion (Huntley-Fenner et al. 2002). All-or-none operation is what distinguishes a core system from a graded composite (Spelke 2022) | **Graded.** Principles dissociate: adults accept teleportation (continuity without cohesion) and remote controls (objecthood without action-on-contact); infants explore *selectively by violation type*, banging solidity-violating objects on the table (Stahl & Feigenson 2015), which a unitary system cannot produce (Revencu & Csibra 2023) | Sets whether a prior is one gate or *n* gates. The graded form yields a **violation-typed prediction error** — an intrinsic-motivation signal pointing at the specific principle that broke — where the unitary form can only report "not an object" | `LEANING` — toward graded on the evidence cited, and the graded form is the better architecture either way |
| T10 | **Is an innate prior architecturally necessary, or just knowledge delivered early?** | **Necessary.** Core knowledge is a bank of domain-specific, entry-gated systems that guide learning top-down from the start; without them the learner has no `g`-side prior at all and the intended graph is unidentifiable ([[wiki/concepts/core-knowledge.md]], gap G16) | **Not necessary.** For an ideal inductor, knowledge `z` presented as the *first observation* replaces `K(µ)` by `K(µ\|z)` in the convergence bounds; as long as the code interpreting `z` is `O(1)`, the agent absorbs it within a few cycles — so "the boundary between implementation and training is unsharp", and innateness is a claim about *timing*, not architecture (Hutter 2000) | Decides whether priors must be built into the architecture or can be supplied as data/context. If B holds at realistic scale, prompting and pretraining are substitutes for architectural priors; if A holds, they are not | `LIVE` — B's equivalence is asymptotic and assumes an `O(1)` interpreter and an agent that can already compress arbitrary input, which is precisely what a bounded learner lacks. The same source supplies A's strongest argument: if evolution encoded something `Ω`-like and incompressible into the genome, no `O(1)` program reaches human competence and the missing part is exactly the installed prior |
| T11 | **Is an installed prior a hard constraint or a strong default?** | **Hard.** Core principles operate all-or-none and *delete* candidate structures rather than reweighting them: an entity that violates cohesion is not admitted to the object system at all, and non-object substances (sand, liquids) never enter it ([[wiki/concepts/core-knowledge.md]]) | **Defeasible.** The same principles are false at the smallest and largest scales — objects are neither cohesive nor continuous, space is not Euclidean or 3-D — and they are demonstrably overridden: by physics and non-Euclidean geometry in adults, by learning to count in preschoolers, by learning about eating and breathing in children's agent concepts (Spelke & Kinzler 2007) | Decides how a prior is implemented. A hard constraint has no path to a domain where it is wrong; a soft regularizer is washed out by data and buys no sample efficiency. The override channel in every documented case is an **external symbol system**, which is also T8's candidate for composition | `LEANING` — toward defeasible, with the open question being the *mechanism* of override, not its existence; note the two positions are the same source's, at the scale of action vs. outside it |
| T12 | **Are the conceptual primitives of infancy installed, or learned from sensorimotor prediction in the first months?** | **Installed.** Core knowledge is a bank of domain-specific systems present at birth, individuating entities by entry conditions and carrying principles (cohesion, continuity, contact) that no amount of sensorimotor correlation would supply ([[wiki/concepts/core-knowledge.md]]) | **Learned, but early.** Butz 2016 takes the *same* evidence — newborns anticipating thumb-to-mouth contact, the rooting reflex suppressed for self-touch, goal-directed reaching in the first months — as showing that a progressively accurate predictive model of one's *own body* is already in place, learned in utero. "Innate" conceptual primitives are then the earliest abstractions over that model, and what is genuinely installed is not content but three *typed prediction channels* plus a segmentation bias ([[wiki/concepts/predictive-coding-free-energy.md]]) | Decides what an architecture must ship with. A: domain-specific content and entry gates (gap G23). B: content-free structural biases plus an embodied data stream — which relocates the whole cost onto the data problem, since developmental-scale self-motivated sensorimotor experience is exactly what no robot can supply | `LIVE, and the discriminating experiment has now been half-run` — Garrido et al. 2025 supply a system with **no** typed channels, no embodiment and no installed content, only prediction in a learned representation space on passive natural video, and it develops the object principles it is tested on: 98% on IntPhys, `g` 8–11 on permanence / continuity / shape constancy over an untrained null, ≥ naive humans on every IntPhys property, at 115M parameters on 128 h of unique video ([[wiki/concepts/violation-of-expectation.md]]). This moves B's burden without discharging it — the result shows a sufficient learned route exists for **two** of the object system's three principles, and the third, **contact**, is the one at chance (solidity, collision). It is also a machine result and not a claim about infants. B's remaining debt is unchanged in kind: nothing shows the human system took this route |
| T13 | **Is long-term potentiation the mechanism behind behavioural learning?** | **Yes** — long-term potentiation and spike-timing-dependent plasticity are the standard biological warrant for the Hebbian rule family, cited as biologically supported (Bi & Poo 2001) and used as the substrate story for fast **M** ([[wiki/concepts/synaptic-plasticity.md]]) | **The timescales do not match.** LTP's critical interstimulus interval is orders of magnitude shorter than behaviourally relevant intervals, and manipulating interstimulus and intertrial intervals together shows **no critical interval exists**; behaviour persists for weeks or months while LTP decays to baseline in days (Power et al. 1997); learned behaviour is rapidly reacquired after extinction (Bouton 2004) with no matching facilitation in LTP (de Jonge & Racine 1985). Implementing structured model building with purely Hebbian mechanisms would be "especially challenging" (Gallistel & Matzel 2013, via Lake et al. 2017) | Decides whether "biologically supported" is an argument for a local coactivity rule at all. If B holds, the plasticity family keeps its engineering case (deployment-time writes, locality, spiking substrates) and loses its biological one — and the wiki's fast **M** needs a different substrate story | `LEANING A` on the interval sub-claim — **behavioral timescale synaptic plasticity supplies the missing mechanism**: a seconds-wide eligibility window gated by a dendritic plateau, producing a reproducible place field from one traversal, measured in CA1 and CA3 in vivo (Bittner et al. 2017; Li et al. 2023; Gonzalez et al. 2023, via Liao & Losonczy 2024). So the family is no longer restricted to a 20 ms kernel and the interval mismatch stops being decisive. B's persistence and reacquisition-after-extinction sub-claims are untouched, and BTSP inherits its own version of the problem: the molecular substrate of the seconds-long trace is unidentified (αCaMKII is a candidate) |
| T14 | **Are structured representations real, or emergent descriptions of sub-symbolic processes?** | **Emergent.** Graphs, grammars, rules, objects, structural descriptions and programs are "useful yet misleading metaphors" — more epiphenomenal than real, emergent properties of sub-symbolic processes; the parallel-distributed-processing strategy is to train a generic network and add ingredients only when forced, and it has produced networks behaving *as if* they had past-tense rules, balance-beam rules, or a tree of living kinds (McClelland et al. 2010, reported in Lake et al. 2017) | **Explicit.** Human-level performance requires explicit representations of objects, identity, relations and causal structure; the caption failures (objects right, relations wrong) and the sample-efficiency gaps (924 h vs. minutes; 23% vs. 4% error at matched pre-training) are read as evidence that the emergent version is not the same representation despite matched behaviour on the trained task (Lake et al. 2017) | Decides whether the wiki should specify `g`, edges and node types as architecture, or train a generic model and look for them afterwards. Note the two sides make the *same* prediction on any single trained task and differ only on transfer, re-goaling and query richness — so the tension is settled by [[wiki/architectural-gaps.md]] G17/G28 instruments or not at all | `LIVE` — and this is the wiki's clearest case of Morgan's Canon biting on both sides: matched behaviour licenses no inference about the representation in *either* direction |
| T15 | **Is training data a design lever or an anchor?** | **A lever, co-equal with the other three.** The control surface is architecture, training data, loss and optimizer; the data lever is the one that blocks a specific shortcut *by construction* (adversarial vulnerability, texture bias), and it is the only one whose effect on identifiability is provable — a single environment cannot distinguish a causal edge from a correlational one, an environment *family* can (Geirhos et al. 2020; [[wiki/concepts/shortcut-learning.md]]) | **An anchor, not a component.** The designed objects are objective function, learning rule and architecture; the environment is "a crucial consideration to anchor the core components, but not one of the components itself", because the components pass through the genome bottleneck while the environment supplies unbounded information that no compact design can contain (Richards et al. 2019; [[wiki/concepts/three-component-framework.md]]) | Whether curating environment families counts as *building the model* or as *choosing the benchmark* — i.e. whether gap G16 is closed by a data programme or must be paid for in architecture and objective | `LIVE` — the two are answering different questions (what a designer writes down vs. what determines the learned solution), and the disagreement is only substantive because the wiki uses the control surface for both |
| T16 | **Does the shortest program consistent with the data generalize?** | **Yes — compression is the provably sufficient bias.** The universal semimeasure dominates every computable hypothesis, convergence is bounded by `K(µ)` alone, and a domain is learnable exactly to the extent it is compressible ([[wiki/concepts/universal-induction.md]], Hutter 2000) | **No — policy compression is antagonistic to generalization.** `TrainSol^opt`, the shortest program optimal on the curriculum, by construction discards everything not needed for the *training* situations, including what evaluation needs. Worked case: on `(−0.75, F), (0.15, T)` the shortest fits (`λx: x > 0`) break on `(−0.1, T)` while a nearest-neighbour learner that stored the raw data survives; being ready for future uncertainty costs description length (Chollet 2019) | Decides whether a minimum-description-length objective is a route to a reasoning model or a trap. It also splits gap G26 in two: compression may fail to *expose* structure (Hutter's version) or fail to *contain* it (Chollet's). **(brainstorm)** Candidate reconciliation: compress the generator over environments (meta-graph), never the policy fitted to one curriculum (instance-graph) — which makes this an argument *for* the two-level factorization. Note the regimes differ — asymptotic passive mixture vs. single finite-horizon program — so both may hold as stated | `LIVE` — Delétang et al. 2023 supplies A's empirical arm without settling it: models trained on exactly this objective compress modalities they never saw better than the hand-built codecs for those modalities, which is generalization by any reading. It also supplies B's: those same models have no object or 2-D structure for the images they compress best, and once their parameters are charged to the code length they lose to gzip by two orders of magnitude ([[wiki/concepts/prediction-compression-equivalence.md]], gap G35) |
| T17 | **Is intelligence universal, or scoped?** | **Universal.** Intelligence is expected reward over a mixture across *all computable environments*; no task distribution is privileged, and the agent is optimal by construction relative to its prior ([[wiki/entities/aixi.md]], Hutter 2000). If so, human intelligence is an implementation of one algorithm, reverse-engineering the brain is the shortest path, and AI is close-ended | **Scoped.** Every measure of intelligence is relative to a stipulated scope of tasks and value weights; the human `g` factor is an evolutionary adaptation, general only in the limited sense that human morphology is (evolved for the savanna, sufficient for Everest and basketball, useless in interstellar space). No Free Lunch bites only under a uniform distribution over problems, which is not the distribution our universe supplies (Chollet 2019) | Sets the research strategy. Universal ⇒ pursue the algorithm and benchmark against optimality; scoped ⇒ AI is anthropocentric by necessity, must be benchmarked *against humans* on shared priors and a shared task scope, and "general intelligence" without a stated scope is not a claim. The wiki's scoring tables assume the scoped reading already (they compare against human performance), without having argued for it | `LIVE` |
| T18 | **Must a world model be generative?** | **Yes — the model's steps must resemble the generative process.** Causal model building defines a good model as one whose steps correspond to how the data were actually produced; the payoff is the richness criterion (classify, generate, parse, create, explain, re-goal), five of whose six queries require running the model *forward* to produce data (Lake et al. 2017; [[wiki/concepts/causal-model-building.md]]) | **No — generativity is what prevents abstraction.** A generative model must account for every detail of `y` and can only handle unpredictable detail by pushing it into a latent; it cannot *discard* it, because it has no abstract representation of `y` to discard it from. A joint-embedding architecture predicts the *representation*, so its encoder drops carpet texture and wind-blown leaves outright. "Generative latent-variable models are not capable of eliminating irrelevant details" — hence the explicit recommendation against them for world models (LeCun 2022; [[wiki/entities/h-jepa.md]]) | Decides what the wiki's environment model is for. If A, the model must be invertible into observations and the richness protocol (gap G17) is a valid instrument. If B, a world model is a compatibility function that "cannot easily be used to predict `y` from `x`", four of the six richness queries are unanswerable in principle, and the wiki loses one of its two shift-free evaluation protocols | `LIVE` — the two agree that predict-everything is the wrong objective and disagree on whether what survives must be runnable forward. **(brainstorm)** The disagreement may be about *level*: a hierarchy could be generative at the level where detail is causally relevant (strokes, contacts) and non-generative above it, which no source states **First controlled-ish datum, and it favours B**: under one surprise metric with a matched untrained-network null, a pixel-space video predictor (VideoMAEv2) scores at the null on IntPhys, GRASP and InfLevel while a representation-space predictor of the same generation scores 98/66/62 — the two still differ in data and masking, so this is evidence about *families*, not an ablation of the prediction space (Garrido et al. 2025, [[wiki/concepts/violation-of-expectation.md]]) |
| T19 | **Is scalar reward a sufficient training signal, or a marginal one?** | **Sufficient in principle** — "reward is enough" (Silver et al. 2021), and the wiki's own ceiling agrees at the ideal level: AIXI maximises expected reward over a universal mixture and needs nothing else ([[wiki/entities/aixi.md]]) | **Marginal in practice, and structurally so.** Scalar reward is low-information feedback, and in standard RL the cost function *is the environment* — an unknown, non-differentiable oracle whose gradient must be estimated by trials. Make the cost a known differentiable function of predicted states instead and the design becomes optimal control, not reinforcement learning: almost all parameters are trained by predicting observations, and reward "plays a relatively minor role" (LeCun 2022). Hutter's own accounting agrees quantitatively — demonstrations beat reward by `≈K(R)` bits ([[wiki/concepts/universal-induction.md]]) | Decides where the wiki's learning signal comes from. If B, the design problem is *specifying a differentiable cost* rather than shaping a reward, which relocates the difficulty from credit assignment to objective design (gap G30) and makes the immutable Intrinsic Cost a first-class architectural decision rather than an environment property | `LEANING` — toward B on sample efficiency, which no side disputes; A survives only as a statement about what is *representable* in the limit, which is the same regime split as T16 |
| T20 | **Is intelligence a property of the process that builds a solution, or of the finished arrangement?** | **The process.** Skill is an output artifact carrying none of the intelligence; the formalism splits the system into a program-synthesis engine (which has it) and the skill program it emits (which does not), and scores only the conversion rate from priors and experience into coverage of unseen situations. A hard-coded solver measures its developer, not itself (Chollet 2019, [[wiki/concepts/skill-acquisition-efficiency.md]]) | **The arrangement.** `ℐ = log₂N/C` measures the arrangement, not its origin: a multiplication algorithm written by hand and a network that learned the same function by gradient descent have identical `ℐ` and both *know*. The gaming channels are closed at the artifact instead — a lookup table has `ℐ → 0` however much data or developer effort produced it, and Blockhead is refuted on storage alone (Choi 2026, [[wiki/concepts/intelligence-density.md]]) | Decides what the wiki is trying to build. Under A the target is a synthesis engine and every measurement must control for the developer; under B the target is a compact arrangement covering an unbounded domain and provenance is irrelevant, which makes hand-installed priors ([[wiki/concepts/core-knowledge.md]]) free rather than disqualifying. It also decides whether [[wiki/entities/bayesian-program-learning.md]]'s hand-authored stroke primitives count against it | `LIVE` — and partly reconcilable: Choi concedes the missing piece is *understanding* (constructing a new arrangement for a new domain) and defers it, which is exactly A's object. The two measure different halves; neither measures both (gap G36) |
| T21 | **Does a structured model's win over deep baselines measure the prior, or the state space it was handed?** | **The prior.** Structured Bayesian models embed conceptual knowledge of goal-directed agents, so they draw the right inferences from a handful of habituation trials and generalize human-like where minimal-bias learners do not — 96.0–99.7% vs. at-or-below chance on the Baby Intuitions tasks needing cross-trial binding, and the same pattern on the related AGENT benchmark, .96 vs .90 overall and .94 vs .65 under leave-one-type-out (Zhi-Xuan et al. 2022, [[wiki/entities/hbtom.md]]; Shu et al. 2021, [[wiki/entities/agent-benchmark.md]]) | **The state space.** The comparison is not matched on inputs: the structured model conditions on noise-free symbolic states with hand-written PDDL dynamics, hand-tuned preprocessing (barriers vanish instantly, trajectories smoothed to diagonals) and three logistic weights fitted on a purpose-built synthetic set, while the baselines work from trajectories or video. The paper concedes only "more upfront conceptual and engineering work" — pricing the difference as effort rather than as information supplied. **The matched-input control now exists and mostly refutes this side**: IRENE reads the same noise-free symbolic JSON, and scores 48.5 on Preference (vs 99.7) and 74.9 on Multi-Agent (vs 99.2) — so the state space buys some of Multi-Agent and none of Preference (Bortoletto et al. 2023, [[wiki/entities/irene.md]]). **The perception control answers the opposite way, and it is now primary rather than second-hand.** On AGENT the two baselines are re-run behind a trained Mask R-CNN + ResNet-34 derenderer instead of ground-truth 3-D state, at a mean bounding-box IoU of 0.07: inverse planning falls **.96 → .65** and the neural model **.90 → .51 = chance**. Two-thirds of the structured model's margin over chance is bought by being handed the state, and the ordering it preserves is between two models that are both nearly unusable (Shu et al. 2021, [[wiki/entities/agent-benchmark.md]]) | Every future claim that a structured prior beats a learned one. The wiki's rule (G17, closing note of [[wiki/architectural-gaps.md]]) is that an architecture assuming away the latent variable is not evidence about it — and here the latent variables the baselines fail on (persistent per-agent state) are genuinely hidden from both, while the ones the structured model was handed (object identity, dynamics) are hidden from only one | `LIVE` — **"matched inputs" names two experiments with opposite answers.** Matched *symbols* (IRENE on BIB) transfer little of the advantage, favouring A; matched *pixels* (the AGENT derenderer) remove most of it, favouring B. Both can be true: the authored agent model is what the neural nets lack, **and** the authored perceptual front-end is what the whole comparison rests on. The experiment nobody has run is a model whose agent hypothesis feeds *back* into the state estimate, so that the two are inferred jointly instead of a fixed reasoner sitting behind a fixed perception module |
| T22 | **Does a ranking metric on balanced samples say anything about a retrieval mechanism?** | **Yes — the model is accurate.** NeuroMatch reports 89.3–97.9 AUROC on balanced positive/negative pairs across seven datasets, ~20% above adapted neural graph matchers and +18.4% over approximate heuristics, and the abstract sells this as "accurate, efficient, and robust" (Ying et al. 2020) | **No — the same paper's confusion matrix shows the deployment number is precision, and it is at chance-plus.** Matching a small query against *all* neighbourhoods in DD gives TP 68.2 / FN 8.3 / FP 70.5 / TN 1030.9: 89% recall but **49% precision**, because subgraph hits are rare and the balanced evaluation sample destroys that skew. The paper reports the imbalance itself as "extreme" | Any use of structural retrieval against a real library ([[wiki/concepts/subgraph-matching.md]], G37): one in two returned schemata would be spurious, and the mechanism has no way to flag which. Generalises past this source — every AUROC-scored retrieval or detection result in the wiki inherits it | `LEANING` (B: it is the same source's own data, on the same datasets) |
| T23 | **What licenses the creation of a new discrete node?** | **A lasting change in the active set of predictive encodings** — segmentation is derived from the predictive substrate itself, needs no extra module, and covers motion onsets, occlusions, property and affordance changes with one detector. But the detectors on offer are hand-set thresholds or long-short-term-memory gates trained by backpropagation through time, and the clustering objective, granularity control and stopping rule are all unspecified (Butz 2016, [[wiki/concepts/event-segmentation.md]]). **Now split from within** (Nguyen et al. 2025): the "lasting change" is driven by *two* separable quantities, prediction error and prediction uncertainty, which uniquely predict human boundaries (t = 5.14 and 14.32) and recruit dissociable networks — so this position is not one criterion but an unarbitrated pair, see T121 | **A posterior over an unbounded context set** — a new memory is instantiated with probability equal to the *novel-context responsibility* under a sticky hierarchical Dirichlet process, so the criterion, the granularity (`γ`), the persistence bias (`κ`) and the cross-context pooling (`α`) are one prior, and noise-versus-boundary is a likelihood ratio rather than a threshold. But a node's content is a **single scalar**, and nodes share no structure with one another (Heald et al. 2021, [[wiki/concepts/contextual-inference.md]]) | Gap G27 — whether the discretisation problem is solved by enriching the representation until boundaries fall out, or by putting a principled allocation prior over impoverished units. The two are not obviously combinable: the Bayesian criterion is tractable *because* the state is scalar | `LIVE` — complementary rather than contradictory on their face, but nobody has run either criterion over the other's representation. **A route to deciding it now exists:** Nguyen et al. 2025 score model-generated boundaries against parcel-wise fMRI pattern-dissimilarity timecourses, and name latent-cause inference (Position B's machinery) as their candidate for the ~16% of explainable variance the prediction-quality triggers miss — so B can be scored on A's own benchmark |
| T24 | **Does an adaptation curve measure learning?** | Implicitly assumed throughout the wiki and by the whole few-shot/meta-learning literature: rate of behavioural improvement is the observable that stands in for structure acquisition, and differences in that rate are differences in learning rate (savings, anterograde interference, environmental-consistency effects were all read this way) | **No.** Behaviour change decomposes into *proper* learning (a stored state moved) and *apparent* learning (only the posterior weights on unchanged stored states moved). Simulated with parameters fit to *other* experiments, COIN reproduces savings, anterograde interference and the consistency effect with Kalman gains and states essentially unchanged — the entire effect sits in the context probabilities (Heald et al. 2021, [[wiki/entities/coin-model.md]]) | Gap G17 and [[wiki/concepts/skill-acquisition-efficiency.md]]: every conversion-rate score read off a learning curve, including in-context-learning curves, confounds acquiring structure with re-weighting stored structure | `LEANING` toward B in the motor domain — evoked recovery is a confirmed novel prediction (ΔBIC 394.1 nats over the dual-rate model) and the decomposition has not been tested outside sensorimotor adaptation |
| T25 | **Does a decodable internal structure license the claim that the model reasons over it?** | **Yes — decodability is the evidence.** The emergent-world-model programme reads a successful probe as the model having built and used a representation of the domain: the whole maze reconstructs from a single token's residual stream at layer 2 at 0.83–0.99 per wall, dedicated components (an adjacency head attending to in-context path-length-1 neighbours, a goal-pointing head) operate over it, the tuned lens shows valid-neighbour mass rising from the same layer, and probe accuracy improves in the same grokking-like steps as exact-path accuracy (Ivanitskiy et al. 2023, following Li et al. 2022 on Othello) | **No — the same paper breaks both implications.** Representation without competence: the model that reconstructs the maze at ≥0.93 for most cells emits topology-violating paths in 13–16% of rollouts (62% for the smaller model out of distribution) while reaching the target ≈100% of the time. Competence without representation: the corridor-trained model solves its task having never acquired a linearly decodable maze representation. And the whole case is correlational — the authors defer ablation and intervention on the identified heads to future work | Decides what an "emergent world model" result buys. If decodability neither entails nor is entailed by competence, it scores the *discovery* half of [[wiki/concepts/latent-graph-discovery.md]] only, and a system can pass it while failing to navigate — which relocates the deficit into search and control (G5, G10, G15) rather than into representation learning | `LEANING B` — B's evidence is internal to A's own experiment; what would move it back is an intervention result (edit the probe direction, change the path), which exists for Othello and not here. **The named missing evidence now exists.** Nanda et al. 2023 add a probe direction to OthelloGPT's residual stream and its play follows the counterfactual board — 0.10 errors against a 2.72 null, matching gradient-based editing, and holding on boards unreachable by legal play. So for that model A's implication survives its causal test, and the maze case's failure is about *the maze model*, not about the inference. But the same paper opens the successor worry, T158: the verified world model coexists with a cheaper circuit that resolves legal moves earlier in depth, so "the model uses it" is true on average and undetermined per input. A third position also favours neither side: decodability is the wrong quantity. In human hippocampus, context and stimulus *decoding* were statistically unchanged between sessions where participants could and could not perform one-shot inference, while cross-condition generalization and parallelism rose and then collapsed again on error trials — so what licenses the reasoning claim, correlationally, is the representation's **format** (Courellis et al. 2024, [[wiki/concepts/population-geometry.md]]) ([[wiki/concepts/representation-probing.md]], [[wiki/entities/maze-solving-transformers.md]]) |
| T26 | **Do newborn dentate granule cells perform pattern separation, or block it?** | **They perform it.** Loss and gain of function agree and are parametric: ablating adult neurogenesis by x-irradiation impairs delayed-non-match-to-place at 2 intervening radial-maze arms but not at 3–4, exercise-driven neurogenesis improves discrimination of adjacent identical stimuli, and genetically enhancing newborn-neuron survival improves contextual fear discrimination (Yassa & Stark 2011) | **They oppose it while immature.** A computational account has immature granule cells *increasing* similarity among temporally correlated inputs — "pattern integration" — because they are far more excitable and respond too broadly to separate, negatively regulating separation until they mature. A reactivation/Arc study goes further: the number of reactivated granule cells matches the newly-generated proportion, suggesting mature cells are functionally "retired" and calling their long-hypothesised orthogonalizing role into question | Decides what the machine analogue of neurogenesis is. Under A it is **capacity added where separation demand is high** — grow the sparse layer when discrimination fails. Under B it is a **temporary integration bias**, i.e. the opposite sign, and the store's separating population is the *young* one with the mature population doing something else entirely. The two prescribe opposite growth policies for a fast **M** ([[wiki/concepts/pattern-separation-completion.md]], [[wiki/concepts/synaptic-plasticity.md]]) | `LIVE` — the review flags this as under active debate and the direct data on mature vs. immature contributions as scant; what would move it is parametric manipulation of environmental similarity with the two populations distinguished |
| T27 | **Is the brain's map metric or topological?** | **Metric.** O'Keefe & Nadel's original proposal was a Euclidean coordinate system; entorhinal cortex tracks *Euclidean* distance to a newly set goal independently of barriers, grid cells tile space with a fixed metric, and place-field position is set by distances to walls (Epstein et al. 2017) | **Topological.** Posterior hippocampus tracks *path* distance during travel and scales with the current node's degree centrality, anterior hippocampus with its closeness centrality in the street network; replay of place cells over connected tracks preserves the tracks' topology rather than their geometry; and the hippocampal-entorhinal system codes spaces defined only by transitions between discrete items, with no continuum | Decides what a learned map is *for*. Under A the map is an embedding and planning is vector arithmetic plus obstacle correction; under B it is a graph and planning is search, with the metric demoted to a heuristic. The review states the relation between the two codes is unclear. **The wiki's reading (brainstorm):** they coexist as an A\* pair — an admissible Euclidean lower bound in entorhinal cortex guiding a topological search in hippocampus — which would explain why the two quantities are dissociable by region and why the Euclidean one updates at *goal-setting* while the path one updates *during travel*. Untested as such ([[wiki/concepts/cognitive-map.md]], [[wiki/concepts/simulation-based-planning.md]]) | `LIVE` |
| T28 | **Is the hippocampus a map, or an index of cortical representations?** | **A map.** In state-space models the connections *between hippocampal cells* are the edges of the graph: clone-structured cognitive graphs learn a de-aliased transition structure inside hippocampus with local, fast, biologically plausible rules, and successor-representation models read place fields directly off a discounted future-occupancy matrix ([[wiki/entities/cscg.md]], [[wiki/concepts/successor-representation.md]]) | **An index.** Models of *generalisation* adhere to hippocampal indexing theory: hippocampal cells are conjunctions binding a cortical structural code to a cortical sensory code, all predictive capability lives in cortex, and remapping is the rebinding that lets one abstraction serve many worlds ([[wiki/entities/tolman-eichenbaum-machine.md]]) | Decides where the fast store sits relative to the graph — whether fast **M** *is* the instance-graph or merely points at it, and therefore what replay consolidates | `LEANING` — Whittington et al. 2022 propose a schedule rather than a winner: hippocampus builds a relational map where no cortical structure yet applies, and reverts to memory once cortex has learned to structure that kind of world, with the transition tied to the behavioural onset of generalisation. Untested. Whittington et al. 2022 (TEM-t) adds a third framing that fits either side: if the computation is transformer-shaped it can run in cortex alone, and the hippocampus is recruited only when memories must persist or must bind across *distant* areas — so hippocampal dependence would be set by memory horizon and binding span, not by whether the task needs a map ([[wiki/entities/tem-transformer.md]]). **Chandra et al. 2023 push B to its limit and remove its main cost**: the hippocampal state is a content-free *hash* of a grid state — a pointer with no predictive content whatsoever, carrying not even a conjunction — and the same architecture nonetheless yields exponential capacity, no catastrophic forgetting across environments, zero-shot spatial inference and ~10⁵-step sequence memory. The index reading therefore no longer trades capacity against generalisation ([[wiki/entities/vector-hash.md]]), which was position A's strongest remaining argument |
| T29 | **Does de-aliasing conflict with generalisation?** | **No — one mechanism suffices.** Latent states inferred from sequences explain the non-spatial hippocampal zoo (splitter, lap, evidence cells) as a single principle: separate states with different futures. A clone model learns them from raw sequences with no structural prior ([[wiki/entities/cscg.md]]) | **Yes — they need separate machinery.** The same clone model would learn the splitter cells and *not* the co-recorded place cells, because it cannot profit from generalising the structure of space; every level of abstraction has to be represented simultaneously, which requires a reusable structural code alongside the de-aliaser ([[wiki/entities/tolman-eichenbaum-machine.md]], Whittington et al. 2022) | Whether a reasoning model needs one latent-state learner or two coupled ones; the same trade-off as G40 seen from the representation side rather than the task side | `LEANING` toward B — the simulations showing both cell classes emerging together are TEM's, and the CSCG-would-miss-place-cells claim is asserted, not demonstrated. **A supports the coexistence half but not the transfer half.** In mouse CA1, place-like (correlated) and splitter (orthogonalized) responses coexist in one population *and* fall on a continuum with cells migrating between them, and the clone model reproduces the split — because the positions that stay correlated (track start and end) are exactly those where no latent distinction exists, not because it generalises the structure of space. In the same animals, a learned state machine *is* reused for a new cue pair at 147 vs 483 trials, which is transfer the clone model cannot express (Sun et al. 2025) |
| T30 | **What is replay for — planning the next path, or building a transferable map?** | **Planning.** Forward replay at choice points resembles the trajectory about to be taken, replay content reflects the imminent plan, and reverse replay supports planning better than forward alone in computational models (Foster 2017; Ólafsdóttir et al. 2018; Mattar & Daw 2018) — this is the reading that licensed experience replay and prioritised replay in machines | **Consolidation, and deliberately anti-planning.** Remote experiences are replayed *more* than the imminent path (Gillespie et al. 2021); replay upsamples the space where least time was spent, reducing the behavioural dwell bias (Grosmark et al. 2021); salient random cues that dominate online representation are *actively suppressed* from ripples (Terada et al. 2022). The proposed principle is a preprogrammed inductive bias toward a map transferable to dissimilar tasks — consolidating everything overfits (Sun et al. 2023; Liao & Losonczy 2024) | Decides what a machine replay buffer should sample. Position A gives reward/TD-error prioritisation, which is what every deployed system uses; position B gives inverse-visitation and cross-episode-recurrence weighting, which nothing uses. It also decides whether gap G14's consolidation channel can share a sampling policy with the planning rollouts of G15, or needs its own | `LIVE`, but B now has a **normative derivation** where it previously had only recordings: if experience mixes recurring update patterns with one-off ones, gating the long-term write on the fast store's own recall of the update is optimal, and it reproduces "keep what recurs, suppress the idiosyncratic" from first principles — including Terada et al. 2022, where the fixed-location cues *are* the reliable memories (Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar 2024, [[wiki/concepts/recall-gated-consolidation.md]]). Position A still has no comparable derivation of why the *imminent* path should dominate. Sun et al. 2023, now ingested, strengthens B on a further axis rather than adjudicating this one: it shows the *amount* of replay is a control variable with an interior optimum set by predictability, and that unregulated reactivation drives generalization below chance — so a buffer tuned for planning value has no term for the harm it does to the map it is consolidating into ([[wiki/concepts/generalization-optimized-consolidation.md]]). Both bodies of evidence are direct recordings and neither is disputed; the review's own resolution is a *schedule* (specific early, generalisable with exposure) rather than an arbitration, and no experiment yet manipulates the two demands against each other in one task. **A staging proposal that could make the two compatible (tentative):** if awake ripples *tag* the just-completed trial and sleep ripples spend the tag, the reward/recency-driven distribution (A) is the selector and the transfer-biased distribution (B) is what the selection seeds, so the two are consecutive stages rather than rivals for one budget (`raw/talk-nd-memory-gating.txt`) |
| T31 | **Is the entorhinal integrator perfect or leaky?** | **Perfect.** Path-integration models of the place code integrate velocity without loss and add dedicated error-correction machinery; a code is only a *position* if every route with the same total displacement lands in the same place, and periodicity is what makes it error-correcting ([[wiki/concepts/path-integration.md]], Whittington et al. 2022) | **Leaky.** `ρ_i < 1`: entorhinal activity is a weighted sum over *recent* movements, not a position. Both extremes fail the data — `ρ = 1` (pure place) and `ρ = 0` (pure head direction) cannot produce the retrospective and trajectory coding actually observed in entorhinal cortex, and leak makes accumulated error stationary rather than unbounded, deleting the need for error correction (Howard et al. 2005; Frank et al. 2000) | Decides whether a latent accumulator in a model should be norm-preserving with a correction channel, or discounted with a bounded horizon — and whether history-dependence (splitter-like coding) is a bug to be removed or the same knob doing useful work. Note the two may not be rivals: grid cells were not yet characterised in 2005, so the leaky account may describe a different entorhinal population than the modular grid code | `LIVE` — the modern grid literature assumes A, but no source in the wiki reproduces retrospective coding from a perfect integrator |
| T32 | **Is serial order carried by oscillatory phase or by rate-coded time cells?** | **Phase.** Theta/gamma nesting assigns each item in a sequence to a distinct gamma cycle within a theta cycle, the standard account of order in the hippocampal literature (Lisman & Redish) | **Rate.** Different neurons fire at different points within a delay period, and order arises from noise-driven transitions between attractors coupled by slightly asymmetric weights, with adaptation retiring each state — no clock, and the span falls out as `7 ± 2` (Rolls 2013, [[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]]) | Decides whether a machine sequence memory needs an oscillatory scaffold or only a recurrent net with adaptation and noise. Under B, order memory and episodic storage share one network and one learning rule, and stochasticity is load-bearing rather than a nuisance to be annealed away | `LIVE` — the rate-coded time cells are a direct observation (MacDonald et al. 2011); which mechanism *generates* them is modelled, not measured, and Rolls offers three candidate generators without discriminating them |
| T33 | **Is CA1 a linear relay or a second recombination-and-completion stage?** | **A relay.** Transfer-curve measurements put CA1 on the identity line — it neither separates nor completes — and human high-resolution fMRI agrees (Yassa & Stark 2011, [[wiki/concepts/pattern-separation-completion.md]]). **Weakened on inspection of the primary human source:** Bakker et al. 2008 gives CA1 a lure bias score of 0.59–0.83 with lure activity statistically indistinguishable from a true repeat, so it licenses only the negative half (CA1 does not separate); the identity-line claim is not in that data | **A recombiner.** Competitive learning in CA1 reassembles the sub-parts of an episode that CA3 had to store separately, into an efficient retrieval cue for the cortical backprojection, and the CA3→CA1 pattern association generalises to the nearest CA1 pattern when CA3 completion was imperfect (Rolls 2013) | Whether the fast store's read-out is a projection or a computation. Under B there is a *second* completion stage after the attractor, which changes what a machine must implement between memory and decoder, and it is also where the wiki's `C^HBP` fan-out constraint starts | `LIVE` — the two are not strictly incompatible (a stage can be linear in similarity while recombining components), and nobody has measured a CA1 transfer curve with the CA3 input recorded, which is exactly the discipline position A demands of everyone else |
| T34 | **Does hippocampus→cortex transfer happen during sleep or during waking?** | **Sleep (and quiet rest).** Sharp-wave-ripple replay during sleep and rest is the measured reinstatement channel, its content is filtered rather than veridical, and the whole machine replay literature is built on it (Marr; Schwindel & McNaughton; Liao & Losonczy 2024, [[wiki/concepts/offline-replay.md]]) | **Waking.** Recall during waking retrieves the *currently relevant* episodes, which are the ones worth turning into semantics; unguided noise-driven firing in sleep would build the bizarre representations we dream about; and non-retrieval during waking is what marks a memory for overwriting, so the retrieval schedule and the forgetting policy are one mechanism (Rolls 2013) | What a machine consolidation loop should sample. B makes *use frequency* the criterion — computable online, no labels, no offline pass — where A's criteria (reward, inverse visitation, cross-episode recurrence) all need a separate offline process. It also decides whether gap G14's channel runs on the same schedule as the store's forgetting policy | `LEANING A` on evidence — direct recordings all favour ripple-driven offline replay and Rolls offers a normative argument with no data. B's *criterion* survives independently of its schedule and no source in the wiki tests it. **The dispute is also narrower than it looks**: recall-gated consolidation implements A's selection criterion (recurrence) on B's schedule (event-by-event, online, no replay), so the criterion and the schedule are separable and selective replay is one implementation of the gate rather than its definition (Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar 2024, [[wiki/concepts/recall-gated-consolidation.md]]) . **A dose–response result now strengthens A where it was weakest — between-subject variance.** Prefrontal NREM slow-wave activity predicts overnight word-pair retention at `r = 0.81` across a 50-year age span, absorbs the effects of age and prefrontal atrophy on retention, and negatively predicts next-day hippocampal retrieval activation, while sleep duration, efficiency, stage composition and spindles predict nothing — so a variable expressed only during sleep carries the retention differences, which waking recall alone cannot explain. Still correlational, and it does not touch B's *criterion* (Mander et al. 2013, [[wiki/concepts/complementary-learning-systems.md]]). **And a split that gives each side half the question (tentative):** ripple content decoded on a behaviour-fitted manifold shows awake ripples replaying the trial just completed and the following sleep's ripples revisiting those same trials — selection computed awake, on the awake state's access to current relevance (B's point), transfer executed asleep, where cortex is receptive and the repetition budget exists (A's point) (`raw/talk-nd-memory-gating.txt`, [[wiki/concepts/offline-replay.md]]) |
| T35 | **Is remapping controlled by the environment or by the animal's inference history?** | **The environment.** The standard reading of the literature assigns remapping types to manipulation types: global remapping corresponds to a change of physical location, rate remapping to a change of condition at the same location (Leutgeb 2005a; Colgin 2010; Alme 2014; Lisman 2017), which is what makes an experimental protocol a valid instrument | **The inference history.** Global remapping occurs between conditions at the *same* location, rate remapping between *different* locations, and the *same* manipulation produces both at different points in training (Lever 2002). Worse, the O'Keefe and Moser labs get opposite results from comparable protocols, and Colgin et al. 2010's attempted exact replication of Wills et al. 2005 reversed it. Sanders et al. 2020 conclude that remapping responds to a large array of experiential factors of which the experimenter manipulates a subset, so cross-lab comparison of remapping extent is invalid ([[wiki/entities/hidden-state-inference-remapping.md]]) | Whether "did it remap?" can be used as a controlled dependent variable at all — and whether an engineered analogue should expose an allocate/reuse decision to the designer or accept that it is set by the full training history | `LEANING` — B has the replication failure on its side; A has no account of the training-dependence, and the reconciliation (categories are one continuum in state uncertainty) is B's |
| T36 | **Does map selection cause context-dependent behaviour?** | **Yes — the field's working assumption**, and the wiki's: remapping is the neurophysiological substrate of context-dependent learning (Colgin et al. 2008), which is what licenses treating map retrieval as the mechanism behind context-appropriate action. Correlational support exists (Kentros 2004; Kennedy & Shapiro 2009) | **Unproven, and there is a counterexample.** Sanders et al. 2020 state that no causal demonstration exists (see also Kubie et al. 2019), and Jeffery et al. 2003 report task-performance *transfer* between two conditions showing near-global remapping — behaviour generalising exactly where the code did not | The whole retrieval-and-allocation story: if a separated map does not gate behaviour, then map identity is a representational bookkeeping device and the behavioural context-effects are computed somewhere else. Also decides whether [[wiki/concepts/representation-probing.md]]'s decoded-vs-used split applies to the hippocampus as it does to transformers | `LIVE` — a decisive experiment is designed but not run (train two groups to remap or not to the same manipulation, verify neurally, then test fear-memory generalisation) |
| T37 | **Is grid-like coding a discovery or a detection artefact?** | **A discovery, and near-universal.** Grid, place and head-direction cells recorded in rat primary somatosensory (S1 hindlimb) and secondary visual (V2M) cortex, surviving whisker trimming and darkness; grid-like coding of gaze in monkey entorhinal cortex without locomotion; hexadirectional BOLD in orbitofrontal, ventromedial prefrontal and cingulate cortex over concept, word and discrete social-hierarchy spaces (Chen et al. 2022, [[wiki/concepts/distributed-reference-frames.md]]). Qu et al. 2026 answer part of the detector objection without leaving fMRI: in 203 subjects, **three independent estimators** (hexagonal modulation, cross-run angle consistency, angular-difference RSA) converge on the same entorhinal cluster, the effect is six-fold only, it survives an entorhinal signal-to-noise control and a distance-per-angular-bin control, and the estimated orientation `φ` transfers to a *different* map learned afterwards — a detector returning noise should not do any of these | **The detector is not trustworthy, per the same paper.** Identifying grid patterns is "a relatively arbitrary threshold phenomenon"; the standard spatial-autocorrelation method generates false positives without spike-shuffle controls; fMRI BOLD does not measure spiking, so hexadirectional modulation cannot be read as a grid population; and **no single-unit grid representation in a conceptual space has ever been recorded** in any species. Patterns are also *missed* when grid scale mismatches enclosure size, so the false-negative rate is unknown too | Every "grids outside space" claim the wiki carries ([[wiki/concepts/abstract-structural-codes.md]] T4, the beyond-space table of [[wiki/concepts/cognitive-map.md]]). If B, the case that `g` is periodic rests entirely on an aggregate signal with an unvalidated detector, and the biological warrant for importing a periodic code is weaker than the wiki has been treating it. Generalises: the same objection applies to any pattern-score threshold used as evidence that a code exists | `LIVE` — the two positions are the same author's, and what would settle it is human or monkey single-unit recording in a conceptual space, which nobody has |
| T38 | **Do grid cells emerge from training on path integration?** | **Yes.** A series of deep-learning models report that recurrent networks trained to path-integrate develop hexagonal grid units, with the 4-fold→6-fold transition attributed to a non-negativity constraint on firing rates — read in the wiki as the trainable route to a periodic `g` ([[wiki/concepts/path-integration.md]], Whittington et al. 2022 reviewing Cueva & Wei 2018, Banino et al. 2018, Sorscher et al. 2019) | **No — the target, not the task, makes them.** >11,000 networks: most path-integrate optimally, almost none show grid units under the lenient one-unit-above-0.8 criterion. Grids require Difference-of-Softmaxes readouts (Cartesian, polar, Gaussian and equi-norm DoG all fail; 1/1086 DoG runs matched DoS, and not across seeds); period is set monotonically by readout width `σ_E`; distributions are unimodal so no modules form and adjacent ratios are ≈1 not ≈1.4; and grids vanish under place-cell-like multi-field multi-scale readouts with position decoding unharmed. Analytically, gradient descent on the readout loss is attractor dynamics whose kernel is the readout *correlation matrix*, so a centre–surround target hand-builds the Turing instability (Schaeffer et al. 2022) | The wiki's warrant for treating `g` as *learnable* rather than *installed*. If B, every trained-network citation for a periodic structural code is a demonstration that the code can be baked into a target, and the biological case rests on recordings plus first-principles attractor theory alone. It also decides whether the wiki's empty objective slot (G30) can be filled by emergence experiments at all ([[wiki/concepts/objective-identifiability.md]]) | `LEANING B` — B is a direct large-scale replication-and-sweep of A's own code, and A's authors' undocumented DoS-for-DoG substitution is verified in the released code. What A retains: nobody disputes that *some* configuration produces grids, only that the task explains it. **A regains one leg:** Whittington et al. 2018 obtain grid, band, border and object-vector units from *unsupervised* next-observation prediction on graph worlds with a one-hot categorical target and no spatial readout at all — a target whose correlation matrix cannot be a centre–surround kernel, so B's mechanism does not apply. Unaudited on every other checklist item ([[wiki/entities/tolman-eichenbaum-machine.md]]). **And one more leg, with the audit partly run:** the spiking rewrite of that same model gets 59.6% ± 18.0 grid units from a one-hot categorical target across four seeds and publishes the ablation table — but its grids are 4-fold, single-scale and phase-uniform, so the population never forms the toroidal manifold, which is exactly the relational invariant B says target-driven models miss ([[wiki/entities/spiking-tem.md]]) | **A third position is now on the wiki and it is neither A nor B: installed.** Peng et al. 2025 ([[wiki/entities/gcq.md]]) build a working world model on grid-like codes that are *defined before training* — the `N²` bump states of a toroidal continuous attractor network, with plain reconstruction as the only objective — and cite toroidal medial-entorhinal population activity present *before sensory experience* plus Gardner et al. 2022's toroidal topology as the biological version: the brain holds preconfigured bump patterns and learning is the assignment of sensory experience to them, not their formation. It is consistent with B (the code is not what the task selects) while denying B's implication that the code is therefore an artefact of a modeller's target. And it contributes the one direct test available: let the same model tune its installed codewords by gradient and it *moves away from them and scores worse* (T150).
| T39 | **Is place-cell remapping random?** | **Random — the standard reading.** Global remapping is defined as rate *and* place becoming statistically independent, i.e. complete reorganisation ([[wiki/concepts/pattern-separation-completion.md]]); classical remapping models have grid modules realign randomly, producing a fresh place-cell set whose anchoring learning starts from scratch in each environment (Whittington et al. 2018 citing Alme et al. 2014) | **Non-random, and constrained by the grid code.** Place cells remap to positions consistent with a preserved grid representation: across two cue-different same-size environments, the grid rate at the place-field peak correlates across environments (r = 0.320, p = 0.002) and the normalised place-peak-to-grid-peak distance correlates too (r = 0.170, p = 0.028) over 115 cell pairs, with the grid–grid correlation matrix itself preserved (p < 0.001) and the model reproducing r ≈ 0.3–0.35 (Whittington et al. 2018). **Replicated in a second lab's data**: 4 mice, real and virtual 60×60 cm arenas, gridAtPlace r = 0.273, p < 0.05 over 64 conservative pairs (r = 0.544 over 148 liberal pairs), against r = 0.322 / 0.63 for the original rat dataset re-run with the same pipeline (Whittington et al. 2020, data from Chen et al. 2018). The same paper adds an independent line of support: hippocampal cells in a non-spatial task remap spatially while **preserving** their lap selectivity, which is only explicable if the structural input survives the remap | Whether structural knowledge survives a map change. If A, each environment is learned afresh and there is no transfer channel through the hippocampus; if B, remapping *is* the rebinding operation and prior structure is inherited for free — which is the mechanism the wiki's whole `p = f(g, x)` story depends on. Also constrains [[wiki/entities/hidden-state-inference-remapping.md]]: allocating a new hidden state must not license an arbitrary new map  | `LEANING B` — B is a direct measurement where A is an assumption inherited from models; effect sizes remain modest and module identity of the recorded cells is unknown (so only a population-level non-random claim is testable), and both datasets were analysed by the model's authors — but there are now two independent datasets, in two species, one of them with a virtual-reality arena, plus a non-spatial confirmation in a third. **One complication from the same author:** TEM-t's place cells are attention *memory neurons*, and those remap **randomly**, i.e. the transformer rewrite of the model that produced position B natively predicts position A ([[wiki/entities/tem-transformer.md]], T40) |
| T40 | **Is a place cell a conjunctive cell or a memory index?** | **A conjunction.** In TEM a hippocampal cell is one element of the outer product `p = g̃ ⊙ x̃` — it fires only when its structural and its sensory input are both active. This predicts non-random remapping (T39), explains multiple fields as multiple consistent regions, explains lap cells retaining lap preference while remapping spatially, and is the form the recorded grid–place correlations support (Whittington et al. 2018, 2020) | **An index.** In TEM-t a hippocampal cell is a *memory neuron* of the Krotov & Hopfield (2020) attention split, active when the current structural query matches a stored address; sparsity comes from the softmax, and it is one neuron per stored memory rather than one per `g`×`x` pair. This yields place fields and random remapping, and it scales to conjunctions across three or more cortical areas at additive cost (Whittington, Warren & Behrens 2022) | Decides what the fast store's units *are*, and therefore what remapping data measure. It is also a scaling decision with opposite signs: the conjunctive form pays `×n_c` neurons per extra bound region and buys the non-random-remapping prediction; the index form pays `+n_c` and loses it. The wiki's `p = f(g, x)` shorthand is silent on which — it names the factorisation, not the binding implementation | `LIVE` — the source proposing B concedes A's data and offers a hybrid: hippocampal *feature* neurons `g̃ = gW_g` inherit the grid relationship, so a population-level grid–place correlation survives alongside randomly remapping memory neurons. Not simulated, and it predicts a *bimodal* hippocampal population (grid-related and not), which the remapping analyses have never tested for · see also T42, where the same product is reported to *abolish* the entorhinal code in a spiking reimplementation |
| T41 | **Must the grid↔hippocampal weights be learned, or must they be frozen?** | **Learned.** Rolls 2013 reports that place-like fields form from an entorhinal grid input *only* with Hebbian training in the perforant path — an untrained sparse projection is not enough — and TEM/TEM-t learn the structural-to-hippocampal mapping throughout training, which is what lets the code specialise to the environment family ([[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]], [[wiki/entities/tolman-eichenbaum-machine.md]]) | **Frozen.** Chandra et al. 2023 report the opposite in both directions: a *fixed random* grid→hippocampal projection plus a learned-once-then-frozen return projection is what produces the exponential fixed-point capacity, and replacing it with statistically matched hippocampal states whose weights are learned bidirectionally **collapses** that capacity; realistic place-cell tuning and the observed cross-environment similarity statistics appear with no training of the projection at all ([[wiki/entities/vector-hash.md]]) | Decides whether the address layer of a machine fast store is a trained encoder or a frozen random hash — i.e. whether the store can specialise its addresses to the domain (needed for similarity-respecting retrieval, G37) or must give that up to keep the capacity (G42) | `LIVE` — the two sides optimise different quantities on the same weights and neither runs the other's test: no capacity measurement exists for a learned grid→hippocampal map, and no transfer or relational-inference measurement exists for a frozen one. Note they are not strictly contradictory — a frozen projection could suffice for *capacity* while a trained one is required for *place-field tuning of the kind Rolls simulates* — but no experiment separates them |
| T42 | **Is the conjunctive place cell `p = f(g̃ ⊙ x̃)` required, or does it block the structural code?** | **Required.** In TEM the elementwise product is the single line from which non-random remapping, multiple place fields, lap-cells-that-remap-spatially and the whole `g`/`x` dissociation follow; it is presented as a hard consequence of the biological constraint that the same neurons must carry both codes (Whittington et al. 2018, 2020, [[wiki/entities/tolman-eichenbaum-machine.md]]) | **It blocks the grid code.** Reimplementing the identical architecture in spiking neurons, the `g ⊙ x_LEC` input to CA1 produced place cells but **no grid cells whatsoever**; grid emergence at 59.6% required removing the product and feeding CA1 from CA3 and LEC III separately (Kawahara & Fujisawa 2025, [[wiki/entities/spiking-tem.md]]) | Whether multiplicative gating is the mechanism of hippocampal binding or a rate-model convenience. If B, the wiki's `p = f(g, x)` factorisation is right about the *factors* and wrong about the *operator*, and T40's index reading gains a second independent argument. Also decides whether a binary-spike latent can carry a conjunction at all: an AND over two sparse binary vectors is far sparser than over two rate vectors, which may starve the gradient to `g` | `LIVE` — one failed reimplementation against one working implementation; the spiking paper reports the failure (S9, S10 Figs) but does not diagnose it, and the confound (binary sparsity vs. the operator itself) is untested |
| T43 | **Is the CA3/CA1 difference in place-field shift a fixed property of their learning rules?** | **Yes — different rules at different synapses.** CA3 recurrent synapses potentiate symmetrically in time while CA1 Schaffer collaterals follow classical asymmetric STDP; a two-layer model with that assignment reproduces the recorded dissociation (CA1 fields drift backwards over laps, CA3 fields much less) and fails to reproduce it if CA3 is given the asymmetric rule (Keck et al. 2025, modelling Dong, Madar & Sheffield 2021) | **Not fixed — it is environment-dependent.** The CA3-stable/CA1-shifting dissociation holds in *familiar* environments; in novel ones both populations shift, more strongly in CA1 (reported in Keck et al. 2025). A static symmetric rule in CA3 cannot produce a shift that exists early and vanishes with familiarity | Decides whether temporal symmetry of plasticity is a fixed architectural constant or a *gated* variable — if gated, the natural controller is neuromodulatory (acetylcholine/dopamine already switch potentiation regimes), which would make "how much geometry vs how much policy to store" an online decision the agent makes rather than a wiring choice the designer makes ([[wiki/concepts/successor-representation.md]], [[wiki/concepts/synaptic-plasticity.md]]) | `LIVE` |
| T44 | **Is a periodic `g` necessary for structural generalization, or just one solution among several?** | **Necessary, or near enough.** The wiki's whole case for grid-like codes is that periodicity is what buys extension to unvisited space, error correction and cheap transfer ([[wiki/concepts/path-integration.md]], [[wiki/concepts/abstract-structural-codes.md]]), and Li et al. 2026 supply the first quantitative support: across mm-TEM runs, grid score in the path-integration units predicts 512-step imagination accuracy at `r = 0.647, p = 2e-4` (Spearman `ρ = 0.783, p = 1e-6`) | **One solution among several.** The same figure contains models with *low* grid scores and *high* accuracy, whose autocorrelation maps show non-hexagonal alternative solutions; the paper describes them and does not characterise them (the sentence is truncated at a page break in this conversion).
