Nothing was intervened on — the correlation is over seeds and hyperparameters, so it is equally consistent with grid score and accuracy being two readouts of how well the action→`g` map was learned | Decides whether periodicity is a *design target* for `g` or an epiphenomenon of a well-trained integrator. If B, then every emergence result the wiki uses as evidence for grid codes ([[wiki/empirical-tensions.md]] T38) is measuring the wrong thing, and the objective slot (G30) should be written against path-invariance and long-horizon accuracy directly rather than against gridness | `LIVE` — first evidence on either side; the correlation is real and the counterexamples are in the same scatter plot ([[wiki/entities/mm-tem-hippoformer.md]]) |
| T45 | **Is the hippocampal map amodal, or biased to space?** | **Amodal relational processing.** The relational theory has the hippocampus linking any associations into large frameworks, space being one instance (Cohen & Eichenbaum 1993; Eichenbaum 2004). The wiki's model side assumes this throughout: TEM learns the same structural code for family trees and for 2-D rooms ([[wiki/entities/tolman-eichenbaum-machine.md]]), and the "beyond space" table of [[wiki/concepts/cognitive-map.md]] lists concept, word, gaze, social-hierarchy and discrete-transition spaces, including hippocampal *angle* coding of a power × affiliation social space (Tavares et al. 2015). The best-controlled positive is non-spatial, discrete and incidental: bilateral entorhinal adaptation scaling with distance on an implicitly learned object graph, with the graph recoverable by MDS and a matching behavioural RT effect in a separate cohort (Garvert et al. 2017) | **Spatially biased.** In the only design that matches relational demand and swaps only the domain — chain-search over the subject's own friends by physical proximity vs by acquaintance, matched on performance, difficulty, reaction time, episodic/emotional/self/imagery ratings — the hippocampus was active for the spatial version only, showed **no** activation for the social version even against a vowel-counting baseline at p < 0.01 uncorrected, and was the *single* region showing the domain × task interaction. Relational demand alone (main effect of task) produced no hippocampal voxels; spatial content alone (non-relational contrast) produced parahippocampal/retrosplenial/parietal but not hippocampal activation. Social navigation instead engaged the mentalising network (medial prefrontal, superior temporal sulcus, temporoparietal junction, temporal poles) (Kumaran & Maguire 2005) | Decides whether "cognitive map" is the right frame for a general reasoning substrate or a spatial specialisation the wiki has been over-extrapolating. If B, then a model that reuses one map module for spatial and non-spatial relational inference is *not* mirroring the human architecture, and the non-spatial half of abstract reasoning may need a second, differently-organised system — which is what the social-cognition network looks like here. Bites [[wiki/concepts/abstract-structural-codes.md]] (T4, T37) and the transfer claim behind `g` | `LIVE` — B is one 18-subject fMRI study but the only matched control in the literature the wiki holds; A rests on many unmatched contrasts plus animal recordings. Two unexcluded reconciliations: (i) familiarity — Kumaran's social network was years old while positive social-map results used newly-learned spaces, so this may be the map-vs-memory schedule (T28) rather than a domain effect, though the spatial framework was equally old; Garvert et al. 2017 sharpens this — a one-day-old, non-spatial, discrete graph does drive entorhinal cortex — so recency plus novelty currently predicts a hippocampal-formation map better than spatiality does; (ii) fMRI nulls are weak evidence, and BOLD in a task where the answer is retrieved rather than computed may miss a code that is present ([[wiki/concepts/cognitive-map.md]]); (iii) **the abstract dimension may ride on the spatial one rather than replace it** — mouse dorsal CA1 cells have firing fields in a 2-D accumulated-evidence × position space, but ~90% of them are *conjunctive*, so the non-spatial variable is carried by the same cells that carry space and was accumulated while navigating. That is consistent with both poles at once: the map takes non-perceptual dimensions (A) and it was never asked to do so without space present (B). The experiment T45 needs is the abstract variable with the navigation removed (Nieh et al. 2021, [[wiki/concepts/population-geometry.md]]); (iv) **the recency reading now has a matched-domain positive.** Park et al. 2021 ran a *social* space built inside the experiment (two 1-D rank orders over 16 people, learned on separate days) and recovered hippocampal and entorhinal 2-D distance coding plus a grid-like code for inferred vectors — in the same subjects whose mentalising network (temporoparietal junction, superior temporal sulcus, dorsal medial prefrontal cortex) was engaged and *shared the entorhinal grid orientation*. So position B's regions and position A's regions are not alternatives in a newly learned social space; they are co-aligned. This does not rescue A for over-learned structure, and it does not supply a matched *spatial* control, but it moves the live variable from spatial-vs-social toward old-vs-new and removes the reading in which social relational inference is handled by a second, differently-organised system |
| T46 | **When landmarks and self-motion disagree, which one sets the structural code?** | **The landmark, by overwriting the integrator.** In the fly ellipsoid-body compass the entire population is imaged at once and the conflict is imposed directly: instantaneously displacing the visual cue makes the heading bump jump to follow it while *preserving its offset*, and varying the closed-loop gain between turning and scene rotation makes the bump track the **cue** with only slight scaling by the animal's own rotation. Self-motion runs the code only when no landmark is available (darkness), and then it drifts. The mammalian head-direction system is described the same way — distal cues rotate head-direction tuning, place fields and grid fields with them (Seelig & Jayaraman 2015; Epstein et al. 2017, [[wiki/concepts/cognitive-map.md]]) | **Physical self-motion, at least for position.** With visual and physical motion decoupled in mouse virtual reality, mEC grid cells predominantly follow *physical motion* while hippocampal place cells follow *visual input* (Chen et al. 2019), and mouse mEC grid firing survives complete darkness on non-visual self-motion alone (Dannenberg et al. 2020) — so the entorhinal integrator is not vision-dominated in the way the heading compass is ([[wiki/concepts/path-integration.md]]) | Decides the update discipline for any anchored structural code: a *reset* (overwrite `g` from the anchor, keep the offset) versus a *blend* (weight the two evidence streams by precision). The wiki's models do neither explicitly — TEM corrects drift by memory recall, which is closer to a blend, while the fly measurement says the correction is discrete and offset-preserving. Also bites G39 (what anchoring does once the anchor is identified) and G43 (concurrent integrators can plainly disagree about which cue stream to trust) | `LIVE` — the cleanest reconciliation is that these are different variables: heading is cheap to re-anchor and useless when wrong, so vision wins; position is anchored by boundaries rather than by any single landmark and has no equally fast reset. Untested — no experiment in the wiki imposes the same conflict on a heading code and a position code in one animal |
| T47 | **Are concurrent structural codes independent frames or one code read out in many places?** | **Independent.** Chen et al. 2022 hold that grid, place and head-direction cells in primary somatosensory and secondary visual cortex arise from a generalized path-integration principle running on each area's *own* self-motion signal — optic flow, proprioception, head movement — and are "complementary yet functionally independent" of the entorhinal integrator; the columnar version makes this thousands of object-anchored frames voting ([[wiki/concepts/distributed-reference-frames.md]]) | **Registered, or singular.** The only case where two regions' frame orientations have been estimated in one task finds them *aligned*: entorhinal and ventromedial-prefrontal hexagonal codes over the same conceptual bird space share a grid angle (`t21 = 2.18`), each stable across sessions more than a week apart (Constantinescu et al. 2016). Alignment is equally consistent with one entorhinal code propagated to prefrontal cortex, which would delete every fMRI row from the replication evidence | Decides whether a model needs an arbitration mechanism over many `g` modules (G43) or a single `g` with distributed readouts — and if frames are merely *registered*, arbitration collapses from coordinate conversion to weighting | `LIVE` — the evidence on each side uses a different instrument (single units for independence, BOLD for alignment) and neither can address the other's claim. Discriminating test: a conflict design where conceptual and spatial axes are rotated relative to one another, asking whether the two angles dissociate |
| T48 | **Where is an inferred, never-experienced association computed — inside the medial temporal lobe or downstream?** | **Inside.** The retrieved-context account has hippocampal reinstatement of an item's stored context pull back everything else that shared it, so transitive and backward associations fall out of medial-temporal machinery alone; hippocampal lesion abolishes exactly those and spares forward association (Howard et al. 2005, [[wiki/entities/temporal-context-model.md]]); recurrent-loop models likewise recirculate within the medial temporal lobe until the inferred item is reinstated | **Downstream.** With the intermediary `Y` and the outcome `Z` dissociated by a many-to-one cue mapping, human and mouse hippocampus during the inference cue represents `Y` and shows *no* evidence for `Z` (nor for `Y`'s value), while `Z` is decodable in medial prefrontal cortex and midbrain — including for neutral outcomes. The hippocampus is causally necessary (optogenetic dorsal-CA1 silencing at the cue abolishes the inference) but supplies the *link*, not the answer (Barron et al. 2020) | Decides the shape of the memory module in an architecture. If A, a fast store that iterates on its own output can return composed answers and the reasoner is one module. If B, the store returns a one-hop retrieval and composition must be completed by a separate downstream valuator — which also means a system whose memory recirculates until convergence is modelling the wrong anatomy | `LIVE` — the two are not strictly incompatible: Barron's ripple result shows the composed `X→Z` link itself being written *in* hippocampus offline, so the disagreement may be about timing (link stored inside, answer assembled outside) rather than about locus. Barron's own caveat is that the multi-day schedule may have already consolidated the outcome code out of hippocampus; a single-day version might put `Z` in both |
| T49 | **How densely is CA3 recurrently connected — and does pattern completion need *structured* wiring or just enough of it?** | **Sparse and non-random.** Rat paired recordings give 0.9% pyramidal→pyramidal connectivity, with the few connections embedded in richly interconnected subnetworks showing an over-representation of disynaptic motifs; a full-scale binary model completes patterns *only because* of that non-randomness (Guzman et al. 2016). Rolls' capacity theory sits nearer this end, assuming dilution 0.04 and arguing dilution is functional ([[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]]) | **Dense and random.** Two independent techniques in mouse agree on roughly 10×: 3D electron microscopy gives 11.2 ± 2.7% within 50 µm (5.5 ± 1.9% by 250 µm), octuple patch gives 8.8% (103/1,172 pairs, 52 mice) with reciprocal pairs (6/586) and all disynaptic motifs at chance given that rate; a spiking model with log-normal weights replays sequences over a wide parameter range at `c ≈ 8.8%` with purely random connectivity (Sammons et al. 2023). Proposed causes of the discrepancy: species (rat vs mouse), and slice storage (beaker vs interface chamber) | Decides whether an artificial fast store must *learn its own topology* (motif enrichment as a prerequisite for completion) or can be wired at random and rely on density — and by how much the wiki's CA3 capacity numbers are off, since `C^RC = c·N` | `LIVE` — both sides are direct measurements, neither is reproduced across species, and slicing truncates axons in both, so both are lower bounds. The EM arm is one animal; the structural distance-dependence it reports is absent functionally and is attributed to the thinner (62 µm vs 400 µm) section |
| T50 | **Is an abstract hippocampal code low-dimensional, or is low dimensionality a red herring?** | **Low-dimensional.** Abstraction is compression: irrelevant detail is discarded and the conditions collapse onto a small number of orthogonal coding directions, so a disentangled geometry is by construction low-dimensional — the reading the wiki has been carrying since Nieh et al. 2021 (`d ≈ 5` in a ~450-dimensional space), and the reason hippocampal maps are contrasted with decorrelated high-dimensional codes built for memory capacity | **Neither — the two axes are independent, and they rose together.** In human hippocampus the emergence of inference brought higher CCGP and parallelism for context *and stimulus* **and** higher shattering dimensionality (0.57 → 0.62), with the parity dichotomy — a pure nonlinear interaction no linear code supplies — becoming decodable, and the *hardest* dichotomies decoded best (Courellis et al. 2024). **And the earlier, stronger version is a rejected null rather than a co-occurrence:** in monkey hippocampus, DLPFC and ACC, shattering dimensionality is > 0.7 before stimulus onset and > 0.88 after, in every area *significantly greater than* that of a perfectly factorised representation — eight condition means at the vertices of a randomly rotated cuboid whose side lengths were fitted to reproduce the observed CCGP for context, value and action. So the disentangled model that accounts for the abstraction is statistically excluded by the expressiveness (Bernardi et al. 2020) | Whether "disentangle the representation" implies "reduce its dimensionality" in a model. If it does not, then a regulariser that pushes intrinsic dimension down is not an abstraction objective, and the wiki's pattern-separation/decorrelation machinery (high-dimensional, for capacity) and its structural-code machinery (abstract, for transfer) stop being rivals for the same population ([[wiki/concepts/pattern-separation-completion.md]], [[wiki/concepts/abstract-structural-codes.md]], T29) | `LEANING B` — B is a direct measurement of both quantities in one population against a behavioural contrast, now in two species, and in the monkey case with the factorised model explicitly rejected as a null; A is an inference from geometry, never measured alongside an expressiveness statistic |
| T51 | **When one item set is embedded in several relational structures, does the map integrate them or hold them in parallel?** | **Integrate — a conjunctive map.** The wiki's two cleanest multi-dimensional results both find fusion. Park et al. 2021: two 1-D rank orders over 16 people, learned on separate days and never co-presented, produce hippocampal/entorhinal dissimilarity scaling with **2-D Euclidean** distance in the product space, significantly better than with either 1-D order alone, plus a grid-like code over inferred vectors across the plane ([[wiki/concepts/cognitive-map.md]]). Nieh et al. 2021: ~90% of evidence-tuned mouse dorsal-CA1 cells are genuinely **conjunctive** for evidence × position rather than tuned to either alone ([[wiki/concepts/population-geometry.md]]). Qu et al. 2026 replicate the Park result at n = 203 and in a different modality: medial prefrontal cortex codes the **2-D Euclidean** distance between items on an attack × defence rank map significantly better than either 1-D rank (`t₂₀₂` = 2.69), so the two dimensions are combined rather than consulted separately. The theory side assumes fusion throughout — a place cell is `p = f(g̃ ⊙ x̃)`, one code binding structure to content ([[wiki/empirical-tensions.md]] T40, T42) | **Parallel and anatomically separate.** Zheng et al. 2024 re-analysed Garvert et al. 2017 with link distance, semantic distance and residual distance entered together: both relational structures over the same 12 objects appear in the hippocampal formation in **non-overlapping** clusters (link → bilateral entorhinal, semantic → bilateral hippocampus, semantic posterior to link on the long axis), each effect statistically zero in the other's region of interest (`t₂₂` = −0.25 and 0.54), and residual/perceptual distance nowhere. The authors' conclusion is explicit: the hippocampal formation "does not construct conjunctive maps that integrate similarities across distinct stimulus dimensions" | Decides the default representation for an entity that participates in more than one structure — one embedding whose metric mixes every known relation, or `k` maps plus a selector. If B, then every wiki architecture that learns a single similarity function per item pair is averaging structures that biology keeps apart, and the missing machinery is a **routing policy over parallel maps** (G12, G40) rather than a better single embedding. If A, the missing machinery is a binding operation and the parallel result is an artefact of the anatomy being asked at fMRI resolution | `LIVE` — the reconciliation both sides permit is that the **acquisition profile** decides: Park's two dimensions were learned in the same mode, at the same timescale, inside one experiment, and fused; Zheng's differ in mode (implicit vs explicit), timescale (one day vs a lifetime) and consolidation stage, and did not. That predicts a switch, not a fact, and no design has varied the profile while holding content fixed. Two instrument caveats: Zheng's separation cannot distinguish a long-axis **gradient** from two clusters given fMRI spatial autocorrelation (the source says so), and a null in one region of interest at n = 23 is weak evidence of absent code; conversely Park's 2-D-beats-1-D contrast is a model comparison over a *fitted* dissimilarity, which cannot detect a second structure that was never modelled |
| T52 | **Is the hippocampal spatial code carried by the sparse, selective excitatory population, or is it equally present in the dense inhibitory one?** | **Sparse pyramidal cells are the code.** The whole capacity argument runs on sparseness — `p_max ≈ kC/(a ln(1/a))` prices memories in a population with `a ≈ 0.02–0.33`, separation exists to keep those codes non-overlapping, and interneurons enter every model in the wiki as a normalizer that *enforces* the sparseness rather than as a carrier of content (Rolls 2013, [[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]]; [[wiki/entities/spiking-tem.md]], where removing theta inhibition destroys the place code by destroying sparsity) | **Position is decodable from interneurons just as well.** All four families show stable position-tuned rate modulation tiling the whole maze, and a CEBRA decoder trained on interneurons only matches one trained on pyramidal cells down-sampled to the same count (`p = 0.63`); leaving out *Vip*, *Id2* or *Pvalb* each raises the error (`p < 0.03`), so the families are not redundant with one another either (Valero et al. 2025, [[wiki/concepts/inhibitory-control-of-coding.md]]) | Whether "population X decodes the variable" licenses "population X is the representation" — the inference every probing result in the wiki makes ([[wiki/concepts/representation-probing.md]]), and whether a machine store's content must live in its sparse selective units or may also live in the dense control pathway that shapes them | `LIVE` — the two are not straightforwardly contradictory (decodability is not storage, and a signal that *sets* the code inherits information about it), but the wiki has no principle that separates the two readings, and the test is small: 57 interneurons, five sessions, two mice |
| T53 | **Is orthogonalization of aliased states a requirement, or a free choice the brain happens to make?** | **A requirement, and the brain's own solution.** Mouse dorsal CA1 drives visually identical, latently distinct task states to near-orthogonality (population-vector angle → 90°) over weeks of learning, in a fixed order, and the wiki's whole capacity argument runs on sparse orthogonal codes being what memory and intelligence need ([[wiki/concepts/pattern-separation-completion.md]], Sun et al. 2025) | **A free choice.** On the identical symbol stream, ReLU and sigmoid recurrent networks, long short-term memory networks and transformers reach full next-observation accuracy with their aliased states still strongly correlated — perfect performance only needs separability in the low-dimensional subspace the readout uses, and every other direction is unconstrained. Orthogonality appears only when it is *imposed*: soft winner-take-all activation, feedback inhibition, or an explicit correlation penalty in the loss (Sun et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/cscg.md]]) | Whether decorrelation goes into the objective slot (G30) as a term to be paid for, or is expected to fall out of prediction. If B, then behavioural success — including out-of-distribution success — is not evidence that a model split the nodes, which adds a class of false positive to G17 | `LEANING B` on the mechanism, `A` on the normative question — B is a controlled comparison of six architectures on one task; what B does *not* explain is why biology pays for the extra property, and the candidate answers (memory capacity, interference, reusability of states across task variants) are untested here |
| T54 | **What does a hippocampal representation do when the environment is metrically distorted?** | **It rescales.** The metric reading of the map: fields stretch or compress with the environment, as grid fields distort when walls move and place fields track distance to boundaries ([[wiki/concepts/cognitive-map.md]] element 2) | **It snaps to a discrete state.** Lengthening the grey zones of the 2ACDC track produces no uniform stretching: on near trials the pre-near-reward tuning *persists* through the extension, on far trials cells *jump* to the pre-far-reward state as if anticipating the later reward, and both reset on the first disambiguating cue (Sun et al. 2025) | Decides whether the machine analogue is a continuous chart with a scale parameter or a state machine with an observation-triggered reset — and therefore whether interpolation between states is meaningful at all | `LIVE` — the two are measured in different paradigms (open-field boundary manipulation vs. a 1-D virtual corridor with a strong task-state structure), so the honest reading is that the map's format depends on whether the task defines discrete states, which is itself the untested claim ([[wiki/empirical-tensions.md]] T27) |
| T55 | **Should a fast store's addressing be random, or should it follow the data?** | **Random, and deliberately so.** Randomisation is what makes codes non-overlapping without any statistics of the input: dentate mossy-fibre hashing hands CA3 an effectively random address ([[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]]), and a fixed random projection of a prestructured grid code yields provably convex, equal-sized, spurious-free basins ([[wiki/entities/vector-hash.md]]). The benefit is that it assumes nothing about the data and cannot be misled by it | **Random addressing is optimal only for *uniform* data, and real data clusters.** With clustered data a uniformly-sampled address set leaves most locations never activated (wasted) while a few are written repeatedly until their contents are noise; the measured fixes are all data-dependent — hard addresses sampled from the data address distribution (best NETtalk results; "dramatic" improvement on spoken digits), self-organising-map placement, per-location activation radii `H_m`, or per-cue weighted Hamming distance — and the optimal active fraction is itself *smaller* under clustering than the uniform formula gives (Kanerva 1993, [[wiki/entities/sparse-distributed-memory.md]]) | Whether the addressing stage of a fast store may stay frozen at initialisation. If B holds generally, Vector-HaSH's exponential capacity and Rolls–Treves' randomisation argument are results about *uniformly spread* content, and any real content distribution costs a fraction of the store that nobody has measured. Two reconciliations: (i) the codes being hashed in both A cases are already near-uniform (grid phases; sparse DG output), so the clustering B worries about was removed upstream — which relocates the problem to whatever produces that code rather than dissolving it; (ii) A's stores address by a *structural* variable and B's by content, and only content clusters | `LIVE` |
| T56 | **What is theta-paced flickering between hippocampal maps?** | **The inference algorithm.** The posterior over hidden states is represented by *sampling*: one state drawn per theta cycle, at a rate proportional to its probability, which is why maps alternate rapidly after ambiguous evidence and why switching should decline as the evidence ratio moves away from 0 (Sanders et al. 2020, [[wiki/entities/hidden-state-inference-remapping.md]]; the same reading is offered for mental exploration among candidate contexts) | **The discharge of a synaptic transient.** Two competing attractor maps with Tsodyks–Markram short-term plasticity: the abandoned map's recurrent synapses rebound (`τ_f = 1.9 s > τ_r = 0.6 s`), giving it a gain advantage that theta troughs periodically let win a cycle. Flickers decay because the rebound does, and re-arm each other, which is why the transient outlasts every time constant in the system (Mark et al. 2017, [[wiki/entities/stp-flickering-cann.md]]) | Whether observed map alternation is evidence that the brain represents a *distribution* over contexts, or only that attractor selection carries a recency term. The wiki's retrieval-and-allocation policy ([[wiki/concepts/contextual-inference.md]]) leans on the first; the exportable machine design differs — sample from a posterior, versus add a decaying per-slot gain bias plus a periodic threshold dip | `LIVE` — the decaying switch rate is predicted by both, so it does not discriminate. Two asymmetric discriminators exist and neither is decisive: B predicts flicker rate falls with **distance travelled from the switch position** (partial `r = −0.157`, `p = 0.027`, but distance and velocity are confounded at `c = 0.64`) and A has no mechanism for that; B's own control shows a *noise-driven* network without short-term plasticity flickers at a **constant** rate with no dependence on teleportation, which rules out generic noise but not a sampler. A also has the wider brief — it explains flickering between maps that were never recently active, which a rebound cannot |
| T57 | **How should a memory store a sequence — the successor state, or the increment that generates it?** | **Store the increment.** Chaining states costs `O(N)` bits of recurrent storage per step and empirically caps out at ≈50 steps for an asymmetric Hopfield network and ≈100 with a scaffold; storing the 2-D velocity on a prestructured manifold instead reaches ~1.5×10⁵ steps, with recall length degrading only as the *log* of the number of admissible shift vectors, because the manifold's own dynamics regenerate the state (Chandra et al. 2023, [[wiki/entities/vector-hash.md]]) | **The ≈50-step ceiling is an artefact of the interaction function, not of state-chaining.** Keep the asymmetric weights and pass the state–pattern overlap through a steep nonlinearity before it drives the next state: capacity becomes polynomial in `N` at `f(x)=x^d` and exponential at `f(x)=e^{(N−1)(x−1)}`, with 200,000 correlated MovingMNIST frames recalled perfectly at `N=784` (Chaudhry et al. 2023, [[wiki/entities/dense-sequence-memory.md]]) | Decides whether a sequence memory needs an action algebra on its latent (hence a path-integrable code, [[wiki/concepts/path-integration.md]]) or only a sharper read-out. The two costs are not comparable as stated: A pays `log\|actions\|` bits/step but requires the latent to have a group structure and cannot store a step that is not an admissible shift; B pays `O(N)` bits/step but stores arbitrary successors, at the price of one hidden unit per stored pattern in its biologically plausible form — so B's exponential scaling is exponential in the *visible* count only. Neither branches | `LIVE` — untested against each other; no published comparison runs a Polynomial or Exponential `DenseNet` on Vector-HaSH's benchmark or vice versa |
| T58 | **Where does the theta-band structure of hippocampal sequences come from — a rhythm imposed on the network, or a bifurcation inside it?** | **Imposed.** Theta is an external drive that the network is subject to: an 8–10 Hz oscillation added to the input current, whose troughs periodically collapse global inhibition and reset the competition between representations ([[wiki/entities/stp-flickering-cann.md]]), or a clock supplied to schedule inference steps ([[wiki/entities/spiking-tem.md]]). Sequence content is then read out *within* a cycle whose timing the network does not set | **Emergent.** No clock anywhere: at intermediate adaptation gain the tracking fixed point loses stability through a Hopf bifurcation, and the resulting limit cycle has frequency `ω = √(2√π αak(1+m)/[ττ_v(J₀+2√π akα)])`, which lands in the theta band for biological parameters and is *independent of running speed*, reproducing theta sweeps and both phase precession and procession from the same solution ([[wiki/entities/adaptive-cann.md]]) | Whether an architecture needs an oscillator module at all. Under A the rhythm is a scheduling signal that a builder must supply and tune; under B it is a symptom of operating near an instability, and its frequency is *diagnostic* of gain rather than settable. The two make opposite predictions under manipulation of adaptation: emergent theta frequency should shift with adaptation strength (as `√(1+m)`) while an imposed rhythm should not, and B additionally predicts that suppressing adaptation abolishes sweeps without abolishing theta in the local field potential | `LIVE` |
| T59 | **Does a stored memory live in the synaptic weights, or in a mask over them?** | The wiki's default and the premise of every capacity result it holds: content is written into the plastic matrix by a Hebbian or pseudoinverse rule, and the weights *are* the memory — [[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]], [[wiki/entities/sparse-distributed-memory.md]] (plastic counters), [[wiki/entities/dense-sequence-memory.md]], [[wiki/entities/vector-hash.md]] (plastic matrix over fixed addresses). Corrupting the weights destroys the memory | Podlaski et al. 2025: apply contextual synaptic refinement to a **symmetric Gaussian random matrix containing no information about any pattern**, and arbitrary chosen patterns become stable attractors with the same overlap `m*_rand` and capacity `α*_rand` as the infinite-context Hebbian case. The weight matrix needs only zero-mean random structure; the memory is the binary mask. Consequences the authors draw: weights may be corrupted substantially with no loss, and **bounded/binary synapses suffice** ([[wiki/entities/context-modular-memory-network.md]]) | Decides where a builder spends precision and where forgetting happens. If content can live in a `N²`-bit mask over a frozen random substrate, then per-environment storage is bits not reals, weight noise is harmless, and the long-standing bounded-synapse objection to Hebbian capacity dissolves. If not, the result is an artefact of the mask being as expressive as the matrix it masks | `LIVE` — the demonstration is real but the mask is per-synapse and per-context, which the authors themselves price as a control network scaling `s·N`, i.e. larger than the memory it addresses; nothing shows the result survives at the coarser (dendritic-branch) gating resolution that is biologically defensible |
| T60 | **What substrate defines a large-scale attractor landscape — anatomy or activity covariance?** | **Anatomy.** The standard whole-brain modelling programme couples neural-mass models through the *structural* connectome (diffusion MRI) and derives dynamics from it; the landscape is a consequence of physical wiring, and structure–function coupling is the thing to be explained (Deco et al. 2012; Golos et al. 2015; Hansen et al. 2015, cited in Englert et al. 2026) | **Activity covariance.** Use the *functional* connectome directly as the coupling matrix (`J = −Σ⁻¹`) and skip the structure–function problem entirely: the resulting model has two hyperparameters instead of dozens, converges ~9× faster than degree-preserving permutations of itself, replicates at `r = 0.93` across three scanners, and predicts task and disease perturbations without refitting ([[wiki/entities/fcann.md]]) | Whether a builder derives a memory/dynamics landscape from wiring constraints or from observed co-activation. Position B buys accuracy and simplicity and gives up every mechanistic (polysynaptic, biophysical) claim; it also cannot be falsified by the covariance fit alone, since a Boltzmann-form model fitted to covariance reproduces covariance by construction — its evidence has to come from convergence speed, eigenvector alignment, flow and perturbation. The two are not exclusive: the stated next step is to use structure as a *sparsity mask* on `J` and learn the rest | `LIVE` |
| T61 | **Are the patterns a fast store holds random, or orthogonalised?** | **Random.** Every capacity result the wiki carries assumes i.i.d. patterns written by a Hebbian rule — `p_max ≈ kC/(a ln(1/a))` ([[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]]), the `s`-context gating multipliers ([[wiki/entities/context-modular-memory-network.md]]), the sequence bounds ([[wiki/entities/dense-sequence-memory.md]]) — and randomness is defended as a feature, since it needs no statistics of the input (T55) | **Orthogonalised.** Free-energy minimisation writes with `ΔJ_ij ∝ σ_iσ_j − L(·)σ_j`, whose anti-Hebbian term subtracts what the network already predicts, driving stored states toward mutual orthogonality — a Kanter–Sompolinsky projector network with maximal capacity and error-free recall. Human functional connectomes show the predicted signature: attractors aligned with the eigenvectors of their own coupling matrix ([[wiki/entities/fcann.md]]) | Every capacity number in the wiki. Random-pattern bounds are the *pessimistic* case; if a local rule can orthogonalise, the ceiling moves and the separation/completion knob (G38) is partly redundant — orthogonality is bought by the learning rule rather than by sparsifying the code. Caveats on B: the rule is never actually run in the empirical work (orthogonality is inferred from a signature in fitted weights), the demonstration is at 122 coarse-grained macroscopic units rather than at neurons, and Chaudhry et al. 2023 report that the batch pseudoinverse route to the same decorrelation is non-local and numerically unstable | `LIVE` |
| T62 | **Should a fast associative store hold instances, or model a distribution?** | **Instances, written in one shot.** The wiki's fast store is defined by one-episode acquisition: the Hebbian outer product is closed-form in the patterns ([[wiki/entities/hopfield-network.md]]), behavioural-timescale plasticity writes a receptive field from a single traversal ([[wiki/concepts/synaptic-plasticity.md]]), and the argument against gradient learning as the episodic mechanism is exactly that 10⁴–10⁶ exposures cannot be one episode ([[wiki/concepts/complementary-learning-systems.md]]) | **A distribution, learned iteratively.** Recall of what was shown is not generation of what was not: to produce novel states in the same style the store must fit `p(x)`, which forces a `−log Z` term whose gradient is only obtainable by free-running the network, hence many passes and two phases with no closed form ([[wiki/entities/boltzmann-machine.md]]) | Whether one substrate can be both the episodic buffer and the generative model, or whether the wiki needs two write rules over the same weights. The stakes are concrete for replay: position A replays *stored* episodes, position B must replay *model samples*, and [[wiki/concepts/offline-replay.md]] reports biology doing the latter (replay of never-experienced trajectories) while the fast-store literature assumes the former | `LIVE` — no source has tried both writes on one matrix; the nearest partial answer is that the two rules differ only in the subtracted term, so a single-phase local rule that predicts its own correlations ([[wiki/entities/fcann.md]]) may implement B at A's cost |
| T63 | **Is the unit that stores a pattern the neuron or the dendritic segment?** | **The neuron.** Every capacity result the wiki carries counts patterns per *cell* and treats the cell as a thresholded sum over its synapses: `p_max ≈ kC/(a ln(1/a))` with `C` the fan-in per neuron ([[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]]), Hopfield's `0.14N` with `N` neurons, the counters of [[wiki/entities/sparse-distributed-memory.md]] pooled by one output unit. Population geometry is measured the same way, one dimension per recorded cell | **The segment.** A pyramidal cell carries ~10⁴ synapses in 100–400-synapse segments, each an independent detector firing on 8–20 co-active clustered synapses, so one cell recognises *hundreds* of patterns and no somatic threshold arbitrates between them; the summing unit is a 20–300-synapse stretch of dendrite and the cell's output is closer to an OR over detections (Ahmad & Hawkins 2016, [[wiki/concepts/dendritic-computation.md]]) | Every unit count and every capacity number in the wiki, in *opposite* directions depending on the mapping: if an artificial unit models a segment, a 10⁴-cell column is 10⁶ units; if it models a cell, artificial networks have ~100× less pattern memory per unit than the biology they are compared against. It also bites credit assignment (a signal must reach a branch, not a cell — [[wiki/concepts/biologically-plausible-credit-assignment.md]]) and interpretability (a "neuron's" tuning curve is a disjunction over ~100 unrelated detectors, which is polysemanticity as an anatomical fact rather than a training artefact). Caveats on B: the paper models no dendrite-to-soma conversion, so "OR over segments" is a conjecture about the output function rather than a result, and its numbers are recognition-error bounds, not storage capacities — the two positions may be measuring different quantities on the same hardware | `LIVE` |
| T64 | **Is the dendritic tree's *structure* load-bearing, or only its sparsity?** | **Structure.** Chavlis & Poirazi 2025 report that dendritic ANNs — a tree partition (each soma reads its own disjoint dendrites) plus receptive-field-restricted sampling — beat randomly connected sparse ANNs (sANNs) of matched size on both efficiency scores across all datasets tested, and argue the point is that *nature located a good sparse architecture* in a space too vast to search, so it can be copied instead of pruned or evolved ([[wiki/entities/dendritic-ann.md]]) | **Only sparsity (plus input sampling).** The same paper's controls: when sANNs are given the *same* input-sampling rule, so the only difference is structured vs. random internal connectivity, the efficiency gap shrinks — "especially for larger models" — and dense ANNs given restricted input sampling alone already recover much of the gain. The tree may be one adequate member of a large equivalence class of sparse graphs at the right density | Decides whether "dendritic" is an architectural prior worth importing or a rebranding of sparsity. If B, the transferable rule is *restrict fan-in and pick receptive fields*, and any mask of that density will do; if A, the partition itself constrains which input variables may interact and is a prior over the latent graph's edge set ([[wiki/concepts/latent-graph-discovery.md]]) | `LIVE` — the decisive experiment (structured vs. random at matched sparsity, matched sampling, *at scale*) is exactly the one whose gap the paper reports as narrowing, and it was not run beyond the small-model regime |
| T65 | **Do cortical lateral connections link similar units, or deliberately mix dissimilar ones?** | **Like connects to like.** Superficial-layer patches reciprocally link domains with matching functional properties — cytochrome-oxidase blobs to blobs, like eye dominance to like eye dominance, like orientation preference to like (Livingstone & Hubel 1984; Malach et al. 1993; Yoshioka et al. 1996), and in tree shrew the patch distribution extends along the visual-field axis matching the cells' preferred orientation (Bosking et al. 1997). Read as Hebbian clustering, the lateral net is an associative memory over tuning similarity | **Mixing is the point.** Patch diameter tracks the basal dendritic spread of the layer-3 pyramids across areas (largest patches, 481 µm, in macaque motor cortex, which has the largest pyramids), so only a cell at a patch's centre samples pure patch input and only a cell mid-gap samples pure non-patch — everything in between receives a graded *mixture*, and the size matching exists to **maximise input diversity** (Malach 1992). Supporting it: like-to-like correlations appear only 400–1000 µm from the injection site, while at short range axons freely innervate functionally diverse territory (Kisvárday et al. 1997; Stettler et al. 2002), and individual cells within one patch each target a *different subset* of the patch set | Decides what a recurrent lateral layer is *for* in a machine model: an attractor/completion net over similar states, or a mixing stage that manufactures conjunctive selectivity by construction ([[wiki/concepts/population-geometry.md]]). Under B, mixed selectivity is a wiring consequence of dendrite-to-patch scale matching and needs no learning to explain; under A it is evidence of a learned code | `LIVE` — Douglas & Martin 2004 endorse B and note A's assumption of unbiased dendritic sampling fails in some layer-4 populations (macaque V1, rat barrel cortex), so the two may hold in different layers. Unresolvable from tracer data alone: patch labelling cannot say which of the labelled cells a given dendrite actually contacts |
| T66 | **Does layer 6 supply a modulator signal, or the location code that decides what a column represents?** | **Modulator.** Douglas & Martin 2004: L6 is the canonical source of *modulator*-type excitation — thin axons, small en-passant boutons — which changes a target's response quantitatively without altering the structure of its receptive field, and its main projections (L6 → L4, L6 → thalamus) are read as gain control on the incoming evidence stream ([[wiki/concepts/canonical-cortical-microcircuit.md]]) | **The location code.** The Thousand Brains Theory assigns L6 a grid-cell-like population path-integrating an efference copy from L5, whose output — broadcast via thalamus to L2/3 and L1 — is one of the *two* terms binding into an object representation. A feature at the wrong location is a different object, so this signal sets *what* the column represents, which is driver-grade content in the layer the anatomy types as modulatory ([[wiki/entities/thousand-brains-theory.md]]) | Decides whether a machine column's location input is a gain term (multiplicative gating on an evidence stream) or a first-class content input (half of a conjunctive code) — and, if the priming reading is right, dissolves the conflict: a signal can be structure-determining *and* sub-threshold, since it selects which units may fire without firing them | `LIVE` — the theory has no anatomy of its own behind the assignment, and Douglas & Martin's driver/modulator typing was imported from thalamic work with its inter-areal function stated as unexplored |
| T67 | **Where does mixed selectivity come from — wiring, or learning?** | **Wiring; it is free.** Conjunctive coding appears with no learning at all: fan-in restriction in a small dendritically wired network makes every unit see too little of the input to be class-specific (Chavlis & Poirazi 2025, [[wiki/entities/dendritic-ann.md]]), and lateral-patch/dendrite size matching means most superficial pyramids sample a patch/non-patch mixture by construction (Douglas & Martin 2004, [[wiki/concepts/canonical-cortical-microcircuit.md]]) | **Learning; random connectivity is not enough.** Prefrontal models do not reach measured levels of mixed selectivity under random connectivity, and do reach them once a Hebbian plasticity term is added (Wikipedia, *Hebbian theory*) `(tentative)` | Whether a reasoning architecture should *build in* an expansion/mixing stage or let one emerge from a local rule — and, if the second, whether the mixing directions inherit Hebb's PCA objective and therefore its bias toward high-variance nuisance structure ([[wiki/concepts/synaptic-plasticity.md]], [[wiki/concepts/shortcut-learning.md]]) | `LIVE` — separable by one measurement nobody in the wiki has run: does mixed selectivity grow with exposure and track input correlation structure (B), or is it present before training and stable through it (A)? ([[wiki/concepts/population-geometry.md]]) |
| T68 | **Does strong activity raise or lower a neuron's intrinsic excitability?** | **It lowers it — homeostatic intrinsic plasticity.** The standard account of intrinsic and synaptic conductance regulation is negative feedback: activity above a set point reduces excitability, which is what keeps a recurrent network with Hebbian potentiation from running away ([[wiki/concepts/synaptic-plasticity.md]], where every rule needs an external stabiliser) | **It raises it — and non-homeostatically.** Learning phosphorylates CREB for hours, which reduces K⁺ conductance, shrinks the post-burst after-hyperpolarization and lowers spike-frequency adaptation, so the cells that just encoded are *more* excitable and preferentially recruited into the next memory. This is stated by Lisman et al. 2018 as an explicit contrast with homeostatic regulation, and it is causally sufficient (KCNQ2/KCNQ3 dominant negatives increase allocation, Kir2.1 cuts activation probability ~5×) | Decides the **sign** of any intrinsic-excitability term a builder adds, and whether the fast store needs its own brake. Under A the term is a stabiliser and can be added freely; under B it is positive feedback whose only named brake is *saturation of synaptic strength* — a ceiling most machine implementations do not have, so importing the mechanism without a weight bound reproduces the runaway the biology avoids by accident ([[wiki/concepts/memory-allocation-excitability.md]]) | `LIVE` — the two may be one system at different timescales (transient anti-homeostatic tag over a slow homeostatic set point), but no source in the wiki states the composition, and nothing measures what happens to the tag when the homeostatic term is blocked |
| T69 | **Is a finite-nudge contrastive rule a biased approximation of backpropagation, or exact gradient descent on a different objective?** | **Biased approximation.** The standard statement of equilibrium propagation: the two-phase update equals the loss gradient only in the limit `β → 0`, and the finite `β` needed for stable learning introduces bias relative to the true gradient — the framing behind the whole "backpropagation-derived local rules generalize worse" result, whose signature (a deficit that does not close by scaling the step size) is exactly the bias branch (Schmidgall et al. 2023; Whittington & Bogacz 2019; Laborieux et al. 2021, whose contribution is *reducing* the estimator bias). **A second, independent argument for small `β`, which is not about bias at all** (Scellier & Bengio 2017): the fully clamped limit `β → ∞` — contrastive Hebbian learning — optimises `E(u^∞) − E(u⁰)`, which can go *negative* when the free and clamped fixed points land in different modes of the energy landscape, and learning then deteriorates (Movellan 1990). Weak clamping is precisely what lets the implicit-function theorem hold `u^β` in the same basin as `u⁰`. On this reading `β` must be small enough to preserve the mode, whatever it does to the gradient | **Exact gradient, different objective.** Modelling the state as a Gibbs distribution rather than a minimiser, the contrastive update is the exact gradient of `J(θ) = A(θ,1) − A(θ,0)` for *any* finite `β`, with no convexity, unique-minimum, weight-symmetry or infinitesimal assumption; the apparent bias is `T·KL(ρ_1‖ρ_0)`, a deliberate regulariser (`J = E_{ρ_1}[ℓ] + T·KL(ρ_1‖ρ_0)`, and `J ≤ E_{ρ_0}[ℓ]`). Empirically the small-`β` regime is not safer but **unusable**: Fashion-MNIST, one `tanh` hidden layer, `β = 0.01` stalls at chance (20–30%) while `β = 1` and a path-integral variant reach ~80% tracking backpropagation, and the SNR of `Δs = s_β − s_0` sits below the sampling-noise floor for `β ≲ 10⁻²` (Litman 2025) | Decides how to score every two-phase local rule, and whether the generalization deficit transfers to them. Under A the rule inherits the deficit and repairs are impossible by averaging; under B it is unbiased for its own objective, all error is sampler variance (fixable by averaging), and the residual question is whether `J` is the objective wanted — which reopens the bias/variance table on [[wiki/concepts/equilibrium-propagation.md]] and on [[wiki/concepts/biologically-plausible-credit-assignment.md]] where contrastive Hebbian learning was listed as unplaced | `LEANING B` — B's theorems are unconditional given standard regularity, and its empirics invert A's assumption about which regime works. But the demonstration is one hidden layer on Fashion–MNIST at ~80%, no depth scaling and no generalization measurement, so B has not been tested where A's deficit was measured; and B's cosine similarity to `∇L_sup` tops out at ~0.5, which is consistent with `J` and `L_sup` diverging as the KL term grows. The mode-preservation argument is orthogonal to B and survives it: it bounds `β` from *above* for a reason B never addresses, so the two together predict a **window** — `β` large enough to clear the sampler's noise floor, small enough to keep both phases in one basin — rather than a monotone preference either way. Nothing measures where that window sits; B's demonstration is one `tanh` hidden layer, a landscape too simple for mode-crossing to arise, and B's Gibbs framing may dissolve the issue entirely at finite `T` (a distribution has no mode to leave) while reinstating it as `T → 0` |
| T70 | **Must a local approximation to backpropagation encode gradients as activity *differences*?** | **Yes — the NGRAD hypothesis.** Lillicrap et al. 2020 unify the entire family of local credit-assignment schemes by the claim that they all represent gradients implicitly as differences in neural activity, either spatially between populations or compartments (predictive coding error nodes, apical dendrites) or temporally between phases (contrastive Hebbian, equilibrium propagation). This is the wiki's organising taxonomy on [[wiki/concepts/biologically-plausible-credit-assignment.md]] — temporal-error vs. explicit-error, with the choice of *where the difference is taken* driving every architectural cost in the four-model table | **No.** Activation Relaxation converges to the exact backpropagated gradients with no layerwise activity difference anywhere: the value units themselves relax onto `∂L/∂x^l` as the equilibrium of `dx^l/dt = −x^l + x^{l+1}·∂x̄^{l+1}/∂x̄^l`, using one neuron type, one parallel backward phase and plain MLP connectivity (Millidge et al. 2020). The authors state it as an explicit counterexample requiring NGRAD to be redefined, while conceding the update can be *read* as a prediction error between a unit's activity and the layer above mapped through backward weights — a difference between two quantities that are never both instantiated as activities | Whether the wiki's temporal/explicit dichotomy is exhaustive, and therefore whether its cost table is the full menu. Under A a builder must pay one of the two documented prices — extra populations/compartments, or a phase pair with cross-phase storage. Under B there is a third cell where the second quantity is a second *use* of the same units, and the price is storing the forward activation across the value→gradient switch. The two positions may be notational: if "activity difference" is loosened to include a difference between a current activity and a stored one, B collapses into A and NGRAD becomes unfalsifiable — which is why B's real content is the cost table, not the taxonomy | `LIVE` |
| T71 | **To repair feedback alignment, should the feedback weights move toward the forward ones, or the forward weights toward the feedback?** | **Move the feedback.** Akrout et al. 2019 update `B` by a Hebbian rule that pushes it toward `Wᵀ`; Activation Relaxation needs the same — its backward weights `ψ` must be *learned* (Hebbian), and **fixed random feedback converged lower and tended to diverge** in that framework (Millidge et al. 2020). On this reading a fixed random `B` is a dead end and feedback plasticity is mandatory | **Move the forward weights.** Keep `B` fixed and random and add `−θ₂ · e_ℓ e_{ℓ−1}ᵀ` to the forward update: in a linear network `E[e_ℓ e_{ℓ−1}ᵀ ∣ B] ∝ Bᵀ`, so the forward weights are driven onto the transpose of the *random* matrix, closing most of the feedback-alignment/backpropagation gap in the online low-data deep regime where feedback alignment otherwise barely learns (Shervani-Tabar & Rosenbaum 2023). Bonus: it removes a two-iteration transport latency per layer that the feedback-side repairs do not touch | Decides which side of the pair a builder makes plastic — and therefore what anatomy is required. Under A the cortex needs plastic, error-carrying feedback projections whose rule is separately hyperparameter-sensitive (Kunin et al. 2020); under B feedback can be fixed random wiring, which is the far cheaper and more defensible claim, at the cost of needing a presynaptic error term at every layer (a synthetic `e₀` is stipulated for the input layer) | `LIVE` — the two have never been run head to head: A's evidence comes from a relaxation framework where the backward weights also carry gradients, B's from a feedforward pseudo-gradient setting at MNIST-class scale. Nor does anything say whether they compose or fight — driving `W → Bᵀ` while `B → Wᵀ` is a two-sided pursuit with no analysis of its fixed point |
| T72 | **Where do spacing effects come from — saturating variables inside a synapse, or a gate at the population level?** | **Synaptic.** Multi-timescale synapse models produce the inverted-U spacing curve unaided: fast internal variables saturate when repetitions are close together, so the effective weight change is largest at an intermediate interval, and the optimal spacing is set by those variables' time constants (Benna & Fusi 2016) | **Systems.** A non-monotonic gating function on long-term plasticity — consolidate when the fast store recalls the update at *intermediate* strength — produces the same curve, with the timescale set by population-level forgetting curves rather than by synaptic state; subdividing the slow store into sub-populations gated at different recall strengths further reproduces the observed smooth growth of retention timescale with training spacing (Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar 2024; Cepeda et al. 2006, 2008) | Decides where a spaced-repetition schedule should be implemented in a machine — as a learning-rate state variable per weight, or as a write-gate on a second module — and whether spacing is a property of the substrate or a policy that can be tuned | `LEANING B` for long intervals, and the discriminator is cheap and untested: the synaptic mechanism requires that **few other plasticity events intervene** between repetitions, because interposed events prevent the saturation the effect depends on, whereas the population gate is indifferent to them. Since real inter-repetition intervals are full of intervening experience, spacing effects measured over days are evidence for B specifically. Both authors expect both mechanisms to contribute |
| T73 | **Should a one-shot store allocate memories to *overlapping* cell populations, or to independently sampled ones?** | **Overlapping, deliberately.** Allocate-to-link: a CREB-dependent excitability tag decaying over hours biases the next memory into the cells that just encoded, and the resulting overlap *is* the linkage — demonstrated behaviourally by transfer of fear to a linked-but-never-paired context (Lisman et al. 2018, [[wiki/concepts/memory-allocation-excitability.md]]) | **Independently, and that independence is what makes the store work.** BTSP's plateau gate is modelled as an input-independent Poisson process at rate `f_q ≈ 0.005`, which spreads memory load uniformly and is credited with the flat recall curve across 10,000 sequentially learned items; the postsynaptically-gated one-shot rule it is compared against recruits the same cells repeatedly and degrades early traces badly (Wu & Maass 2025, [[wiki/entities/btsp-cam.md]]) | Decides whether a fast store's write-target rule may carry information at all. If A, temporal linkage is free and interference is the price; if B, non-interference is free and *every* edge between episodes must be built by a separate mechanism. Both are claims about hippocampal encoding, and both are supported — so the likely resolution is that they operate at different timescales (seconds-wide gating within an episode, hours-wide tagging across episodes) rather than that one is wrong | `LIVE` — no experiment has measured plateau incidence conditioned on a cell's recent excitability tag, which is the direct test |
| T74 | **Is content-addressable recall necessarily relaxation?** | **Yes — retrieval *is* the dynamics.** The wiki's attractor stores make completion a descent to a fixed point, and the cue is an initial condition rather than a key ([[wiki/entities/hopfield-network.md]], [[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]], [[wiki/concepts/attractor-dynamics.md]]); the property is derived from symmetric recurrence and a Lyapunov function | **No.** A two-layer feedforward binary-weight map returns the stored trace from cues with up to 1/3 of their bits masked, because one-shot gated learning makes each unit's weighted-sum distribution *bimodal* and the threshold sits in the gap — basins created by the learning, not by recurrence, and read in one step instead of ~100 (Wu & Maass 2025). CA1, the modelled area, has almost no recurrent collaterals | Whether "attractor" in this wiki names a dynamical property or merely a robustness property of a map. If B, every completion result quoted as evidence for recurrent attractor dynamics is also compatible with a feedforward store, and the argument for recurrence has to be made on sequences and search instead of on completion | `LEANING` toward B for *completion specifically* — the feedforward demonstration is explicit and quantitative, and nothing in it needs the recurrent case to be wrong; what recurrence still uniquely buys (motion, sequences, iterative refinement) is untouched |
| T75 | **Does expanding a code's dimensionality improve linear separability?** | **Yes — that is the point of expansion recoding.** Cover's theorem is the stated justification for reservoir computing, for the cerebellar granule-layer expansion in [[wiki/entities/sparse-distributed-memory.md]], and for [[wiki/concepts/sparse-distributed-representations.md]]'s recognition-by-subsampling, whose error rates fall superexponentially in dimension: lift the input into a higher-dimensional nonlinear space and the probability that the classes are linearly separable rises. Under this reading, more effective dimensions are strictly better and the only cost is units. | **Only up to a dataset-dependent point, after which it reverses.** Cazalets et al. 2025 measure effective dimensionality (fewest principal components reaching 90% variance) against cluster quality across ~500-unit reservoirs: the best-performing networks sit at *moderate* dimensionality with near-optimal within-class compactness, and architectures pushing dimensionality higher do not convert it into accuracy — "beyond a dataset-dependent tipping point, extra dimensions mainly inject noise and erode class structure". The operative split is **task-agnostic expressivity** vs **task-relevant effective dimensionality**, which are measured to come apart. | Every design choice justified by "expand, then separate linearly": reservoir size, granule-layer expansion ratios, the width of a random-feature basis, and any regulariser that rewards decorrelation for its own sake. It also bounds the reading of [[wiki/concepts/population-geometry.md]]'s shattering-dimensionality results in the other direction from T50 — high dimensionality is no more a proxy for a useful code than low dimensionality is a proxy for abstraction. **Not yet a real contradiction:** Cover's theorem is about *random* lifts of a fixed input, while the reversal is measured over architectures whose lifts differ in kind, so the two may be measuring different things. The discriminating experiment — hold the growth rule fixed and sweep `n` alone — is not run. **A third property splits the difference and is monotone in dimension:** *addressability*. `d ≥ log C(n,k)/log(1+1/γ)` is a necessary condition for a `d`-dimensional similarity store to be able to return every `k`-subset of `n` items, and the measured critical-`n` of freely optimised embeddings rises monotonically (cubically) in `d` with no reversal ([[wiki/concepts/retrieval-capacity.md]], Weller et al. 2026). So dimension is strictly good for *which questions can be asked* and possibly bad for *how well classes separate*, which suggests A and B are arguing about two different quantities that a single architecture must trade off. | `LIVE` |
| T76 | **Does spike-timing potentiation scale up or down with the existing weight?** | **Up — the rule is multiplicative.** `ΔJ_j = ε·J_j·[γ + Σ_f W(t_j^f − t^n)]`: the increment is proportional to the current weight, so the strongest synapses gain the most. This is what makes delay selection *self-reinforcing* rather than diffusive in the barn-owl model, and it is the form assumed wherever the wiki treats spike-timing plasticity as a competitive selection mechanism (Gerstner et al. 1996, [[wiki/concepts/temporal-coding.md]]) | **Down — potentiation is confined to weak synapses.** Percentage potentiation against log initial excitatory postsynaptic current gives `r = −0.72`, `p = 0.00017`, with significant LTP essentially absent above 500 pA; depression over the same range shows no strength dependence at all (`r = 0.037`, `p = 0.89`). The measured rule is multiplicative-down on the potentiating branch and additive on the depressing one (Bi & Poo 1998) | Decides whether a spike-timing rule can implement winner-take-all selection by amplitude. Under A, competition ends in a saturated subset and everything else at zero — the mechanism [[wiki/concepts/temporal-coding.md]] uses to tune delays and the mechanism that gives the rule its self-normalization. Under B the winners stop growing first, selection has to be carried entirely by the depressing lobe and by the nonspecific term `γ`, and the stationary weight distribution is heavy-tailed-but-unimodal rather than bimodal — which is the distribution actually measured in cortex (Sammons et al. 2023, [[wiki/concepts/synaptic-plasticity.md]]) | `LEANING B` on the physiology — B is the direct measurement and A is a modelling assumption made two years earlier; but the model's *results* (delay selection, self-normalization) have not been re-derived under B, so what A is doing in that construction is not yet known to be replaceable |
| T77 | **Is a population's existing latent basis a hard limit on what it can learn, or only a slow one?** | **A hard limit on any timescale a learner cares about.** Rhesus monkeys learn BCI decoder perturbations that stay inside the fitted 10-D intrinsic manifold within a single session, and fail on perturbations that require new co-modulation patterns — with initial impairment, principal angles, required preferred-direction change, search-space size and hand movement all matched between the two types. The failure produces no washout aftereffect (so no partial learning to detect) and the gap is as large in the final sessions as in the first, across ~2 years of alternating exposure: the animals never learned *how* to learn off-manifold (Sadtler et al. 2014) | **Only slow — the manifold is itself learnable, just not in hours.** The same authors' posit: within-manifold learning harnesses fast adaptation, outside-manifold learning engages skill-learning machinery and should benefit from multi-day exposure, with the intrinsic manifold expanding or reorienting. The prior BCI literature they cite reports learning that is "remarkably extensive", and nothing in the study exposed an animal to one outside-manifold mapping for more than a session — alternating types daily is precisely the schedule that would prevent manifold change from accumulating | Whether a model must be *endowed* with a latent basis spanning the target domain or can grow one. Under A, architecture selection is the dominant term and pretraining chooses the reachable set; under B, basis growth is a second, slower learning process that no wiki architecture implements and that would need its own objective ([[wiki/concepts/manifold-constrained-learning.md]], [[wiki/architectural-gaps.md]] G47) | `LIVE` |
| T78 | **Is failure to scale evidence against a learning rule's biological plausibility?** | **Yes — behavioural realism is a plausibility criterion.** Humans learn complex tasks unrelated to their evolution, so the brain has a powerful general-purpose learning algorithm; a candidate rule that cannot reach human-level competence on hard tasks is thereby disqualified, and demonstrating good performance at least establishes that *the rule is not the limiting factor*. Adding neuromodulation or evolutionary priors to a model whose learning rule is crippled teaches nothing about the brain (Bartunov et al. 2018, stating the criterion while producing the negative result it condemns) | **No — machine-learning benchmark performance is a red herring.** A rule stripped of the varieties of plasticity, neuromodulation, evolutionary priors and architecture the brain actually has may fail for any of those reasons rather than because the rule is wrong; poor ImageNet performance is then a statement about the *missing phenomena*, not about the algorithm. The same paper concedes this reading, and the confounds are real: hyperparameters, initialization and architecture have had a decade of tuning for backpropagation and none for target propagation | Whether the wiki should read the ImageNet result on [[wiki/concepts/biologically-plausible-credit-assignment.md]] as *falsifying* the target-propagation family or merely as *not yet supporting* it. Under A the programme should be abandoned or redirected to the jobs backpropagation cannot do (T7); under B the negative result is a call for the tuning effort backpropagation already received. It also sets what a plausibility argument must contain: physiological realism alone, or physiological plus behavioural | `LIVE` — the discriminating experiment is stated but unrun: give one biologically motivated rule the same architecture-and-hyperparameter search effort convolutional backpropagation received, and see whether the gap is search or substance |
| T79 | **Where does fast sensorimotor learning physically happen — in the circuit that carries the manifold, or upstream of it?** | **Upstream: the inputs change, the local network does not.** Within-session BCI learning in M1 preserves the population activity repertoire exactly (repertoire change ≈ 0, `p = 0.55`, `n` = 384; no covariability change along either mapping) while re-associating intents with existing patterns. Connectivity change *within* M1 would be expected to move the repertoire, so the authors read the locus as a change in what M1 receives for a given sensory input — cortical or subcortical, undetermined (Golub et al. 2018) | **Locally: fast learning is synaptic change in the recording area.** The default reading of every fast plasticity rule in the wiki — spike-timing-dependent, behavioural-timescale, recall-gated — is that the area whose activity changes is the area whose weights change, and the wiki's fast/slow architecture places the fast store *in* the circuit that does the task ([[wiki/concepts/synaptic-plasticity.md]], [[wiki/concepts/complementary-learning-systems.md]]). Repertoire preservation is an inference about connectivity from activity, not a measurement of connectivity; a sufficiently low-rank or readout-restricted local change could also leave the repertoire intact | Decides where to put the fast learner in an architecture. Under A, fast adaptation is a **routing** module upstream of a frozen representational circuit — the retrieval/key-rebinding shape — and the representational network needs no fast plasticity at all. Under B, the same network holds both timescales and needs a mechanism to keep fast change from corrupting slow structure. The experiment that separates them (record the presumptive M1 input areas during the same perturbation) is unrun ([[wiki/concepts/manifold-constrained-learning.md]]) | `LIVE` |
| T80 | **Can a population run a stored sequence backwards?** | **Yes — reverse traversal is a normal mode.** Hippocampal place-cell sequences replay both forward and reverse after experience, CA3's symmetric spike-timing kernel lets one weight matrix generate both directions, and the forward:reverse mixture is used as a knob that sets whether the consolidated map stores the policy or the geometry ([[wiki/concepts/offline-replay.md]]) | **No — the flow is directional and volition cannot flip it.** Macaques driving a BCI cursor from ~90 M1 units fail, across sessions and under a corridor constraint that makes it the only rewarded solution, to emit an existing neural trajectory in time-reversed order; the two directions of cursor movement turn out to use *distinct* neural trajectories rather than one path traversed both ways (`raw/talk-nd-brain-learning-limits.txt`, **(tentative)** — talk only, no paper read) | Decides whether a model may treat a stored sequence as reversible for free. If B generalises, an asymmetric-recurrence sequence generator cannot serve backward credit propagation, and reverse traversal needs its own machinery — which is exactly what [[wiki/concepts/offline-replay.md]] uses reverse replay *for* | `LIVE` — the two are not yet a contradiction: different areas (hippocampus vs M1), different regimes (spontaneous offline generation vs online volitional control), different timescales (compressed ripple vs behavioural). The discriminating test is whether *hippocampal* reverse replay is achievable under volitional BCI control, or whether M1 shows spontaneous reverse sequences offline — neither is run |
| T81 | **What determines whether a memory consolidates — that it *recurs*, or that the slow learner can *model* it?** | **Recurrence.** Reliable and unreliable synaptic updates are indistinguishable at one synapse but separable across a population, so the optimal filter gates the long-term write on the fast store's own recall of the proposed update, `Δw_LTM ∝ g(w_STM · w*)` — one broadcast scalar per event (prediction accuracy, reward × confidence, or familiarity), no offline pass, and a proven scaling advantage over any synapse-local mechanism under bursty reinforcement (Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar 2024; [[wiki/concepts/recall-gated-consolidation.md]]) | **Predictability.** Consolidation is transport of gradient signal into a slow learner, so its value is the *generalization* it buys: the optimum is monotone only at infinite SNR, interior at intermediate SNR, and zero at low SNR, where continued transport drives generalization below chance. The criterion is whether the relation is modellable *by this student* — which noise, architecture mismatch, unobserved inputs and the student's own attention all reduce interchangeably (Sun et al. 2023; [[wiki/concepts/generalization-optimized-consolidation.md]]) | The two criteria come apart on a specific, buildable case: a **reliably recurring but unmodellable** relation — a nonlinear regularity a linear learner meets daily — passes A's gate at full strength and is refused by B. Sun et al. state the disagreement from their side as a prediction: if the brain uses frequency as its heuristic for predictability, frequent misinformation consolidates over rare accurate testimony, which is precisely the failure mode a recurrence gate has by construction. A machine test is cheap — stream high-frequency/low-reliability against low-frequency/high-reliability relations and ask which is written to slow **W**. They also differ on *what* is regulated: A gates the write event-by-event and needs no offline period, B rations the *number of reactivations* per relation and is naturally offline | `LIVE`. Not symmetric in scope: A derives its criterion from the statistics of experience with fixed random representations and is agnostic to the learner's capacity; B derives its criterion from the learner's approximation error and is agnostic to how experience arrives. **They are plausibly composable rather than rival** — recurrence as the cheap online prefilter on which updates enter the replay pool, predictability as the stopping rule on how long the pool is replayed **(brainstorm)** — but nobody has run both in one model, and neither supplies the other's missing half |
| T82 | **Is permanent hippocampal dependence an optimal refusal or a transport failure?** | **Refusal.** Consolidation is regulated by predictability: for a relation the slow learner cannot model, the optimal transfer is zero, so the memory stays in the fast store forever and the absence of cortical involvement is the *correct* outcome of a working system (Sun et al. 2023, [[wiki/concepts/generalization-optimized-consolidation.md]]) | **Failure.** The same observable — persistent hippocampal activation at delayed retrieval plus weak hippocampal–prefrontal coupling — is produced by a degraded channel: in older adults it tracks reduced prefrontal slow-wave activity, itself mediated by medial prefrontal grey-matter atrophy, with hippocampal volume and every non-SWA sleep variable predicting nothing (Mander et al. 2013, [[wiki/concepts/complementary-learning-systems.md]]) | Whether the wiki's central consolidation datum — that many memories never leave the fast store — is evidence *for* a regulator or evidence of a lossy pipe. It also decides what a machine system should do when it observes incomplete transfer: under A, nothing (the stopping rule worked); under B, repair the channel. Since predictability is not measurable in any published paradigm, the two are currently confounded in the retrograde-amnesia and ageing literatures both theories postdict | `LIVE` — separable in principle by manipulating the channel while holding the material fixed: slow-oscillation stimulation should rescue retention for high-predictability material only if B is the operative cause, and should leave low-predictability material untouched under A. Not run. Note the two are not exclusive — a rationed channel and a throttled one compose, and B's mechanism sets the *ceiling* on the budget A rations |
| T83 | **Is memory allocation decided by the excitability of neurons just before learning?** | **Yes — allocation is a local winner-take-all over a decaying excitability tag.** CREB-dependent excitability set by a recent learning event is *sufficient* (KCNQ2/3 dominant-negative → preferential recruitment; Kir2.1 → ~5× less likely to be in the trace; a step-function opsin raising excitability just before conditioning determines which amygdala neurons store the association) and *necessary* (silencing the tagged cells impairs recall). The prior is temporal proximity and nothing else; the rule needs no comparison, no retrieval and no similarity metric (Lisman et al. 2018, [[wiki/concepts/memory-allocation-excitability.md]]) | **Only inside a window, and it is overruled from cortex outside it.** Inhibiting vmPFC→MEC projections during encoding of a second context 7 d after the first *demotes* the neurons that were most active in the home cage immediately before learning (24% → 18% of the top-10% ensemble) and promotes neurons tagged 7 days earlier, whose CREB tag has long since decayed (15% → 27%) — the opposite of what a pre-learning-excitability competition predicts. Activating the same projection forces separation even at the 5 h interval where local integration normally wins (de Sousa et al. 2026) | Whether an allocator is a local, content-blind, load-independent competition or a two-term rule with a top-down bias. Under A a machine store allocates by an eligibility vector alone; under B it needs a cross-module path from the slow learner to the fast store's allocator, computed from similarity between the current episode and consolidated content — which no memory-augmented architecture in the wiki has, and which is only available once the slow store has something to compare against (G14, G38). Caveats on B: excitability was never measured directly (activity is the proxy), inhibition and activation are not clean opposites of one variable (both reduce the home-cage fraction), and the effect is absent at 5 h and absent when the two episodes share no features | `LEANING` — the two are compatible as a *partition of the time axis* (blind-and-cheap inside the tag window, informed-and-cortical outside it), which is the reading both sources' data support; what is untested is whether the top-down term also operates within the window and is simply not needed there |
| T84 | **Should the fraction of units written per memory be fixed, or should it track load and statistics?** | **Fixed set-point.** Within a region the engram fraction is conserved across memories — 10–20% in lateral amygdala, 2–6% in dentate gyrus — and neither raising shock intensity nor switching valence from fear to reward changes it; the budget is defended by feedback inhibition, since blocking interneurons enlarges the trace (`raw/talk-nd-memory-engram.txt`, **(tentative)** — talk only, no paper read) | **Load- and statistics-dependent.** Every capacity-derived optimum in the wiki makes the coding level a function of something that changes: `p = (2MT)^(−1/3)`, which *falls as the store fills* ([[wiki/entities/sparse-distributed-memory.md]]); `f* ∝ λ`, tracking the recurrence rate of the memory being written ([[wiki/concepts/recall-gated-consolidation.md]]); `a` as a per-context control variable whose optimum depends on the number of contexts ([[wiki/entities/context-modular-memory-network.md]]) | Decides whether a machine store needs an adaptive sparsity controller at all, or whether one inhibitory gain per store — set once, never scheduled — is the right design.
