It also decides how to read overlap: with `k` fixed, every edge written by co-allocation is capacity spent, so a constant budget is what bounds runaway linking ([[wiki/concepts/engram.md]]) | `LIVE` — the two may not be measuring the same thing: the theory optima are asymptotic (best `a` for a *store* of given size and load), the biological constant is per-*episode* and measured over a handful of memories, and no engram study varies the number of stored memories, which is the only variable the theory says should move it |
| T85 | **Is phasic dopamine a reward *prediction* or a reward *association*?** | **Prediction (temporal difference).** Phasic midbrain dopamine matches a reward prediction error qualitatively (Schultz, Dayan & Montague 1997) and quantitatively (Bayer & Glimcher 2005), with the cue-onset burst produced by chaining value estimates backwards across adjacent time steps ([[wiki/concepts/amortized-inference.md]]) | **Association (PVLV).** TD's chaining requires an unbroken sequence of predictable states; where the stimulus stream is near-unpredictable (the 1-2-AX task) the chain breaks and TD-based gating models degrade. PVLV reproduces the same firing record with two Rescorla–Wagner systems using only within-step terms: PV cancels the burst at expected reward, LV is *trained only when reward is present or expected* and is therefore free to fire at cue onset without ever being punished for it — so the anticipatory burst signals a learned association, not a prediction (O'Reilly & Frank 2006, [[wiki/entities/pbwm.md]]) | Any model that uses dopamine to train an internal control policy (what to store, what to attend, when to plan): TD is fine on tasks with predictable state sequences and fails exactly where the reasoning tasks live. The two accounts are behaviourally close and differ in the *unpredictable-sequence* regime, which is where the discriminating experiment is | `LIVE` |
| T86 | **Is a working memory held in persistent spiking, or in synaptic state that spiking only reads?** | **Persistent activity.** Sustained delay-period spiking is the classic prefrontal correlate, and a recurrent network settling into one attractor per item reproduces it, recovers from distractors by basin recapture, and is what every attractor account of maintenance in the wiki assumes ([[wiki/concepts/attractor-dynamics.md]], [[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]]) | **Synaptic state.** In macaque dorsolateral/ventrolateral prefrontal cortex during distracted delayed-match-to-sample, sample identity is at chance in the population after ~1 s of a 4 s delay while the animal performs at ~99% — and the activity has *not* returned to baseline, it has returned to a state carrying no sample information. Trained networks with short-term plasticity reproduce exactly that profile (spike decoding falls, synaptic-weight decoding stays high for the whole delay) and are more brain-like than fixed-synapse networks across the whole hyperparameter sweep (Kozachkov et al. 2022, [[wiki/entities/stsp-working-memory-rnn.md]]; the hippocampal existence proof is [[wiki/entities/stp-flickering-cann.md]]) | Decides what fast **M** is made of, and what it costs: under A maintenance is continuous spiking and the item is always readable; under B maintenance is free, the item is invisible until probed, and "hold" and "use" are separate operations with separate energy budgets ([[wiki/concepts/working-memory.md]]). It also decides which failure mode to design against — basin escape versus decay of `τ_f` | `LEANING B` for delayed-match-to-sample, **with an explicit task confound the paper names**: spatial delayed-response tasks report much higher delay spiking, plausibly because they require withholding a *known* motor response rather than holding information for later computation. So the two positions may be reporting two different tasks, and the general claim rests on one animal. Note the positions are not exclusive — Masse et al. and Lundqvist et al. 2018 report spiking rising when a memory is manipulated, which is A's mechanism operating as B's read-out — and Lundqvist et al. add that even the *expression* is discrete, since on single trials the informative spiking occurs inside brief gamma bursts whose timing is only weakly correlated across trials, so the trial-averaged sustained signal is an averaging artefact. Related to T2 (is the fast level a separate store or recurrent activity?), one level down: this row asks the same question inside a single circuit; T87 is its sibling — the other way to deny persistent spiking while keeping the memory in activity |
| T87 | **Is a working memory held at a fixed point, or in a moving trajectory?** | **Fixed point.** Sustained delay-period firing is what a recurrent net trained on a delay task converges to on its own — point or line attractors, one per item — and it is the only design that survives an *unknown* delay length by construction, since a state that does not move needs no read-out clock ([[wiki/concepts/attractor-dynamics.md]], [[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]]) | **Trajectory.** Mouse Neuropixels across >30 regions during olfactory delayed paired association show sequentially peaking, sharply tuned units tiling the delay, which trained vanilla RNNs do not reproduce (transient index far below the animals'). Adding per-unit self-inhibition, sparse recurrence and a sensory/association/motor topology reproduces it — 38.2% vs. 32.6% stimulus-selective units, the same sensory-to-motor selectivity gradient, dPCA trajectories whose velocity does not decay — and the transient arm *beats* the persistent arm at equal parameter count on distractors, 2–6 remembered items and spatial navigation, with higher activity entropy and lower mean squared rate (Liu et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/trnn.md]]) | Decides whether fast **M** should be built to hold a state or to run one: under A the design problem is basin depth and gate timing, under B it is trajectory separation and when to read out. It also decides where capacity comes from — number of distinguishable stable states versus entropy of the activity over the delay | `LEANING B` on the modelling side, with three caveats the paper states: the two arms are separated *post hoc* by a measured index rather than by an imposed mechanism, the water-maze vanilla RNN scores like a memoryless net and may simply have failed to learn, and the capacity argument reuses the entropy term that is already a component of the transience measure. The variable-delay objection to B is answered but not closed (3–6 s, ~4-point accuracy cost). Sits beside T86: the two rows are the two ways of denying persistent spiking — move the code, or hide it in the synapses |
| T88 | **What is prefrontal delay-period activity *about* — the item held, or the place attention is pointed?** | **The item.** Sustained, spatially tuned firing after a cue disappears is the classic memory trace (Fuster & Alexander 1971; Funahashi et al. 1989), inactivating dorsolateral prefrontal cortex produces "mnemonic scotomas" in localized regions of space (Funahashi et al. 1993a), and antisaccade recordings find more than twice as many cells coding the *stimulus* location as the movement target — read as evidence for a sensory memory. Every maintenance design in [[wiki/concepts/working-memory.md]] takes this reading, T86 and T87 argue only about *how* the item is carried | **The pointer.** With the attended and remembered locations dissociated inside one trial, **61%** of spatially tuned dorsolateral prefrontal cells code the attended location and **16%** the remembered one (`I_Att` = 1.84 vs. `I_Rem` = 1.21; 8.8 vs. 5.3 spikes/s preferred-minus-least-preferred), ensemble decoding agrees, tuning to the attended location is *stronger late in the delay* than early with sensory input identical in every coordinate frame, memory tuning is absent at cue onset and develops during the trial, and both locations are equally reward-predictive until the trigger so reward anticipation cannot explain the asymmetry. A's two pillars are reinterpreted: a "mnemonic scotoma" is equally a localized failure to attend, and antisaccade/brightness cues confound the remembered location with the attention-attracting one (Lebedev et al. 2004) | Everything the wiki reads off prefrontal delay recordings. T86's "sample decodable across the delay" and T87's transient index are both scored on tasks that left attention uncontrolled, so a delay signal cannot be assumed to be about the stored item. For a builder it splits fast **M** in two: a content register and an attentional pointer register, which no wiki architecture separates (gap G48) | `LEANING B` for *this* task and this region, with three limits the authors state: attention was not independently measured (inferred from task design and a 36 ms reaction-time cost for reoriented saccades); motor preparation to either potential target is not excluded; and the result is regional rather than global — ventrolateral of the principal sulcus the population is 28% attention / 2% memory cells, dorsomedially it is 8% / 9% / 10%, so a recording site chooses the answer. The paper explicitly declines to replace maintenance monolithism with attention monolithism: delay activity also carries rules, categories, task selection and event prediction |
| T89 | **Is a working memory cleared by decay or by an addressed erase signal?** | **Decay.** Every fast store in the wiki forgets by a time constant or by a capacity rule and none has an erase primitive driven by relevance: short-term-plasticity traces lapse at `τ_f`, `τ_r` ([[wiki/entities/stp-flickering-cann.md]], [[wiki/entities/stsp-working-memory-rnn.md]]), a maintained attractor drifts once the adaptation gain exceeds `τ/τ_v` ([[wiki/entities/adaptive-cann.md]]), and the one explicit free list in the wiki reclaims slots by **usage** and read-order bookkeeping rather than by whether the content still matters ([[wiki/entities/differentiable-neural-computer.md]]) | **An addressed signal.** In macaque prefrontal cortex the largest time × frequency effect in the whole dataset is a post-trial elevation of beta bursting *at the informative sites only* — non-informative sites are relatively suppressed — while spiking information about the last object drops sharply; and before a test object that an earlier mismatch has made irrelevant, beta rises and the gamma/information ramp is cancelled (Lundqvist et al. 2018, [[wiki/concepts/working-memory.md]]). The same prospective, relevance-driven deletion appears in single units: whichever of two locations has just become irrelevant decays while the other improves, with the switch *preceding* the saccade that makes it irrelevant (Lebedev et al. 2004) | Whether a machine fast store needs a third primitive alongside read and write, and what its addressing mode is: under A capacity is recovered by waiting or by eviction policy, under B the controller must be able to name a target and cancel it — which is the operation gap G48's prospective clear and gap G42's occupancy accounting both require. It also changes the failure analysis: a decaying store fails by losing what it should have kept, an erasing store can fail by clearing something still needed, and only the second shows up as a *control* error | `LIVE` — the erase evidence is entirely correlational (local-field-potential burst rates, no causal manipulation of beta), and decay is not ruled out by it: post-trial beta could accompany a lapse it does not cause. The discriminating experiment is stimulating or blocking the beta channel and asking whether information persists past its usual clearing point |
| T90 | **Is a working-memory operation implemented by one controller, or by the conjunction of several subsystems each representing it differently?** | **One controller.** The frontoparietal control network is the only one of four cortical communities whose multi-voxel patterns separate all four operations (maintain / replace / suppress / clear), with pairwise classification 0.968–0.999 against 0.53–0.99 elsewhere; on this reading it *issues* the operation and the visual, somatomotor and default-mode patterns are downstream consequences of top-down drive — the machine-natural design, and the one the wiki's control/storage separation already assumes ([[wiki/entities/pbwm.md]], [[wiki/entities/differentiable-neural-computer.md]]) | **The conjunction.** Each community represents the operation set under a *different* quotient — visual cortex carries held vs. not-held, somatomotor cortex singles out `clear`, the default-mode network adds suppress ≠ clear — and these run in parallel, so the operation may be identified by the joint pattern (maintain = its visual + somatomotor + default-mode + frontoparietal signature) rather than by any one of them. The distinctions are not redundant copies: somatomotor cortex separates `clear` from `suppress` (0.762) better than visual cortex does (0.610), so a subsystem the controller would be "driving" carries a distinction its supposed driver's downstream targets do not share (DeRosa et al. 2024) | Whether a machine store needs an operation *code* at one site or an operation *signature* distributed over its subsystems — and therefore where a removal can be verified. Under A, log the controller's emitted opcode; under B, no single site's state says which operation ran, and the wiki's whole practice of probing one layer for the control variable is mis-specified (gap G49, [[wiki/concepts/representation-probing.md]]) | `LIVE` — the authors state both readings and the design cannot decide: fMRI, cued operations, no manipulation, and no trial-level link between a community's pattern and whether the item was in fact removed. The discriminating test they propose is predicting removal success (classifier fit on the item itself, Kim et al. 2020) from each community's pattern separately and jointly |
| T91 | **Is the medial temporal contribution to arbitrary mapping restricted to spatial consequents?** | **Spatial.** The monkey neuropsychology emphasises spatial factors: premotor cortex is required for visuo*motor* but not visuo*visual* mappings, and on the same logic the "hippocampus" is proposed to subserve visuo*spatial* but not visuovisual mappings — a division of labour by consequent type across the network, with only prefrontal cortex impaired for both (Wise & Murray 2000, reviewing the ablation record) | **General.** Human neuroimaging finds hippocampal and parahippocampal activation during *nonmotor* associative learning (Henke et al.), for less-practiced versus well-practiced memories (Petersson et al.), and for novel word pairs (Dolan & Fletcher) — consequents with no spatial or motor content at all. The same studies report prefrontal activation for nonmotor consequents, which *does* agree with the lesion record, so the divergence is specific to the medial temporal claim | Whether a machine fast store should be typed by *what kind of consequent* it binds (one store per output modality) or be a single modality-agnostic binder addressed by the controller — [[wiki/concepts/arbitrary-sensorimotor-mapping.md]], [[wiki/concepts/complementary-learning-systems.md]] | `LIVE` — and partly a methods artefact: lesions test necessity in monkeys, imaging tests engagement in humans, so "activates during word-pair learning" and "not required for visuovisual mapping" can both be true. The decisive experiment — an ablation with a nonmotor, nonspatial consequent — is the systematic antecedent × consequent sweep the review itself says has never been run |
| T92 | **Is working memory the specialized function of prefrontal cortex?** | **Yes — maintenance is what the region is for.** The wiki's control layer is built on it: delay-period firing that outlasts the cue is the canonical prefrontal signal, gated prefrontal maintenance is the biological anchor for LSTM-style gating, and [[wiki/entities/pbwm.md]] identifies the store with prefrontal stripes outright ([[wiki/concepts/working-memory.md]]) | **No — at least not for ventral prefrontal cortex, and not as the *sole* function.** Monkeys with ventral prefrontal removals retain nonspatial visual stimuli for up to 8 s in delayed matching-to-sample (Rushworth et al. 1997a), yet the same region's ablation abolishes arbitrary visuomotor mapping in a task where the instruction cue stays on screen through response selection and execution — near-zero storage demand, near-total deficit. The deficit is also not perseveration: post-lesion the animal repeats a previous incorrect response no more often than it emits any other alternative (Murray et al. 2000) | Whether a machine control layer should model prefrontal cortex as *a store with a gate* or as *a binder that computes cue × action × outcome conjunctions and happens to hold them* — the second predicts that removing the store's maintenance capacity leaves the binding function intact, which is the opposite of PBWM's ablation profile | `LIVE` — partly a scope dispute: the maintenance evidence is overwhelmingly dorsolateral and spatial, the falsifying evidence ventral and nonspatial, so "prefrontal cortex" may simply be too coarse a unit for either claim. It compounds with T88, which already says most of the dorsolateral delay signal is an attentional pointer rather than an item |
| T93 | **Does abstracting a rule enable generalization, or only speed it up?** | **Enabler.** Monkeys given two abstract rules (match / non-match) cued across modalities performed above chance on pictures they were **seeing for the first time**, which per-exemplar learning cannot produce: the alternative solution is 16 paired associates that say nothing about a new picture, so each new stimulus set would cost a fresh set of errors. Rule coding was correspondingly the most prevalent signal across prefrontal cortex, and the efficiency argument is representational — one abstract rule population instead of one population per cue modality (Wallis et al. 2001, in Miller et al. 2002; [[wiki/concepts/cognitive-control.md]]) | **Rate multiplier.** Higher-order rules are explicitly stated *not* to be necessary for acquiring the lower-order mappings under them — each exemplar can be learned one at a time without ever encoding the general rule — and the one place the strategy's value is quantified it is worth an exact 33 percentage points of initial-choice error, a better starting point and not learning: after PFv+o removal, reinforced mappings were not acquired at all across a 50-trial block despite the strategy loss accounting for only part of the error (Wise & Murray 2000; Murray et al. 2000; [[wiki/concepts/arbitrary-sensorimotor-mapping.md]]) | Whether an architecture should expect a rule layer to change the sample-complexity *class* on a new instance set or only its constant. Under A a reasoning model must contain a rule level to transfer at all; under B a rule level is an optimisation over an instance learner that must exist anyway, and building the rule level first is building the wrong thing (gap G8) | `LIVE` — and possibly not a disagreement about mechanism but about **edge type**: Miller's rules quantify over a *relation* (same / different) that is fully defined on stimuli never seen, whereas Murray & Wise's mappings are arbitrary edges with no shared content to factor out, where by construction no abstraction can predict a new edge. If that reading is right the discriminating variable is whether the target edge is computable from the rule, and the two results are the two ends of one axis rather than rival claims |
| T94 | **When a behaviour becomes automatic, does the controller drop out or hand over?** | **Drops out.** Repeated biasing strengthens the direct sensory→motor pathway until it no longer needs prefrontal cortex; automaticity is the same control mechanism run to convergence, and it *releases* controller capacity (Miller et al. 2002, [[wiki/concepts/cognitive-control.md]]) | **Hands over to another prefrontal region.** Prelimbic cortex mediates voluntary, goal-directed initial responding; the incremental effect of overtraining that makes behaviour autonomous is mediated by **infralimbic** cortex — an adjacent prefrontal subregion with its own limbic and autonomic output ports, not a subcortical bypass (Heidbreder & Groenewegen 2003, [[wiki/entities/medial-prefrontal-cortex.md]]) — **Third position — it neither drops out nor moves subcortically; it moves *within* prefrontal cortex.** Novel tasks activate anterior prefrontal cortex while overlearned tasks activate medial and slightly more posterior prefrontal regions (Koechlin et al. 2000), and prefrontal neurons respond to *known* actions, not only new ones — so the reviewers reject both the guided-activation prediction that the controller approaches irrelevance for frequent rules and Fuster's claim that prefrontal representation is reserved for non-habitual actions. No theory in the table specifies the transfer step either way (Wood & Grafman 2003, [[wiki/concepts/controller-knowledge-vs-process.md]], gap G51) | Cost accounting for skill acquisition ([[wiki/concepts/skill-acquisition-efficiency.md]]): under A a mastered skill frees the control layer, under B it occupies a different part of it, and the two predict opposite things about how many automatic behaviours can run at once. Also decides what a controller lesion does to an overtrained policy — A predicts it survives, B predicts it survives only if the lesion spares the receiving subregion. Not a clean contradiction: A is primate lateral prefrontal cortex with a rule-learning task, B is rat medial wall with instrumental overtraining, and neither measured the other's variable | `LIVE` |
| T95 | **Does the control layer store knowledge of its own, or only run procedures over knowledge stored elsewhere?** | **Representational.** Prefrontal cortex stores memories — goal-oriented event sequences with categories, social rules, event boundaries and grammars — localized by which posterior region supplies their content; lateral prefrontal cortex is argued to derive from motor areas that *store motor programs*, so a gradual evolutionary path to a contentless module does not exist, and a 275-study imaging survey finds consistent regional differentiation between attention, episodic memory, working memory, language and semantic memory (Wood & Grafman 2003, [[wiki/concepts/controller-knowledge-vs-process.md]]) | **Processing.** The controller is a general algorithm independent of the modality of what it processes: adaptive coding makes prefrontal neurons a temporary, task-specific operating space with little regional specialization, and the supervisory attentional system is a set of priority-setting mechanisms with no knowledge base. Every recording of highly flexible prefrontal tuning across tasks and stimulus domains is evidence for this side | Whether a machine control module is trained (a schema/plan library with its own parameters, written by the outer loop) or wired (contentless gating over a frozen backbone) — and what a controller ablation should destroy: specific classes of plan, or control in general with contents intact | `LIVE` |
| T96 | **Is the controller one homogeneous pool or partitioned by content?** | **Homogeneous.** Prefrontal neurons adaptively code whatever the current task requires, so the same cells serve attention, working memory and control, and functional differences between orbitofrontal and dorsolateral cortex reflect only different balances of response strength rather than category-distinct modules (Duncan's adaptive coding; Miller & Cohen's "no modular discretely localized forms") | **Partitioned by content class.** 275 imaging studies show consistent localization differences between functions; social behaviour impairment follows ventromedial damage while mechanistic/reflective impairment follows dorsolateral damage; dissociable networks for emotional vs. non-emotional and social vs. non-social content; emotion-specific neurons in human ventral prefrontal cortex — with the partition predicted to follow *connectivity* to the posterior or subcortical region supplying the content (Wood & Grafman 2003, [[wiki/concepts/controller-knowledge-vs-process.md]]; anatomically echoed by the dorsal/ventral output-port split in [[wiki/entities/medial-prefrontal-cortex.md]]) | Whether one control module suffices or the control layer must be factorized — and if factorized, whether the factorization is chosen by the designer or falls out of which downstream systems each part is wired to | `BOTH, AT DIFFERENT THRESHOLDS` — the first quantitative arbitration makes the dispute one of degree. Running five content domains head-to-head across the human medial wall, **17%** of voxels prefer one domain over all four rivals at the strong criterion (posterior ≥ 0.60) and **88%** do at the weak one (> 0.50, chance 6%), with one subdivision 99% covered by weak wins and another only 75% (Lieberman & Meyer 2018, [[wiki/concepts/representation-probing.md]]). So B is right about the gradients and A is right about the substrate: mostly shared tissue carrying domain-preferring gradients, plus one region shared by construction. It also relocates B's own social pole from ventromedial to *dorso*medial, so the partition is real while the framework's specific assignments are not ([[wiki/concepts/controller-knowledge-vs-process.md]]) . **A third partition, cut on a different axis, is not in the dispute and settles part of it** (Friedman & Robbins 2021, [[wiki/concepts/control-unity-and-diversity.md]]): whatever is true of content, the controller is demonstrably factorized by *operation* — a common component plus updating- and shifting-specific components with separate genetic influences, separate receptor dependences (D1 working memory vs. D2 flexibility) and double-dissociable lesion sites (orbitofrontal reversal vs. ventrolateral BA-12/47 set-shifting; BA-8 conditional selection vs. BA-9/46 monitoring). Pure adaptive coding cannot produce components that a single drug moves in *opposite* directions |
| T97 | **Does the cortical controller hold a memory from the start, or only after consolidation?** | **Only remote.** Medial prefrontal cortex is selectively engaged by memories weeks old and dispensable for the same task 1–2 days after learning: metabolic imaging at 5 vs. 25 days, greater remote dendritic spine growth, and lesion/inactivation deficits confined to remote recall across radial-arm maze, water maze, contextual fear, conditioned taste aversion, trace eyeblink and cocaine reinstatement — with the hippocampus showing the complementary profile (reviewed in Euston et al. 2012) | **Both, with remote worse.** The same region is needed for recent navigational, object-place and fear memory learned 1–2 days earlier, and for the *consolidation* of tasks whose acquisition it is not needed for; the authors' reading is that at recent delays the controller represents the terms while the hippocampus holds the mapping, so its loss is compensable, and at remote delays it holds both (Euston et al. 2012, [[wiki/entities/medial-prefrontal-cortex.md]]) | Whether the slow learner is written to *during* experience or only by the offline channel. Under A a machine controller can be trained purely from replay after the fact; under B it is co-active at encoding and the replay channel only completes an association it has already started, which also predicts different lesion timings. The confound neither side controls is compensation: a region silent at learning may have its function reallocated, so a null recent-memory result cannot distinguish "not involved" from "involved and substitutable" | `LEANING` (toward B, but the discriminating prediction — that experiments showing a selective remote role also show a weak recent one on closer examination — is the reviewers' own and is untested) |
| T98 | **Does the episodic store address the controller by delivering content, or by setting its inhibitory gain?** | **Content.** The wiki's account of the hippocampus→prefrontal channel is information transfer: ventral/intermediate hippocampal input supplies *context* — which room, which task phase, which rule — and prefrontal cells phase-lock to hippocampal theta with coherence rising at choice points and after rule acquisition, i.e. the channel is read as a data path whose traffic increases when more context is needed (Euston et al. 2012, [[wiki/entities/medial-prefrontal-cortex.md]]). | **Gain.** The measured synaptic effect is short-latency AMPA excitation of prelimbic pyramidal cells **followed by inhibition**, because the same terminals drive GABAergic interneurons. Inactivating ventral hippocampus *lowers* prelimbic interneuron firing and *raises* pyramidal firing, and in extinguished rats raises freezing — so the channel's normal action is to withhold prefrontal output the context does not license, and removing it disinhibits rather than starves the controller (Spedding & Jay 2012). | Whether a model should route the retrieved context into the controller as an *input feature* (concatenate, attend) or as a *multiplicative/subtractive gate on the controller's output repertoire*. The two make opposite predictions under ablation: cutting a data path degrades performance toward chance, cutting an inhibitory gate produces context-inappropriate but confidently executed responses — which is what the fear-renewal and extinction-recall disconnection results look like. | `LIVE` |
| T99 | **What indexes which module a memory needs — its age, or its overlap with what is already stored?** | **Age.** The recent/remote framing organizes the whole consolidation literature: the hippocampus holds the mapping at 1–2 days and medial prefrontal cortex holds it at ~30 days (shown to 200), with selective remote-memory lesion deficits and greater remote dendritic spine growth, and a bounded 0–2 h post-task window in which cortical disruption destroys the later memory (Euston et al. 2012, [[wiki/entities/medial-prefrontal-cortex.md]], T97) | **Integration demand.** Prefrontal involvement tracks how much the new event conflicts with existing associations, not how old it is: in associative-inference, transitive-inference and food-location-schema tasks mPFC damage impairs *acquisition* and blocks inference from the outset, while in single-shock contextual fear — where the animal has no prior model of the box — it is dispensable early and needed only later. Hippocampal duration is set by a second variable, how much episodic detail the read-out requires (flat retrograde gradient for autobiographical memory, consolidation-limited for semanticized memory). Under this reading the recent/remote pattern is what low-integration-demand paradigms produce (Preston & Eichenbaum 2013, [[wiki/concepts/schema-assimilation.md]]) | Whether a machine consolidation schedule is a clock or a signal. Under A, replay after `t` steps with a decay — the current default everywhere in the wiki. Under B the schedule is a function of two online quantities: disagreement between the fast store's retrieval for the current cue and the new observation, and the detail the task's read-out demands. B also explains away A's own worst inconsistency (the same delays giving opposite results across paradigms) as an uncontrolled third variable, which is a stronger position than a tie | `LEANING` (B: it accounts for A's evidence as a special case, and A has no account of the schema-task results. But the trigger is defined by experimental design and never measured within a trial, so B is currently a reframing without an instrument) |
| T100 | **At which task phase does the hippocampal→prefrontal channel matter — encoding or retrieval?** | **Encoding.** Optogenetic inhibition of ventral-hippocampal *terminals* in medial prefrontal cortex impairs a four-goal T-maze specifically during cue encoding, and pathway gamma (30–70 Hz) tracks successful encoding and correct trials, vanishing under terminal inhibition (Spellman et al. 2015, in Jin & Maren 2015, [[wiki/entities/medial-prefrontal-cortex.md]]) | **Retrieval.** The best-controlled rodent lesion result puts medial prefrontal necessity at retrieval only: inactivation before training, or during the delay, has no effect on spatial win-shift; inactivation before the test does (Euston et al. 2012) | Whether a store's *input link* and the store itself can be assigned different task phases. If both hold, ablating a module and ablating its input give opposite phase signatures, and every lesion design that hits the region is blind to the difference — as is every machine memory evaluation, none of which ablates a connection separately from its endpoints ([[wiki/concepts/working-memory.md]], gap G52) | `LIKELY BOTH` — different manipulations (terminal inhibition vs. regional inactivation), different tasks, and a reading that reconciles them: the channel writes the trial's context→cue mapping into the controller at encoding, and the controller is queried later. Untested, because no experiment runs both manipulations in one task |
| T101 | **Can the control layer address the episodic store directly, or only through a relay?** | **Only through a relay.** The direct hippocampus→medial-prefrontal projection is monosynaptic and essentially unreciprocated, so the return route must be nucleus reuniens or entorhinal cortex — the premise of [[wiki/entities/nucleus-reuniens.md]] and of this wiki's whole controller→store story (Heidbreder & Groenewegen 2003; Jin & Maren 2015) | **Directly.** A monosynaptic anterior-cingulate → dorsal CA1/CA3 projection exists in mouse and is optogenetically necessary *and sufficient* for spatial-memory retrieval, preferentially innervating learning-created hub neurons (Rajasethupathy et al. 2015, in Sigurdsson & Duvarci 2016; [[wiki/entities/medial-prefrontal-cortex.md]]) | Whether the controller→store write needs an intermediary module at all. If both hold, the resolution is that the *tiers* differ — the ventral tier receives from the store and reaches it only via relay, the dorsal tier writes to it directly — which makes the controller's read port and write port different modules, an asymmetry no architecture has | LEANING |
| T102 | **When forward and reverse inference disagree about a region's function, which wins?** | **Forward.** Meta-analysis over many studies is the field's — and this wiki's — standard evidence for structure–function claims: a region that reliably activates for a task class is taken to serve it, and accumulated study counts are what make the map trustworthy. Almost every localization claim on the wiki's biological pages descends from this instrument | **Reverse.** Forward inference computes `P(activity \| task)` and the claim needs `P(task \| activity)`; going from the first to the second is affirming the consequent. The concrete disagreement: the most social-selective tissue in human medial prefrontal cortex under head-to-head reverse inference appears in *no* social-cognition meta-analysis and not in the same authors' forward map, because signal dropout leaves that location with ~¼ the study count of its dorsal counterpart — forward inference needs accumulated counts, a rate ratio does not (Lieberman & Meyer 2018, [[wiki/concepts/representation-probing.md]]) | Every regional claim the wiki imports from imaging, and every machine selectivity sweep that ranks units by mean activation under a condition — those are forward-inference instruments and inherit the same failure. If B holds, an under-sampled or rarely-active component is systematically invisible to the standard analysis | `LEANING B` on the logic, which is not in dispute; the empirical caution is that reverse inference here is text-mined keyword tags over reported peaks, so B's instrument is weaker in construct validity exactly where it is stronger in inferential direction |
| T103 | **What is the common component of control?** | **Inhibition.** Once a higher-order factor is fitted over inhibition, updating and shifting, it predicts the inhibition factor almost perfectly, and a bifactor reparameterization yields updating-specific and shifting-specific factors but **no inhibition-specific factor** — the variance shared across stopping tasks *is* the variance shared across all control tasks, and every control operation can be described as suppressing something (an intruding item, a stale item, an abandoned task set) | **Goal maintenance and biasing.** The same statistics follow from a controller whose only primitive is an actively held goal that biases processing: inhibition tasks load hardest because a lapsed goal lets the prepotent response win by default, so *individual differences* in stopping are differences in goal activation rather than in a stop process. The inhibition reading also assumes that processes sharing the word share a mechanism, which is false — the several "inhibitions" dissociate and some are not neural inhibition at all (Friedman & Robbins 2021, [[wiki/concepts/control-unity-and-diversity.md]]) | Decides whether a machine controller needs a dedicated suppression channel with its own parameters ([[wiki/concepts/inhibitory-control-of-coding.md]]) or gets suppression for free from bias into a competitive substrate ([[wiki/concepts/cognitive-control.md]]) — and the two designs predict different factor structures over a population of trained instances | `LEANING B` on parsimony, not on data: B derives A's statistics while A cannot derive B's, and B is what the wiki's own mechanism page already implements. The discriminating measurement — whether inhibition-specific machinery (global motor suppression via the right inferior frontal gyrus→subthalamic pathway) carries low between-subject variance — is assumed by B and has never been made. A third possibility neither side states: stopping is a *hub with a fixed spoke*, so the shared hub varies and the spoke does not |
| T104 | **Is the working-memory capacity limit a resource ceiling or a safety margin the system imposes on itself?** | **A ceiling.** Every account in the wiki locates the bound in something scarce: distinguishable noisy transitions in an attractor chain give `7 ± 2` ([[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]]), superposition interference among concurrent control states degrades both ([[wiki/concepts/cognitive-control.md]]), and softmax competition at the read spreads one unit of mass over every candidate ([[wiki/concepts/attention.md]]). On all three the store is already doing the best its substrate allows | **A chosen margin.** Recurrent excitation is unsafe, so prefrontal microcircuits run a Ca²⁺→K⁺ shunt whose *purpose* is to prevent runaway activity, and the store therefore operates below its achievable capacity: blocking the SK channel (apamin) or IP₃-mediated Ca²⁺ release (xestospongin C) in rat prefrontal cortex **improves** working memory, while removing the same brakes genetically lowers cortical seizure threshold and, for KCNQ2/3, causes childhood epilepsy. The ~10–30 s point at which hippocampal connections become necessary is offered as the same feedback's doing (Arnsten et al. 2010, [[wiki/concepts/dynamic-network-connectivity.md]]) | Whether a machine store's capacity is a fact about its design or a hyperparameter: if B holds, every recurrent store with a stability regulariser is running an unmeasured margin, and the sweep that measures it (lower the regulariser until instability, record capacity) has never been run. It also decides whether "enlarge the store" or "loosen the brake" is the right intervention (gap G42) | `LIVE` |
| T105 | **Does model-based *behaviour* license the inference that a model-based *algorithm* is running?** | **Yes — that is the point of the protocol.** The two-step task was built to separate the two: after an uncommon-transition reward, a model-free learner repeats the first-stage action and a model-based learner switches, so the stay pattern reads out which algorithm produced the choice; humans and rats show the model-based pattern, and model-based reward-prediction errors appear in ventral striatum (Daw et al. 2011; Miller et al. 2017, via [[wiki/concepts/simulation-based-planning.md]]) | **No.** A recurrent network trained *only* by a model-free policy-gradient algorithm, tested with weights frozen, reproduces the model-based stay pattern, the multi-lag regression profile, and reward-prediction errors regressing on a model-based algorithm at `r² = 0.89` against `5.8 × 10⁻⁷` for model-free. Nothing in the trained system simulates a transition model; the behaviour is a property of the emergent inner algorithm, and the outer algorithm that installed it is model-free by construction (Wang et al. 2018, [[wiki/entities/meta-rl-agent.md]]) | Decides what the wiki's cleanest evaluation protocol certifies. Under A the two-step task and the re-goaling test (gap G17) probe mechanism; under B they probe only the *input–output profile of the inner loop*, and an architecture claim needs a second instrument — intervening on the rollout (silencing the simulator, as in the Barron et al. 2020 optogenetic test) rather than scoring the choices. B also weakens the model-free/model-based dichotomy itself: the same recurrent substrate is model-free at the training timescale and model-based at the episode timescale | `LIVE` — B is a constructive counterexample and stands as such; what is unsettled is whether biological model-based control is of this emergent kind or of the simulating kind, which the two instruments together can distinguish |
| T106 | **Do task-trained prefrontal-like recurrent networks settle into attractors, or avoid them?** | **They avoid them, and the avoidance is what makes them cortex-like.** Trained with adaptation currents, sparse recurrence and block topology, a delay network suppresses fixed points entirely and carries the memory in a transient trajectory, beating its persistent-activity twin on distractors, item load and navigation ([[wiki/entities/trnn.md]], Liu et al. 2025); and fixed-weight networks that *do* converge on point/line attractors are more fragile to synapse ablation and *less* like recorded prefrontal populations than short-term-plasticity networks with no persistent delay activity ([[wiki/entities/stsp-working-memory-rnn.md]], Kozachkov et al. 2022) | **They settle into attractors, and here the attractor is the computation.** RNNs meta-trained on planning with within-trial reward change converge on a spacetime attractor: perturbation of the settled state decays if weak, flips the representation to an entire alternative path if strong, and does nothing in random directions; networks too small to build one fail the task rather than finding another solution ([[wiki/entities/spacetime-attractor.md]], Jensen et al. 2026) | Whether relaxation is the wiki's default prefrontal mechanism or an artefact of easy delay tasks. The candidate reconciliation is that the two literatures train on different objectives — *hold an item* (where a moving trajectory is cheaper and more robust) versus *satisfy a set of constraints over a future trajectory* (where a fixed point is what "consistent plan" means) — which predicts that the same network should abandon attractors when the delay task is made purely mnemonic, and is untested | `LIVE` |
| T107 | **Which structure holds the graph that a plan runs on — the hippocampal formation, or the cortical controller?** | **Hippocampal-entorhinal.** The wiki's whole cognitive-map account puts the relational structure in the medial temporal lobe: place and grid codes are the map ([[wiki/concepts/cognitive-map.md]]), the Tolman–Eichenbaum Machine factorises structure from sensory content in entorhinal-like cells ([[wiki/entities/tolman-eichenbaum-machine.md]]), the successor representation is a hippocampal predictive map, and chained retrieval during two-stage inference is abolished by silencing dorsal CA1 (Barron et al. 2020, [[wiki/concepts/simulation-based-planning.md]]). Prefrontal cortex reads the map and valuates | **Prefrontal.** The hippocampal space code is *too redundant and too distributed* to be a compact topological map; it supplies instances (places, routes, episodes) and the cortical network learns the graph over them. Measured along the cascade, population size falls and receptive fields grow from place cells → prefrontal columns, and every unit type the graph-planner needs (state, distance-to-goal, prospective-sequence, strategy-switch) is present in navigating-rat medial prefrontal recordings and recoverable by blind clustering; the hippocampus is modelled as pure localisation input (Martinet et al. 2011, [[wiki/entities/pfc-columnar-planning-model.md]]) | Where a builder puts the adjacency matrix, and what the fast episodic store is *for*. Under A the fast store holds the graph and the controller queries it, so consolidation moves a graph. Under B the fast store holds instances and the controller *abstracts* a graph from them, so consolidation is a change of representational format and the two structures should carry measurably different graphs — the coarse one only in cortex. The two are not fully exclusive (both may hold a graph at different resolutions), and that reconciliation is exactly what the coarse/fine column split predicts: multi-scale codes exist in entorhinal cortex too (Hafting et al. 2005; Kjelstrup et al. 2008), so the open question is whether cortical chunking is redundant with hippocampal scale, or orthogonal to it (chunking by *action structure* rather than by distance) | `LIVE` — the prefrontal-graph model's hippocampal component is a deliberate stub (no remapping, no forward sweeps), so it cannot be evidence *against* a hippocampal graph; it establishes only that a cortical graph is sufficient for detour behaviour and consistent with prefrontal single-unit data |
| T108 | **When a new situation is ambiguous between stored structures, does the learner mix them or commit to one?** | **Mix.** Expression is a probability-weighted mixture over *all* memories and updating is responsibility-scaled across all of them, with both evidence channels significant and additive and no interaction (cue `p = 3.8×10⁻³`, feedback `p = 1.26×10⁻⁴`) — offered as direct evidence against winner-take-all retrieval (Heald et al. 2021, [[wiki/concepts/contextual-inference.md]]) | **Commit.** C-TS collapses the posterior onto its mode each trial, assigns the trial definitively to one task-set, updates only that one, and keeps no history of alternative assignments; the corticostriatal network is stricter still, since only the *gated* prefrontal stripe can learn at all. Restricting C-TS to the network's regime cost little and fit humans about as well (Collins & Frank 2013, [[wiki/entities/c-ts-model.md]]) | Whether a machine retrieval layer is a soft router or a hard one — and therefore whether an ambiguous new situation degrades gracefully or produces a systematic wrong-rule error. The two make opposite predictions about partial matches: a mixture blends toward the correct policy, an argmax commits to a rule that is rewarded on half the stimuli and is measurably slow to abandon it (negative transfer, `E(N_new)` 0.84 for the overlapping new context against 0.99 for the incongruent one). Confounded by content: COIN's memories hold one scalar, where committing is nearly free, while C-TS's hold a policy whose credit assignment is ambiguous unless a single owner is named | `LIVE` — the two were fit to different domains and neither has been run on the other's task; the discriminating experiment is a partial-match probe, which only the argmax account predicts a signature for |
| T109 | **Is medial premotor cortex (SMA/pre-SMA) part of the arbitrary stimulus→action binding network?** | **No.** Monkey ablation of medial premotor cortex — supplementary motor plus anterior cingulate motor areas — leaves arbitrary visuomotor mapping essentially intact; the region sits in the review's explicit *specificity* list, the one that makes the positive network a network rather than a list of everything (Wise & Murray 2000, [[wiki/concepts/arbitrary-sensorimotor-mapping.md]]) | **Yes, and centrally.** Human fMRI of higher-order categorical stimulus→response learning finds midline SMA/pre-SMA in every subject as one of four acquisition-selective regions; its activity declines across runs only while the rule is being acquired, is greater for a learnable set than for a matched ruleless one, correlates negatively with trial-by-trial accuracy, and its blockwise coactivation with dorsolateral prefrontal cortex accounts for **63%** of between-subject variance in accuracy — the largest effect reported (Boettiger & D'Esposito 2005) | Whether an architecture needs a separate module holding the *set of candidate responses* on which the controller's bias acts, or whether the controller can bias premotor/striatal storage directly. Position B assigns SMA/pre-SMA exactly that role and makes controller↔candidate-set coupling the performance-limiting quantity; position A says the slot can be deleted with no cost. Partly reconcilable — ablation asks whether a region is *necessary*, imaging whether it is *engaged*, and a redundant region can be both engaged and dispensable — but the effect size on the imaging side is too large for the null to be read as "not involved", and the two used different tasks: monkey one-to-one cue→action edges against human higher-order categorical rules over 10-member sets, which is precisely the manipulation the human study argues made the prefrontal signal visible at all | `LIVE` — the discriminating experiment is a medial premotor ablation on a *higher-order* mapping task (sets, not single cues), which nobody has run; the wiki should not cite either row as settled |
| T110 | **When a task affords no higher-order structure, does the learner withdraw from the abstract level or keep imposing it?** | **Withdraws, and measurably.** With two rule sets identical in stimuli, instructions, trial count and action space, and differing only in whether a 2nd-order regularity exists in the reward table, the 2nd-order frontal band (prePMd) is engaged above baseline in *both* sets at the beginning of learning and then declines specifically in the flat set by the middle phase (`F(1,19)=4.3, p<.05`) while staying flat in the hierarchical one (`F=.05`) — so within ~120 trials the learner has estimated that no structure exists at that depth and disengaged (Badre et al. 2010, [[wiki/concepts/policy-abstraction-hierarchy.md]]) | **Keeps imposing it, at a price it does not have to pay.** Subjects build a context→task-set hierarchy on a problem that neither requires nor rewards one, paying six links against four and slower initial learning, and the commitment is durable enough to produce systematic negative transfer when a new context partially matches the imposed rule (Collins & Frank 2013, [[wiki/entities/c-ts-model.md]]) | Whether a machine needs an explicit disengagement rule for an abstraction level, or whether an over-applied hierarchy is a tolerable default. Under A a level carries its own engagement gain driven by whether its hypotheses earn reward, and the wiki's parallel-search story comes with a built-in pruning step; under B the hierarchical prior is unconditional and the cost is paid on every flat problem, which is gap G23's "a prior needs an entry test" in its sharpest form. Partly reconcilable — attenuation of a BOLD signal is not abandonment of a representation, and the two tasks differ in what the flat alternative costs (18 arbitrary edges, which is genuinely hard, versus 4 links, which is not) — so A's decline may be reallocation toward the level that is working rather than a decision that no structure exists | `LIVE` — the discriminating test is behavioural and unrun: after a flat set is mastered, introduce a 2nd-order regularity and measure how long it takes to be found. A predicts a delay proportional to the disengagement; B predicts none |
| T111 | **Is a control representation a stable property of the tissue, or instantiated by the demand?** | **Stable, and modulated.** Every representational claim in the wiki reads this way: a region or a layer *has* a code for rule, category, target or task set, and task conditions change how strongly it is expressed — which is what licenses probing a trained model once and reporting what it contains ([[wiki/concepts/representation-probing.md]], [[wiki/concepts/cognitive-control.md]]) | **Instantiated.** In monkey posterior medial prefrontal cortex the *number of neurons representing the task at all* falls significantly (`p < 0.01`, χ²) when the task is retrained so only one response tactic is valid, and returns to its original size when both are restored — same animals, same penetrations, actions still varying, accuracy 99–100%, and no change in pre-SMA or SMA recorded in parallel. Earlier studies found no forelimb-task representation in this territory because their tasks required one tactic; the authors conclude the conventional concept of cortical motor representation "necessitates reconsideration" (Matsuzaka et al. 2012, [[wiki/concepts/policy-abstraction-hierarchy.md]]). A second instance from the same laboratory extends the claim to a *downstream* area and to a *sensory* variable: pre-SMA carries no significant cue-position signal in the mixed-tactic task, though pre-SMA is spatially tuned in reaching tasks with no tactic choice — read as the upstream area having absorbed the binding and never passing the argument down (Awan et al. 2020, [[wiki/concepts/policy-abstraction-hierarchy.md]]) | Whether a null probe result is evidence about the model or about the evaluation distribution — and whether a machine controller should be built as a always-present module whose output is gated, or as a representation that exists only while a decision at its level is live. Under B, "component X does not represent Y" is unfalsifiable without a demand manipulation, and every interpretability sweep in the wiki is missing that control | `LIVE` — not a clean contradiction, since a population that stops carrying the task may still hold its wiring, and the measure is participation (task-related cells per penetration) rather than per-cell rate. But the reversibility across a two-week retraining cycle is a control the machine literature has no analogue of, and the discriminating experiment is cheap: probe the same site under matched distributions differing only in whether the probed decision is live |
| T112 | **Does removing a control signal reduce downstream selection, or eliminate it?** | Biased competition: the controller adds excitatory bias to systems already running their own competition, so removing it should leave a degraded but non-zero downstream signal (Miller et al. 2002; [[wiki/concepts/cognitive-control.md]]) | Muscimol inactivation of ventral prearcuate cortex drives feature selection in the frontal eye field to **chance** (AUROC 0.633 → 0.508, post-effect `P` = 0.53) while spatial selection in the same 42 cells is unchanged (0.717 → 0.698, `P` = 0.21) — elimination, not attenuation (Bichot et al. 2015; [[wiki/concepts/priority-map.md]]) | Whether a control module is an additive prior over a competent substrate or the sole computer of a signal the substrate cannot produce — the two predict different lesion profiles and different failure modes for an ablated component | `LEANING B, with a reconciliation` — the biased area has **0%** object selectivity of its own, so there is no local competition over features for a bias to tilt; bias-not-routing may hold wherever the target area duplicates the represented variable and fail wherever it does not, which makes "does the downstream area independently represent the biased dimension?" the discriminating test. **Test run (Bichot et al. 2019):** the same inactivation, recorded in **V4** — an area that *is* colour- and shape-selective — still eliminates feature selection before the first saccade in both monkeys and after it in one, with a reduced-but-surviving effect in the other (`t` = 3.4, `P` = 0.001) and the loss independent of each unit's own colour/shape tuning, while spatial selection in the same cells is intact. The predicted residual is one third the control effect, in one animal, in one window, and is not separable from incomplete muscimol coverage — so **B holds even where the substrate duplicates the variable**, and additive bias survives only as a small correction term |
| T113 | **Is a control-band oscillation the control resource, or the receipt of one?** | **A resource whose amount matters.** Raising the gain of the human control loop (ventral internal capsule/ventral striatum deep brain stimulation) raises task-induced prefrontal theta power, and the per-subject theta increase tracks the per-subject response-time improvement on a conflict task and predicts clinical outcome (`n = 8, r = 0.76, p = 0.03`, AUC 0.87) — the authors' framing is that "amplifying those oscillations facilitates control", and their proposed clinic titrates stimulation *to the theta biomarker* (Widge et al. 2019, [[wiki/concepts/cognitive-control.md]]) | **A receipt of a process running elsewhere.** Theta-band coupling is precisely the component that *survives* cutting the direct projection between two regions, so in the wiki's one causal decomposition theta indexes a clock supplied by a third structure rather than the content or drive on the link (Spellman et al. 2015; Yamamoto et al. 2014, [[wiki/concepts/inter-areal-synchrony.md]]); and in monkey prefrontal–hippocampal recordings the *band*, not the amount, codes outcome (Brincat & Miller 2015) | Whether a machine controller should have a scalar "control amount" worth reading out and optimising, or whether band power is a coordination artefact and the transferable variable is timing. Under A a closed-loop system can titrate control to a measured oscillation, which is the entire clinical proposal; under B such a system tunes to an epiphenomenon and any behavioural gain came from something the stimulation also did | `LIVE` — the two are not directly opposed (power at one site is not coupling between two), and the decisive design has not been run: the stimulation was delivered at 50–130 Hz and theta was only ever *measured*, so a theta-band-targeted manipulation with a matched off-band control is what would separate the readings. What the human result does establish independently of the band question is the gain claim itself — control performance rose with no change to the task model |
| T114 | **Does behavioural guidance by a feature imply an attentional gain signal for that feature in sensory cortex?** | **Yes, by assumption** — the priority map is `sim(template, stimulus(ℓ))` over the cued features, and feature-match enhancement in V4 is the read-out of that similarity; both cued dimensions should enhance matching items ([[wiki/concepts/priority-map.md]]) | **No.** In 20-item colour×shape conjunction search, monkeys fixate same-**shape** distractors far above chance (`t` = 21.9 / 11.2, `P` < 10⁻⁹ / 10⁻⁷) yet V4 shows **no significant enhancement** for a same-shape distractor in control *or* inactivation sessions, while the same-**colour** enhancement is present and is abolished by VPA inactivation (Bichot et al. 2019) | Whether a model can be validated by behavioural guidance curves at all: if shape guides the scan without a shape-match gain at the recorded stage, then the priority map is either computed on a dimension the recorded area does not express, or assembled somewhere other than where the gain is measured — and matching a model's attention weights to fixation statistics would certify the wrong mechanism | `LIVE` — plausible deflations: V4's shape tuning at 4–5 dva eccentricity may be too coarse for these shapes, the shape match may be computed in IT/TEO instead, or the target-conjunction enhancement may swamp single-feature effects; none is tested here, and a null is not a dissociation |
| T115 | **Does the preparatory attentional template hold the target?** | **Yes — that is what makes it a template.** Ventral prearcuate cortex holds persistent *object-selective* firing for the cued object through the delay and across saccades, and it is the similarity of each location to *that* code that the priority map computes (Bichot et al. 2015, [[wiki/concepts/priority-map.md]]); every attention and retrieval layer in the wiki likewise sets the query to an encoding of the goal | **Not necessarily — and here, not at all.** Over an 8 s delay after a face cue, human cross-task MVPA decodes the *learned associate* (a scene category never shown) in the inferior frontal junction, parietal eye fields and parahippocampal place area, while the cued face is decodable in no template region (only left intraparietal sulcus, `P` = 0.019 uncorrected) and no region carries both; the swap is behaviourally motivated — matched distractor faces are hard to discriminate, the four scenes are not, and the associate stops being used when the target is easy (Zhou & Geng 2025, 2024) | Whether "what am I looking for" is a function of the goal or a decision with its own utility. Under A a query register is written by the goal encoder and is inspectable as such; under B an architecture needs a proxy-selection sub-policy (predictive validity × detectability), a monitor for proxy failure, and a reload path — and interpretability that reads the query register expecting the goal will find something else | `LIVE, plausibly reconcilable` — the two are not strictly opposed if the register holds *whatever currently maximises expected guidance value*, which reduces to the target when the target is the most discriminable cue (the monkey studies' condition). What is untested is the crossover: nobody has varied target discriminability and associate validity independently. Two deflations for B: fMRI cannot show the delay-period associate causes the guidance, and the task cannot separate template installation from plain cued associative recall |
| T116 | **When a functionally defined subensemble and its parent population rank two channels in opposite orders, which reading is the pathway's function?** | **The whole population.** It is the unbiased sample of what the projection carries, and any subensemble is defined by a criterion imported from a different task — here, amygdala-projecting prefrontal cells separate social from non-social stimuli better than accumbens-projecting cells across all recorded neurons (`P < 0.001`), which is the claim a downstream reader receiving the whole projection would act on | **The subensemble.** Averaging over a population masks the cells that carry the state — the same dataset finds the ranking reversed within centre-ON cells (`P < 0.001`), and it is the subensemble, not the population, whose activity tracks anxiogenic-zone entry, unfamiliar-open-arm entry and social preference across three assays a day apart (Lai et al. 2026) | Every claim of the form "module X encodes Y" in the wiki, and every model-vs-brain comparison that fits a decoder to all units: the cell-selection rule is a free parameter that can flip the sign of the result, and nothing in the data picks it. A downstream structure receiving the axons has no way to apply the experimenter's subensemble criterion, which argues for the population — unless the subensemble is itself defined by a projection or cell type the reader can address | `LIVE` |
| T117 | **Does perceiving a sequence rest mainly on top-down predictions, or on within-level priors over motion?** | **Top-down.** The wiki's hierarchical stance throughout: a level's percept is constrained by the prediction descending from the level above, and abstraction is what disambiguates the stream — the structural priors of [[wiki/concepts/predictive-coding-free-energy.md]], the manifold-shaping control parameters of the hierarchical dynamical model itself, and the whole read of feedback as the carrier of context | **Within-level.** In the same model, simulated lesions dissociate the two: cutting the top-down connections that shape the low-level manifold (structural priors) degrades recognition, but cutting the self-connections coupling orders of motion (dynamical priors, i.e. the generalised-coordinate constraint) is **worse** — frequency tracking survives while sequential structure is destroyed and the stream is no longer parsed at all (Friston & Kiebel 2009) | How to spend capacity in a sequence model. Under A the payoff is depth and richer top-down channels; under B most of the work is done by each level's own prior over how its state moves, and a shallow model with strong dynamics would beat a deep one with weak dynamics. It also relocates [[wiki/concepts/event-segmentation.md]]'s open problem: if the dynamical prior parses the stream, the missing boundary detector may be missing because no boundary variable exists | `LIVE` — the evidence is one simulated lesion on one hand-built two-Lorenz model with known parameters, and "worse" is reported qualitatively (inflated prediction error, lost sequential structure), not scored. The comparison has never been run on a learned system, where the relative strength of the two priors is itself trained rather than designed |
| T118 | **What does a superficial pyramidal spike mean — a candidate interpretation, or a residual?** | **A candidate interpretation.** The superficial layers are where the column explores possible readings of its input: L2/3 pyramids receive feedforward, lateral-patch and deep feedback input, compete through perisomatic inhibition in an iterated soft winner-take-all, and what ascends to the next area is the *selected* interpretation, with the deep layers committing it to output (Douglas & Martin 2004) | **A residual.** The same cells encode precision-weighted prediction error on hidden causes and nothing else; expectations live on supragranular *interneurons* and on deep pyramids, inhibition *encodes* the hidden-state variables rather than arbitrating, and what ascends is only what the level above failed to predict (Bastos et al. 2012; Friston & Kiebel 2009) | Everything read off superficial-layer recordings, and the design of any laminar-inspired module. Under A the forward channel carries content and the receiver gets a hypothesis; under B it carries only what is unexplained and the receiver must already hold the model. The two also disagree on inhibition's job — selection versus representation — so they predict opposite effects of silencing supragranular interneurons: loss of competition (A) versus loss of a state variable (B). Both readings fit the same wiring diagram, which is why the anatomy alone cannot decide | LIVE |
| T119 | **Where does the variability in a learning task enter the generative model?** | **At the output.** This is what the backpropagation limit of a predictive-coding network assumes: a chain of latent layers in which one variance parameter `Σ^(0)` dominates all others, so the residual is entirely at the level where the target is supplied. It is the regime in which the local update provably converges to backpropagation, and the regime every result on [[wiki/concepts/biologically-plausible-credit-assignment.md]] is scored against | **At every level, and the graph is not a chain.** Classifying letters has variability in stroke angle and stroke length as much as in pixel colour; and a child learning letter sounds has a latent *letter* that causes both the seen shape and the heard sound — a two-armed tree with noise on both arms, not a chain with noise at one end. Predictive-coding networks with **no** variance parameter dominating are therefore argued to be the better model of cortex, and they are the ones that learn bidirectional associations (Whittington & Bogacz 2017) | Decides whether backpropagation-approximation is the target for biologically plausible learning or merely a calibration point of it. If the second column is right, the wiki's whole scoring of local rules by "how close to backpropagation" measures the wrong thing, and the precision ratio becomes a design knob rather than an approximation error | `LEANING` — toward the second column. The argument is structural rather than empirical (no experiment measures cortical precision ratios), but it is supported by the fact that the equivalence limit is *anti-biological*: `Σ^(0) → ∞` drives error-node activity to zero, which spikes cannot encode, while the usable setting `Σ^(0) = 1` costs nothing measurable on MNIST (0.00% train, 1.7–1.8% validation, same as backpropagation) |
| T120 | **Is an infinitesimal nudge unusable, or is it the correct regime?** | **Unusable.** With a Gibbs–Boltzmann state distribution at finite temperature, the perturbation `Δs = s_β − s_0` is indistinguishable from sampling noise for `β ≲ 10⁻²`; on Fashion-MNIST, `β = 0.01` equilibrium propagation sits at 20–30% (near chance) while `β = 1.0` tracks the backpropagation baseline at ~80%, and update–gradient cosine similarity rises monotonically from ~0 at `β ≈ 10⁻³` to ~0.5 at `β = 1`. The infinitesimal limit is not a safer approximation, it is one whose signal is below the noise floor of the sampler that measures it (Litman 2025, [[wiki/concepts/equilibrium-propagation.md]]) | **Correct, and two orders of magnitude smaller still.** On full ImageNet with a 10-layer convolutional predictive-coding net, top-5 error is **flat across `0.0002 ≤ β ≤ 0.1`** provided `K ≥ 4`, and the whole result (13.23% top-5 against a 12.20% backpropagation baseline) is obtained in that regime. The floor on `β` is named explicitly and it is not statistical: perturbations must survive propagation through the network *given limited machine precision* (Kerjan et al. 2026, [[wiki/concepts/predictive-coding-free-energy.md]]) | Whether `β` is a hyperparameter to search or a quantity fixed by the theory — and, underneath it, whether the free phase is *sampled* or *solved*. Litman's `s_0` is a Monte-Carlo draw from `ρ_0`, so differencing two draws pays a sampling-noise tax that grows as `β` shrinks. Kerjan's free equilibrium is the unique global minimum of `E_PCN` with `E = 0`, reached deterministically in one forward pass, so there is no draw and no tax — only round-off. If that reading is right, the "infinitesimal equilibrium propagation fails" result is a fact about **stochastic** energy-based learners and does not transfer, and every architecture decision that follows from it (finite `β`, path-integral updates, a temperature to tune) is conditional on the free state being sampled rather than computed | `LIKELY BOTH` — no experiment varies the free-phase *estimator* while holding the energy and the task fixed, which is the one comparison that would settle it. The cheap test: run Litman's Fashion-MNIST setup on `E_PCN` at `T → 0`, where the free phase becomes a forward pass, and see whether the `β = 0.01` failure survives |
| T121 | **Is event-model updating controlled by one signal or by several?** | **One.** Every detector in the wiki thresholds a single scalar and emits an untyped boundary: Event Segmentation Theory thresholds prediction error, the long-short-term-memory gate thresholds one accumulated quantity, the sticky hierarchical Dirichlet process thresholds one novel-context responsibility. Parsimony is the argument, and a single trigger is what makes the boundary consumable — one node-creation rule downstream ([[wiki/concepts/event-segmentation.md]], [[wiki/concepts/contextual-inference.md]]) | **At least two, and probably more.** Prediction error and prediction *uncertainty* (epistemic variance across 32 dropout predictions) each uniquely predict human boundary density despite correlating at r = 0.499 — uncertainty at t = 14.32, error at t = 5.14 (df = 1636) — and they recruit dissociable networks with opposite post-boundary signatures: error → ventrolateral prefrontal shift then widespread pattern stabilisation, uncertainty → dorsal-attention shift with almost none. In the parcels responsive to both, the timecourses still differ significantly. The two together leave ~16% of explainable variance, with latent-cause inference named as a third mechanism. The authors accept the cost explicitly: such an architecture "would be inelegant—but evolution's engineering solutions are sometimes complex and kludgy rather than simple and sleek" (Nguyen et al. 2025, [[wiki/concepts/event-segmentation.md]]) | Gap G27 and everything downstream of it. If B holds, a segmentation module has no single threshold to tune, needs an arbitration policy between triggers that nobody has specified, and should emit **typed** boundaries — `REPLACE` (commit a new node) vs. `INVESTIGATE` (resample, do not commit) — which no wiki architecture consumes | `LEANING B` — B has the only measurement that separates the two signals rather than assuming they co-vary, and it separates them both behaviourally and anatomically. What A retains: the two triggers are still hand-thresholded, so "two signals" may be two hand-set readouts of one underlying precision-weighted quantity that a properly normative account would recombine (see [[wiki/concepts/expected-free-energy.md]], where `ℓ` and `Φ` are two terms of *one* objective with no free mixing coefficient) |
| T122 | **What does phasic dopamine carry — a reward prediction error, or the precision of a prediction error?** | **Reward prediction error.** The standard reading the wiki runs on everywhere: `δ = r + γV(s′) − V(s)`, a *teaching signal* delivered to a plasticity rule from outside the network ([[wiki/entities/pbwm.md]]'s PVLV, [[wiki/entities/meta-rl-agent.md]]'s input channel, every temporal-difference model here). Its evidence is the firing record itself, reproduced in detail by two Rescorla–Wagner systems, and it now has a primary source in the wiki that states the whole taxonomy A belongs to — dopamine `δ`, serotonin `γ`, noradrenaline `β`, acetylcholine `α` — together with circuit placements and six control laws for the other three ([[wiki/concepts/neuromodulatory-metaparameters.md]], Doya 2002). That sharpens the disagreement: B assigns acetylcholine the *precision of exteroceptive inference* and A assigns it the *learning rate*, so the two accounts now differ on at least two of the four chemicals, not one. | **Precision — "the value of prediction error", not "the prediction error on value".** Under the free-energy formulation, value ≡ surprise and expected value ≡ entropy, so the value function is redundant and dopamine's classical *neuromodulatory* action (postsynaptic gain) is read as encoding the precision of proprioceptive/interoceptive prediction errors — i.e. the Rescorla–Wagner **learning rate** and incentive salience, symmetric with acetylcholine doing the same for exteroceptive inference in posterior cortex. Supporting observation: low dopamine ⇒ imprecise priors ⇒ small prediction errors ⇒ **poverty of action** (bradykinesia in Parkinson's disease and under neuroleptics), which an RPE account explains only indirectly (Friston 2009, [[wiki/concepts/precision-weighting.md]]). | Which slot the signal occupies in an architecture. Position A makes it an input to a *learning rule*; position B makes it a run-time **gain register** inside the *inference dynamics* — the very register gaps G50 and G56 say the wiki lacks. A builder cannot implement both without deciding which one the modulator's set-point is optimising. Both readings are now stated side by side on [[wiki/concepts/reward-prediction-error.md]], together with the licence that keeps this deferrable — the striatal mechanism needs only that the signal move bidirectionally with outcome. The wiki has no experiment separating them, and the source lists the reconciliation of this taxonomy (dopamine = reward error, serotonin = reward timescale, noradrenaline = action randomness, acetylcholine = memory-update speed) with a single precision role as explicitly open. | `LIVE` |
| T123 | **Does active inference remove the exploration–exploitation weight, or only fix its value?** | **Removes it.** The wiki's reading of [[wiki/concepts/expected-free-energy.md]] since the convex-MDP ingest: written over occupancies the objective is `⟨ℓ,μ⟩ + Φ(μ)`, one linear cost plus one convex term with **no mixing coefficient anywhere in the expression** — the balance is the geometry of the objective, and the curiosity bonus `−log ρ_t(s)` is a gradient, not a knob (Milosevic et al. 2026). | **Only fixes its value, and the value is a substantive empirical claim.** The ρ-POMDP reduction makes expected free energy *identical* to reward planning plus expected information gain at `w = 1` nat; the authors state outright that EFE "does not eliminate the weight, it derives one". The derived value is reward-near-optimal in 9 / 22 / 32 % of 100 random two-state environments at `H = 1 / 2 / 3`, the success-maximising weight is 20–200× larger on every environment tested, and the advantage vanishes below `γ = 0.99` and reverses at `γ = 0.90` (Cooper & Velasquez 2026). | Whether the epistemic term needs a calibration argument. Under A it does not, and any implementation may adopt it unexamined; under B the wiki's candidate for the empty objective slot (G30) carries a hyperparameter whose default is defensible only in a named regime — high reward asymmetry (`α ≥ 5`), several observation actions, `H ≥ 2`, near-undiscounted, hidden state preserved by observation. Note the positions are not contradictory in the derivation, only in what follows from it: B accepts A's algebra and denies that a coefficient stops being a coefficient by being derived. | `LEANING` (to B — it is A's own claim, measured) |
| T124 | **Which epistemic term does the work — uncertainty about the *state*, or uncertainty about the *model*?** | **The state.** Both structural results in the wiki score uncertainty about where the agent is or will be: Milosevic et al. 2026 reduce expected free energy to `⟨ℓ,μ⟩ + Φ(μ)` with `Φ = −Σ_t H(ρ_t)`, the negative entropy of the predictive *state* marginal, and explicitly **exclude** parameter-novelty terms from the convex-MDP result; Cooper & Velasquez 2026 identify the objective with a ρ-POMDP whose utility is information gain about a belief over a *fixed hidden state*, and prove the identity only where observation leaves that state unchanged. Both are theorems. | **The model.** Decomposing the same objective into entropy corrections separates the observation side into ambiguity (`H[q(y\|x,θ)]`, precision of the sensor at reachable states) and novelty (`H[q(y\|x)] − H[q(y\|x,θ)]`, expected information gain about the parameters `θ`), and ablation puts nearly the whole effect on novelty: with the environment layout as `θ` and one-shot sensing, the full scheme retrieves `98.7%` of good rocks for reward `4.01` while every ablation — including the ambiguity-only heuristic — never probes at all (`0.0%`, reward `1.00`), and Wumpus success is `40.7%` vs. `≤24.0%`. Ambiguity substitutes for novelty *only* when a sensing action persistently changes the observation kernel (Nuijten et al. 2026). | Whether the wiki's derived exploration drive transfers to structure discovery. A latent-graph agent's unknown *is* a parameter, and its probes are typically one-shot readings that do not alter the sensor — the exact regime where A's covered term is worth nothing and B's excluded term is worth everything. It also means the two clean theoretical results characterise the term with the smallest measured effect: no existence, uniqueness, convergence or weight-calibration result covers parameter novelty. | `LIVE` — not a contradiction in algebra (the terms are different objects over different distributions) but a direct conflict over which one an implementation should carry; B has the only measurements |
