| T125 | **Does the epistemic term help or hurt once it is implemented?** | **Helps — it is the whole point.** Three structural results in the wiki agree the term is derived rather than tuned and that it pays: `−H(ρ_t)` is the objective's only nonlinearity and its gradient is a coverage bonus (Milosevic et al. 2026); `w = 1` sits at the Pareto knee on every one of eleven benchmarks and its advantage *grows* with state-space size (Tileworld 8×8: 66.5% vs. 2.5%; Cooper & Velasquez 2026); ablating the novelty correction takes rock retrieval from 98.7% to 0.0% (Nuijten et al. 2026) | **Hurts, in every form anyone has shipped.** Five one-step neural estimators of `G` crossed with three action-selection rules on dSprites: the *only* configurations that solve the task are the ones with the epistemic term deleted (`G⁴ = −ψr`), one variant diverges to `NaN`, and the "principled" estimate `D_KL[P_θs ‖ Q_φs] − ψr` collapses the policy onto a single action — action-prior entropy → 0, transition-network variance low for that action alone — because a divergence between the agent's forward model and its own encoder is minimised by *not acting differently* (Champion et al. 2023, [[wiki/entities/deep-active-inference-agent.md]]) | Decides whether a reasoning agent's exploration drive can be derived from its planning objective or must be supplied separately (ε-greedy, an entropy bonus, a tuned coefficient). If B holds in general, the wiki's cleanest theoretical answer to exploration is unavailable in code | `LIVE` — but the two sides may not be testing the same object. A's evidence is tabular/tree-search, over horizons > 1, and A's own novelty term is over *parameters*; B's five definitions are all horizon-1 and all over *states*, in a regime Cooper & Velasquez independently report as degenerating to myopic information gain. **The discriminating experiment is stated and unrun:** a deep implementation with depth > 1, an information gain over `θ` rather than over `s`, and a sign audit — *can an agent that freezes its own policy drive this term to zero?* If yes, the term is a self-consistency penalty, not an information gain . **A's side also now has a datapoint that is not an agent at all:** on N=25 humans in a contextual two-armed bandit where information carries an explicit price, active inference with all three `G` terms beats both model-free and model-based reinforcement learning by BIC, and participants pay for the cue far more often than they decline it — behaviour a reward-only learner has no reason to produce (Zhang et al. 2025, `raw/zhang-2025-novelty-variability-active-inference.md`). This is *fit to a biological decision-maker*, not task performance, and the paper concedes the complete class theorem (Wald 1947) makes it weak evidence: some prior always exists that reproduces any behaviour. **A's side also has a gradient-optimised entry, which narrows B to amortised neural estimators:** minimising a variational free energy with epistemic priors by Adam on a factorized categorical posterior — no message passing, no tree search — takes a stochastic cue maze from 0–17% to 100% success and beats tree-search Sophisticated Inference (94%), so the failure mode is not "gradients on an epistemic term" but specifically a one-step neural `G`-estimator built from generative rather than variational factors (Nuijten et al. 2026, `raw/nuijten-2026-efe-planning-variational-inference.md`) |
| T126 | **Which correction to plain variational inference does the work — the action-entropy planning term, or the epistemic priors?** | **The epistemic priors, overwhelmingly.** Ablated on three grid-worlds where the layout is the unknown parameter `θ`: the planning correction `+Σ_t H[q(u_t\|x_{t-1})]` is worth `51.9 → 54.5%` on Frozen Lake, while the ambiguity and novelty corrections carry the rest — `0.0% → 98.7%` rock retrieval, `5.5% → 40.7%` on Wumpus. Without novelty a probe that only informs is worthless and the agent walks past it (Nuijten et al. 2026, `raw/nuijten-2026-what-inference-is-active-inference.md`) | **The planning term, wherever the horizon is long.** Same group, same year, three different environments: on MiniGrid DoorKey-8×8 at `T=20` the planning correction alone takes success from **4% to 89%** — without it the agent cannot chain find-key → unlock → reach-goal — and adding the full epistemic priors on top changes nothing (89%, "neither help nor hinder"), because the key and door are revealed incidentally by the field of view rather than by a dedicated probe (Nuijten et al. 2026, `raw/nuijten-2026-efe-planning-variational-inference.md`) | Decides what a builder must implement first. The two corrections are cheap separately and neither implies the other, so the question is which failure a given task will exhibit — inability to *value a detour* (needs epistemic priors) or inability to *coordinate a long sequence* (needs the planning correction) | `LIVE` — plausibly not a disagreement but an unformalised regime split: epistemic priors pay when uncertainty is concentrated in a latent parameter resolved only by a dedicated action, the planning correction pays when the horizon is long and uncertainty dissolves as a by-product of moving. **No principle predicts in advance which regime a task is in**, and a reasoning agent doing [[wiki/concepts/latent-graph-discovery.md]] is in both at once |
| T127 | **Is uncertainty itself represented, or only the value of resolving it?** | **Uncertainty is a first-class represented quantity.** [[wiki/concepts/precision-weighting.md]]'s three-way partition gives precisions their own sufficient statistic `μ^λ`, their own substrate (synaptic gain / neuromodulation / local synchrony) and their own timescale (second-order, minimising the path integral `A = ∫F dt`), separate from states and parameters; the whole attention-is-precision claim, and every learning-rate-as-precision mechanism the wiki imports, requires the magnitude of uncertainty to exist somewhere as a signal (Friston 2009) | **Only its value is.** Regressing human source-space induced power on each term of expected free energy separately, the *value of reducing* novelty (rostral middle frontal gyrus, insula, lateral orbitofrontal) and the *value of reducing* variability (medial orbitofrontal cortex) both survive FDR correction at multiple stages, while the **degree** of novelty has no surviving correlate anywhere and the degree of variability likewise. What is carried is uncertainty already multiplied by what resolving it is worth (Zhang et al. 2025, `raw/zhang-2025-novelty-variability-active-inference.md`) | Decides whether an architecture needs a separate uncertainty register at all. If B holds, `Π` and an epistemic bonus are not two variables read from one number but one variable — and a model that exposes uncertainty magnitude to downstream control is representing something the brain does not. It also bears on [[wiki/concepts/expected-free-energy.md]]'s open question of whether the epistemic term is a *state* of the planner or a *term in its loss* | `LIVE, weak` — B is a null under low power and the authors say so: the task never varied uncertainty magnitude parametrically, so degree had little variance to regress on. The discriminating experiment is a design that holds the value of resolving fixed while sweeping the amount to resolve |
| T128 | **Are all precisions inferred, or are some structural constants?** | Precisions are a represented quantity optimised by the same free-energy descent as states and parameters, carried by synaptic gain — attention, salience and learning rate are all one inferred number (Friston 2009, [[wiki/concepts/precision-weighting.md]]) | At least one prior is **cognitively impenetrable**: in the hollow-mask illusion a correct, confidently held, reportable belief that the mask is concave never revises the percept, which is claimed to be evidence for a prior in older visual circuitry that higher levels cannot write to (`talk-nd-free-energy-principle`, **(tentative)** — an explainer, no experiment) | Decides whether an architecture may treat every prior's precision as a learnable parameter, or must partition the hierarchy into inferred and frozen gains — and whether "top-down wins" means "the agent's current best belief wins" or "a fixed prior wins" | `LIVE` — B's source is non-scientific and the illusion has scientific literature the wiki has not ingested; the tension is well-posed regardless, since A's formalism has no place to put a frozen gate |
| T129 | **Is the direct/indirect relation a scalar balance, or two concurrently active ensembles?** | **A balance.** The classical model reads movement as a shift in the net drive on a single output interface, and the wiki's model of it computes a literal difference, `net_j = [Go_j − NoGo_j]⁺` ([[wiki/entities/pbwm.md]]). Dopamine depletion moving the two pathways in opposite directions, optogenetic activation of each pathway producing opposite motor effects (Kravitz et al. 2010; Hikida et al. 2010), and the opponent plasticity mechanism all fit it | **Two ensembles, both up.** Mink's elaboration predicts that during ongoing behaviour activity *increases* in ensembles of **both** pathways — select this program, suppress the competing ones — and primate limb-reaching recordings confirm it (Turner & Anderson 1997). On this reading the informative variable is *which* ensembles are co-active, and in Parkinson's models it is the **timing** of the two pathways' activity rather than the level that predicts movement choice and initiation (Bevan et al. 2002); Gerfen & Surmeier 2011 add that whether the two pathways even receive the same cortical information is unresolved | Decides whether an opponent gate can be collapsed to one scalar per channel. If B holds, a difference discards the identity of the co-active ensembles and the relative timing between them, and no architecture in the wiki represents either — PBWM's `kWTA` over `[Go − NoGo]⁺` cannot express "both channels active, different sets" at all | `LIVE` — the two are not strictly incompatible (a difference can be computed over co-active populations), but no wiki model computes one, and the timing result is outside both formulations |
| T130 | **In a gated working memory, is the basal ganglia a write-enable or the maintenance loop itself?** | **A write-enable that then lets go.** The module releases a reverberating loop and drops out — either a thalamo-cortical loop (Monchi et al. 2000; FROST, Ashby et al. 2005) or a cortico-cortical one whose switch it flips (Frank et al. 2001; Moustafa & Maida 2007; [[wiki/entities/pbwm.md]]). Four of the five reviewed working-memory models take this position, and the cortico-cortical variant has an argument behind it: for thalamus to carry maintenance it would need as many dedicated cells as prefrontal cortex has, which is implausible (Frank et al. 2001) | **The loop runs through it.** In Schroll et al. 2012 the reverberation passes through the direct pathway, so the module *is* the maintenance mechanism, with the hyperdirect pathway supplying the reset that ends it. This is the most recent and most capable of the five — the only one that both learns what deserves maintenance and simulates delayed alternation and 1-2-AX — and it departs from the others without arguing for the departure (Helie et al. 2013) | Decides whether gating capacity is consumed for the whole retention interval. Under A, interference between maintenance and concurrent action selection scales with the *rate of writes*; under B, with the *number of items held*. No wiki architecture represents the second cost at all, and a transformer's key/value cache is position A taken to its limit | `LIVE` — no behavioural or lesion datum in the review separates them; the distinguishing dual-task simulation is cheap and has not been run |
| T131 | **When a behaviour becomes automatic, is the policy distilled into a new substrate or migrated to another loop?** | **Distilled and the module bypassed.** In SPEED (Ashby et al. 2007) the basal ganglia's role is to fire the correct premotor plan consistently enough for Hebbian learning to build a direct sensory-association → premotor cortico-cortical association; once that shortcut is strong the module is no longer in the path. This is the literal version of [[wiki/concepts/cognitive-control.md]]'s withdrawal step, and it predicts that practice *returns* controller capacity | **Migrated, and the module still required.** Control moves from an anterior visual-coordinate striatal loop to a posterior motor-coordinate one but never leaves the striatum (Nakahara et al. 2001), or the habitual action keeps being selected by the same direct/indirect competition (Chersi et al. 2013). Miyachi's muscimol dissociation — anterior caudate/putamen disrupts early production, middle-posterior putamen disrupts late production — is direct evidence that a striatal site is still load-bearing after overtraining | Decides the parallel capacity of automatic behaviour: distillation into cortical weights is unbounded, migration is capped by the number of loops. It also decides whether "handing a skill over" frees the controller, which is the assumption [[wiki/concepts/skill-acquisition-efficiency.md]] prices skills with | `LEANING A` — Desmurget & Turner 2010 inactivated the motor segment of the internal globus pallidus in trained monkeys, disconnecting the module from frontal cortex: movement kinematics were impaired, **sequence knowledge was not**. That is what A predicts and what both B models fail. Not decisive: the result is about an overlearned motor sequence, and B's late-phase striatal site is upstream of the inactivated output |
| T132 | **Is the indirect pathway a veto or an exploration channel?** | **A veto.** It carries `NoGo` — action-specific suppression of the alternatives, which must be individually addressable for probabilistic learning to work, and whose removal degrades performance (Frank 2005); the same reading drives the reaching and handwriting models (Bischoff 1998; Contreras-Vidal & Stelmach 1995) | **An explorer.** Chakravarthy's group inserts a third regime between `Go` and `NoGo` whose substrate is the indirect pathway: when the value gradient is absent or too weak to learn from, it sets the *stochasticity* of action selection. Dopamine-depleted simulations spend less time in `Go` and more in the other two, reproducing movement undershoot, bradykinesia and increased path variability in reaching, and increased search times in saccadic visual search (Magdoom et al. 2011; Krishnan et al. 2011) | Decides whether exploration is a hyperparameter or an anatomical channel with its own neuromodulatory control. Every wiki architecture bolts exploration on externally ([[wiki/entities/pbwm.md]]'s `Random Go` at fixed `p`); B makes annealing fall out of the same dopamine signal that sets the selection balance. The readings give opposite lesion predictions — A says a damaged veto makes an agent impulsive, B says it makes the agent deterministic and stuck | `LIVE` — the two are not formally exclusive (a stochastic veto is a veto), but they are fitted to disjoint data: A to probabilistic-learning and reversal behaviour, B to Parkinsonian motor kinematics, and no model in the 19-model census implements both. **A third position denies the premise:** in the optimal-decision mapping the indirect pathway plays no part in selection at all — the computation needs one inhibitory copy of the salience (direct pathway), one exponentiator (subthalamic nucleus) and one log-compressor (external pallidum), and the D₂ → pallidum projection is excluded from the core model and shown only *not to break it*. If that is right the pathway is free for the learning phase, and both A and B are looking for a selection-time job that the circuit does not need done (Bogacz & Gurney 2007, [[wiki/concepts/evidence-accumulation.md]]) |
| T133 | **What does the direct/indirect pathway balance set — the discount factor or the exploration temperature?** | **The discount factor.** The indirect pathway supplies the delayed term in the striatal temporal-difference computation, so raising its relative contribution raises the effective `γ`; serotonin from dorsal raphe reaches both striatum and the dopaminergic nuclei and is the proposed knob (Doya 2002, [[wiki/concepts/neuromodulatory-metaparameters.md]]). | **The exploration temperature.** Chakravarthy's family inserts an *explore* regime between `Go` and `NoGo` whose substrate is the indirect pathway: when the value gradient is flat, that channel sets the stochasticity of action selection (Magdoom et al. 2011; Krishnan et al. 2011, via Helie et al. 2013, [[wiki/entities/basal-ganglia.md]]). | Two theories read the same one-dimensional anatomical ratio as two different metaparameters, and both cannot be recovered from it without confounding — an agent that lengthened its horizon would be indistinguishable from one that started exploring. It also decides which gap the anatomy answers: G24 (no principled planning horizon) or G61 (exploration is always an external schedule). | `LIVE` — neither is implemented, and the shared premise is weaker than either: the balance may not be a scalar at all, since both pathways are active during movement and *timing* rather than level predicts choice (T129) |
| T134 | **Does serotonin set the prediction horizon or report the average reward?** | **The discount factor `γ`.** Serotonergic depletion makes rats take the small immediate over the large delayed reward (Mobini et al. 2000), low serotonin tracks impulsivity, and depression is recovered as a short-horizon regime in which a cost-then-reward policy scores below doing nothing (Doya 2002, [[wiki/concepts/neuromodulatory-metaparameters.md]]). | **The predicted average reward** in average-reward reinforcement learning, which replaces `(1−γ)V(s)` in the temporal-difference error and therefore makes serotonin a straightforward *opponent* of dopamine (Daw, Kakade & Dayan 2002). | The two predict opposite signs of the serotonin→dopamine interaction, and only under one of them is the horizon a settable parameter at all — under B the wiki has no candidate substrate for `γ`. | `LIVE` — with a stated discriminating experiment nobody in the wiki has run: measure the serotonergic effect on dopamine activity in conditions where predicted future reward `V(s)` is positive versus negative. A predicts facilitation then inhibition; B predicts inhibition in both |
| T135 | **What is compared when a brain decides whether to plan — the reliability of the two answers, or the cost and benefit of computing one?** | **Reliability.** Both controllers are run as approximate Bayesian learners and, per action, the estimate of the one with the smaller posterior variance is adopted; the arbitration variable is already maintained by each learner, nothing is priced in time or calories, and the crossings that produce habitisation fall out of two variance curves (Daw, Niv & Dayan 2005, [[wiki/concepts/amortized-inference.md]]). | **Cost and benefit.** Arbitration as a *value of computation*: plan when the expected improvement in the choice exceeds the resource cost of the deliberation, which the wiki has been citing alongside Position A as though they were one proposal (Keramati, Dezfouli & Piray 2011). | They need different machinery and predict opposite things under a speed constraint. Under A, adding time pressure changes nothing about which controller is *right*, so habitisation should track accumulated data and search depth alone; under B, time pressure is the whole cost term and should shift control to the cache even in a task where the tree's estimate is far more certain. Only A specifies an implementable quantity, and only B explains why deliberation stops when nothing is uncertain. | `LIVE` — with a cheap discriminating experiment: hold training, reward proximity and task complexity fixed and vary only the response deadline. A predicts no shift in devaluation sensitivity, B predicts a shift toward habitual responding |
| T136 | **Does a hierarchical learner deliver a subgoal-specific reward of its own?** | **Yes — pseudo-reward.** The options framework and MAXQ give each subtask its own reward function, delivered on reaching that subtask's subgoal, and it is what shapes the option-specific policy when external reward is silent; the framework therefore predicts a reward-like response to *subgoal attainment* in structures responsive to exogenous reward, and phasic dopamine at subtask boundaries scaled by the option-level prediction error (Botvinick, Niv & Barto 2009, [[wiki/concepts/temporal-abstraction-options.md]]). | **No.** The HAM framework shapes subtask policies with ordinary exogenous reward plus fixed structural constraints on the machine, and has no pseudo-reward at all; MAXQ additionally decomposes the value function into components rather than keeping one `V_o` per option, so the prediction errors computed in a given domain differ between frameworks. | It decides whether a machine hierarchical learner needs an *internal reward generator* per subgoal — with all of the specification burden that carries (who sets the subgoal, what its pseudo-reward is worth relative to the real one, what stops the agent from farming it) — or whether structure alone suffices. It also decides whether the flagship neural test is a test of HRL at all: a null result at subgoals refutes options/MAXQ and leaves HAM untouched. | `LIVE` — no experiment has yet looked for a reward-like signal at subgoal attainment in a hierarchically structured task, and the source names this as the discriminating measurement. |
| T137 | **Is the subthalamic nucleus a threshold unit or an exponentiator?** | **A threshold/saturating unit.** Every other model of the circuit assumes an ordinary neural transfer function and treats the nucleus as a fast, diffuse excitatory brake: piecewise-linear in Gurney et al. 2001, sigmoid in Frank 2005, undifferentiated "raise the floor for everything" excitation in Guthrie et al. 2013. On this reading the nucleus contributes a *magnitude* — more conflict, more brake — and nothing depends on the shape of the curve | **An exponentiator, exactly.** If the loop implements the multihypothesis sequential probability ratio test, the algorithm *requires* `STN_i = exp(y_i − GP_i)` and `GP_i = Σ − ln Σ`, so the nucleus and the external pallidum jointly compute `ln Σ_j exp(y_j)` — the log-softmax denominator. Exponential fits to pooled published firing-rate data hold up (Hallworth et al. 2003; Wilson et al. 2004), the prediction explains the nucleus's otherwise odd features (non-zero spontaneous rate = `exp(0)`, unusually high attainable rates), and the near-linear pallidal prediction matches Nambu & Llinás 1994 and Kita & Kitai 1991 (Bogacz & Gurney 2007, [[wiki/concepts/evidence-accumulation.md]]) | Decides whether the module is a generic competition or a **normaliser**. Under A the conflict term is a heuristic brake whose functional form is free, and a builder may replace it with any monotone function; under B the form is forced, selection is a threshold on a log-posterior, and the biological argument for putting a softmax denominator in its own subcircuit ([[wiki/entities/basal-ganglia.md]]) stands or falls with the curve. It also decides how much anatomy the selection computation needs: B does not use the indirect pathway at all (T132) | `LEANING B` on the fits, `LIVE` on their sufficiency — the exponentials are refits of *in vitro* current-injection data collected for other purposes, over a limited rate range (<135 Hz, where an exponential and the rising limb of a sigmoid are hard to separate), with no synaptic input and no behaving animal. The discriminating measurement is the `f–I` relation *in vivo* under synaptic drive, which the source names as its own outstanding test |
| T138 | **Does the separation/completion bias actually need a controller, or is a well-placed constant enough?** | **It needs one, and gap G38 says so.** The bias tracks the behavioural contrast rather than stimulus novelty, is not one number even within one animal at one time (different track positions decorrelate in a fixed order), and the wiki carries four candidate actuators for moving it at runtime — the cholinergic mode bit, the CA3→DG backprojection, BTSP's allocation rate `f_q`, and four separately-addressable interneuron families ([[wiki/concepts/pattern-separation-completion.md]], [[wiki/concepts/inhibitory-control-of-coding.md]], [[wiki/entities/btsp-cam.md]]) | **A constant is enough on a stationary distribution.** Sweeping the bias exhaustively in a working agent gives a wide tolerant plateau, not a narrow optimum: 43.54% of the threshold space reaches ≥90% accuracy, a contiguous region gives 95.03% ± 2.34%, and the accuracy distribution is negatively skewed (−0.495). The frozen threshold is never adjusted and the agent still beats every learned-representation baseline (Poursiami et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/hami.md]]) | Decides whether G38 is a blocking gap or a refinement. Under A a fast store without a runtime bias controller is incomplete, and building one is on the critical path; under B the design effort belongs elsewhere and the threshold is a one-time calibration. The plateau also changes what evidence would settle it: A's evidence is all *variation* in the biological bias, which is only decisive if the variation buys performance — none of the four candidate actuators has been shown to improve a task score by moving the knob | `LIVE`. The two positions are not tested on the same thing: the plateau is measured on a stationary observation distribution with four fixed rules and a frozen near-perfect encoder (NMI 0.97–0.9997), which is exactly the regime where a constant should work. The discriminating experiment is the sweep re-run under drifting contexts or a degrading encoder — if the plateau narrows or moves, A wins on its own terms |
| T139 | **What defines a behavioural segment boundary — predictive failure, or clustering under a smoothness prior?** | **Predictive failure.** Every detector on [[wiki/concepts/event-segmentation.md]] thresholds a quantity produced by a *predictor*: accuracy (Event Segmentation Theory), epistemic variance (Baldwin & Kosie; Nguyen et al. 2025), or latent-cause posterior uncertainty. A boundary is where a model of the dynamics breaks, and the two triggers even recruit separate cortical networks | **No predictor is needed.** [[wiki/entities/hisd.md]] segments Craftax and unmodified Minecraft with *no dynamics model at all*: frames are assigned to `K` prototypes by visual dissimilarity under an unbalanced optimal-transport plan with a temporal-smoothness penalty, and boundaries are wherever the argmax changes. It beats methods that do model the dynamics (OMPN, CompILE) on every task with more than two skills, including the perceptually aliased case where walking-toward-wood and walking-toward-stone are pixel-identical (Harvey et al. 2026) | Whether a builder needs the world model *before* the discretisation or after. If clustering-plus-smoothness suffices offline, the node set can be recovered from unlabelled video and the predictive machinery only ever has to work over already-discrete states — which reverses the dependency [[wiki/concepts/latent-graph-discovery.md]] assumes. The two positions are not strictly opposed: the optimal-transport route needs the whole corpus, a maximum skill count, and pre-extracted features, so it cannot run online, and it is silent on the *typed* boundaries (replace vs. investigate) the predictive account delivers for free | `LIVE` |
| T140 | **Is delay discounting one non-exponential valuation or two competing exponential systems?** | **Two systems.** Hyperbolic-*like* discounting is produced by a steep-`β` system (proposed as ventral striatum, medial prefrontal and posterior cingulate cortex, valuing immediate rewards) working against a patient-`δ` system in lateral prefrontal and posterior parietal cortex; the evidence is that the three regions respond more when an immediate option is on the screen (McClure et al. 2004, via Kable & Glimcher 2007). Behaviourally the two-exponential sum fits choices **as well as** the hyperbola, so choices alone cannot separate the accounts | **One system, one non-exponential kernel.** The same three regions' activity varies when only the *delayed* option changes — which the impulsive-system reading forbids — and the discount rate refitted to their time courses is neither steeper than the person's behaviour (`β` requires it: difference median −0.0081, `P = 0.001`) nor shallower (`δ` requires it: +0.0024, `P < 0.0001`), while against behaviour itself the difference is centred on zero (median −0.0009, `P = 0.67`) (Kable & Glimcher 2007, [[wiki/concepts/subjective-value.md]]) | Decides whether a builder needs *two* value heads with different horizons and an arbitrator between them — the natural machine reading of β–δ, and a close relative of the model-based/model-free arbitration of T135 — or one head emitting an already-discounted scalar. Under B the wiki's arbitration machinery is not needed for intertemporal choice at all, and the observed preference reversals must come from the kernel's shape rather than from a contest | `LEANING B` — B's test is strictly stronger (a mean-offset test, not a contrast), it reproduces in ROIs selected without reference to the discount rate, and it does not challenge A's original finding, only A's interpretation of it. Residual: B never presented two *delayed* options, which is the condition where a purely-immediate system would be silent and the accounts separate most cleanly |
| T141 | **Is the brain's discount kernel exponential, or hyperbolic?** | **Exponential — a settable `γ`.** Every value function in the wiki is `V = E[Σ_k γ^k r]`, the discount is a scalar metaparameter with a proposed chemical carrier (serotonin) and a proposed anatomical substrate (the direct/indirect balance), and the whole closed-loop control story depends on `γ` being one number that multiplies a recursive term (Doya 2002, [[wiki/concepts/neuromodulatory-metaparameters.md]]) | **Hyperbolic.** `SV = A/(1+kD)` fits every subject better than a single exponential (median `R² = 0.95`), consistent with a large animal and human literature, and the *neural* discount function refit to value-region time courses matches the hyperbolic behavioural one with zero mean offset (Kable & Glimcher 2007, [[wiki/concepts/subjective-value.md]]) | A hyperbolic kernel is **time-inconsistent**: `SV(D)` is not the composition of one-step discounts, preferences between a fixed pair reverse as both approach, and no stationary value function exists for dynamic programming to converge to. So under B the wiki's rollout scoring, its TD error ([[wiki/concepts/reward-prediction-error.md]]), and its `γ`-as-metaparameter substrate are all descriptions of something the brain does not compute — while under A the behavioural hyperbola must be an artefact of aggregation, uncertainty about receipt, or a nonlinear utility over amount | `LIVE`, and the two literatures barely touch: A is fitted to trial-by-trial learning in tasks lasting seconds, B to stated preferences over delays of hours to months. The cheap discriminating construction is a machine one — train an agent with exponential `γ` and refit both kernels to its *value units*; if a network optimising `γ^k` returns a hyperbolic neurometric function, B's finding stops being evidence about the algorithm at all |
| T142 | **Should the world model and the policy be decoupled, or learned as one entangled model?** | **Decoupled.** The wiki's default everywhere: learn `p(z′\|z,a)` and `p(x\|z)` task-agnostically, keep `π(a\|z)` (or a cost) separate, and the payoff is re-goaling — the same transition structure serving any new objective at query time (gap G28). Dreamer/PlaNet-style state-space models, [[wiki/entities/h-jepa.md]]'s configurable world-model-plus-cost, and every model-based/model-free contrast on [[wiki/concepts/simulation-based-planning.md]] assume this split. | **Entangled, and deliberately so.** The predictive-coding neurorobotics lineage (Tani and successors) trains a single joint model over sensation *and* action and generates behaviour by inference on it, reporting its robot results from that entangling rather than despite it; the affordance argument gives the reason — an action possibility is a property of the agent–environment pair, so an object's representation already contains what can be done to it, and factoring it out is throwing away the content ([[wiki/concepts/affordance-grounded-symbols.md]]). Taniguchi et al. 2023 pose the decoupling question as open. | Decides whether G28 is a gap to be closed or a target to be abandoned: if entangling is what makes robot-scale learning work, immediate re-goaling is not free but has to be bought back with a separate mechanism. Also bites [[wiki/concepts/objective-identifiability.md]] — a task-agnostic representation has no task to be identified against. | `LIVE` |
| T143 | **How much of the world needs to be inside the model at all?** | **Enough to plan on.** The wiki's entire framing: infer the latent graph, then navigate it ([[wiki/concepts/latent-graph-discovery.md]]); the richer and more causally faithful the internal model, the more queries it answers. | **Less than assumed — the world is its own best model.** Passive dynamic walkers walk with no controller and no model, from body–environment interaction alone; morphological computation makes the body itself part of the information processing, and subsumption architecture got flexible behaviour from no world model whatsoever. Taniguchi et al. 2023 close their survey on exactly this speculation: behaviours may be *emergent* from embodied interaction rather than externalised internal trajectories. | Sets how much of a reasoning system's competence is allowed to live outside its representation. The wiki has no page for the position and no criterion for which parts of a task are cheaper to offload to dynamics than to model — but note the scope limit: every cited demonstration is sensorimotor, and none of them does anything counterfactual, which is the capability the model is wanted for. | `LIVE` |
| T144 | **Does a world model's prediction quality tell you how good a world model it is?** | **Yes — that is what training it means.** Every objective in the wave is a prediction loss (reconstruction ELBO for RSSM/Dreamer, embedding distance for JEPA, video fidelity for the neural-simulator branch), and [[wiki/concepts/prediction-compression-equivalence.md]] makes the stronger claim that log-loss *is* a measure of structure found, so lower prediction error means more of the environment captured. | **No, not for control.** Conventional metrics like mean-squared error on future predictions "may not correlate with the performance of downstream tasks"; a model generating visually sharper predictions "might not necessarily enable a safer or more efficient control policy" (Long et al. 2025). The pixel-loss weighting is the mechanism: a small task-critical object contributes almost nothing to reconstruction error and vanishes from the latent (Dreaming), so the metric is anti-correlated with utility in exactly the cases that decide the task. **A second, exactly quantified mechanism now exists.** For an action-conditioned (LeJEPA-style) predictor under invertible observations and linear–Gaussian latent dynamics, a model with on-policy excess risk `δ` can have counterfactual error `δ / ρ_tr(π)`, where `ρ_tr` is the weakest conditional action excitation of the behaviour policy — attainably, and diverging as that policy becomes deterministic. At `ρ_tr = 0` two models with *identical* on-policy risk disagree about what alternative actions do. So prediction quality and control utility are not merely uncorrelated; the gap between them has a closed form, and the term that governs it is not in the model (Zhang et al. 2026). **A third mechanism, and it is not about representation at all.** Under an identical cross-entropy-method planner, energy function and GPU, an embedding-space model evaluates 800 candidate actions in 16 s while a 7B latent-diffusion video model evaluates 80 in 4 min, and the video model — the one whose training target *is* perceptual fidelity — scores 0% on both pick-and-place tasks against 80% and 50% (Assran et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/v-jepa-2.md]]). Prediction quality and control utility can come apart simply because a search's quality is a function of how many candidates the model lets it afford. | Model selection across this whole wave. If prediction error does not order models by planning utility, the RSSM-vs-JEPA comparison is not decidable on the numbers either lineage reports, and G62 has to close before any of it means anything. | `LIVE` |
| T145 | **Does conditioning a predictor on the action make it represent the effect of actions?** | **Yes — that is the point of the action-conditioned turn.** The whole latent-world-model lineage (V-JEPA 2-AC, LeWorldModel, Dreamer, TD-MPC) inserts the action into the latent predictor and justifies it empirically, by action sensitivity, rollout quality and downstream planning performance; a low action-conditioned prediction loss is taken as evidence that the model has captured how the world responds to what the agent does. **The strongest version of A does not rest on the loss at all.** AdaWorld extracts an action code from one video and applies it to a *different scene*, scoring the result against that scene's ground truth: FVD 767.0 against 1545.2 for the same architecture with the action condition zeroed, 70.5% human preference, and — on Procgen goal-reaching with a fixed MPC planner — 56.67% success against the action-agnostic model's 26.00%, which is the random planner's score. Transplanting a code across contexts is a test on-policy memorisation cannot pass (Gao et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/adaworld.md]]). | **No — action-conditioning plus low training loss demonstrates nothing.** In the one setting where it is provable, identifying the action response additionally requires positive **conditional** action excitation in the data. Measured at `σ = 0` (actions a deterministic function of state): on-policy prediction stays accurate, counterfactual error is large, and the state and action components `A` and `B` are not separately identifiable — the data constrain only the closed-loop map `A + BK`. The architecture is action-conditioned; the *learned object* is not (Zhang et al. 2026, [[wiki/concepts/learned-world-models.md]]). | Every action-conditioned world model trained on data from a competent or converged policy — i.e. all of them past the early-training phase. It also decides how much of the JEPA-vs-RSSM comparison is about architectures at all: two models trained on the same low-excitation data are both estimating the same under-determined object. Limits: linear–Gaussian latent dynamics, invertible observations, synthetic 2-D experiments, unreviewed preprint. The two positions are less opposed than they look and the residue is the interesting part: A's transfer test is *cross-context*, not *counterfactual* — every code it applies was extracted from a transition that actually occurred, so it never asks the model about an action the environment never took from that state, which is precisely the query B proves is under-determined. **That query has now been run once, qualitatively.** Driving V-JEPA 2-AC with an *identical* action sequence under an open versus a closed gripper produces a cup that stays put in one case and travels with the arm in the other — an action pairing the environment did not execute, answered correctly. It is one decoded figure with no metric attached, and it is the only counterfactual probe of a large action-conditioned world model in the wiki (Assran et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/v-jepa-2.md]]). | `LIVE`, with A ahead on everything that has been measured and B holding a query that has now been asked once, without a number |
| T146 | **Does a violation-of-expectation score measure the ability it names?** | **Yes — that is the protocol's whole virtue.** Paired plausible/implausible continuations of an identical history need no labels, no distribution shift and no absolute calibration; the score is a relative judgement of exactly the property under test, which is why the wiki lists it as an instrument for G17 ([[wiki/entities/hbtom.md]]). | **Not as reported.** The score is not fixed by the protocol but by three free choices made after the fact: (i) *which moment of the error trace counts as surprise* — `max_t` vs `mean_t` moves IRENE's Blocking Barrier from 83.5 to 99.4 and its Multi-Agent from 74.9 to 63.6, and the benchmark authors and a competing group justify opposite choices. **The primary source now states the reason in the open**: BIB uses the maximum because alternatives "like the mean expectedness of steps" *"consistently resulted in lower VOE scores"* (Gandhi et al. 2021, [[wiki/entities/bib.md]]) — the parameter was fixed by the scores it produced, at the benchmark's origin, and every BIB number in the wiki inherits it; (ii) *which subtasks are averaged* — two of three Instrumental subtasks are heuristic-solvable, so the average ranks the barrier-ignorant model above the barrier-aware one; (iii) *what the model may be handed* (T21). Alignment with infant looking times is likewise produced here by a model with no agent variable at all (Bortoletto et al. 2023, [[wiki/entities/irene.md]]). | Every BIB, AGENT and IntPhys-style number in the wiki, and G17's sixth instrument: paired VoE remains the cheapest protocol that needs no labels, but it certifies nothing until the error statistic is pre-registered and the shortcut-solvable subset is broken out. Sharpens the general reporting rule already on [[wiki/concepts/shortcut-learning.md]]: a *drop* in aggregate score can be evidence of structure learning. | `LIVE, two of four parameters now closed by practice` — Garrido et al. 2025 close (ii) and add the control the protocol never had: `n = 20` **randomly-initialised networks** of the same architecture run through the identical surprise pipeline, with every result reported as a per-property effect size over that null (Welch's t-test, Hedges' `g`) instead of as an accuracy. That is the machine analogue of habituation trials, it costs one forward pass per seed, and it makes a benchmark's low-level bias subtractable rather than arguable. What stays open is (i): `max_t` is still chosen, and still chosen because it scores better. Full treatment on [[wiki/concepts/violation-of-expectation.md]] |
| T147 | **Does making a learned model inspectable require a discrete bottleneck?** | **Yes — verification needs a finite symbol set.** The wiki's standing answer to the verifiability problem of [[wiki/concepts/learned-world-models.md]]: quantise the state into an equivalence class of situations and learn transitions as rules over that alphabet, which is what makes classical planners, PDDL rules and formal proof applicable at all ([[wiki/concepts/affordance-grounded-symbols.md]] — DeepSym, LatPlan, Konidaris et al.). A continuous latent has no object a checker can consume. | **No — put the learnable parameters in the *functional form* instead.** A KAN moves the learned degrees of freedom onto per-edge univariate basis functions, so the transition law is a sum of individually plottable curves; symbolic regression on one such activation returned `0.995u(1.003 − u)` against a truth of `r·u(1−u)`, `r = 1`, giving a closed form a human or a solver can check with the state left continuous and no quantisation anywhere (Koenig et al. 2024, [[wiki/entities/kan-ode.md]]). The symbolic step is *optional postprocessing*, so unlike SINDy it does not restrict what can be fitted. | Decides whether the discretisation problem (G27) is a prerequisite for the reification problem (G8/G11) or an independent one. Under A a builder must solve carving before it can get rules; under B a continuous learner can be symbolised in place, and G27 is only needed for the *state vocabulary*, not for the transition law. | `LIVE`, and B's evidence is thin in a specific way: the symbolised module was `[1,1,5]` — one node learning one scalar residual inside an otherwise hand-written PDE — so the readability plausibly came from the hand-made factorisation rather than from the KAN, and no result shows extraction surviving a composed multivariate `Φ`. B also never re-integrated the extracted equation to confirm it reproduces the model it replaced, which is the check that would make this a claim about the model rather than about the ground truth |
| T148 | **Should a discovered action alphabet be discrete or continuous?** | **Discrete.** The wiki's standing position everywhere the vocabulary question comes up: a finite symbol set is what makes planners, PDDL rules and formal verification applicable ([[wiki/concepts/affordance-grounded-symbols.md]] — DeepSym's binary bottleneck, LatPlan's discrete state autoencoder), what T147 position A demands for inspectability, and what Genie-style VQ codebooks assume for playability. A discrete edge label is also the only kind [[wiki/concepts/latent-graph-discovery.md]] is literally written over. | **Continuous, with discretisation as optional read-out.** A VQ-8 codebook reimplemented under identical conditions transfers barely better than *no action condition at all* — LIBERO FVD 1504.5 against 1545.2 action-agnostic and 767.0 continuous — while continuity is what makes the two operations that matter possible: averaging 100 encoded samples to initialise a new action, and interpolating two codes to compose one. Discrete controls are recovered afterwards by clustering the continuous codes, in whatever number is wanted (Gao et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/adaworld.md]]). | Gap **G4**: whether the induced edge-label alphabet is a codebook or a vector space, and therefore whether composition over it is symbolic or arithmetic. It also decides whether G27's discretisation problem must be solved for *edges* as well as nodes, or only for nodes. | `LIVE`, and the evidence is internally split: the same VQ-8 variant that collapses on cross-context transfer is second-best and within 1% of the continuous model on unseen-environment fidelity (Habitat 23.31 vs 23.58 PSNR). The natural reading — 8 codes suffice to *index* a small discrete action space after finetuning but cannot *carry* an arbitrary demonstrated transition — is a hypothesis about codebook size that nobody has swept | **A third design point, from the opposite direction:** [[wiki/entities/gcq.md]] uses a codebook that is discrete *and metrically ordered* — codewords are bumps on a torus, so `e_i + a` is another codeword and differences between codes are actions. That dissolves the dichotomy the row is written over: lookup and composition-by-arithmetic are both available, because the discreteness is a sampling of a continuum rather than an unstructured symbol set. The cost is that the metric is installed rather than discovered, so it buys the composition property by assuming the domain's topology (G47). **A fourth reading says the axis is wrong.** [[wiki/entities/neo-neural-theorizer.md]] runs both design points as baselines under one protocol that holds out *compositions* rather than scenes, and **both score 0.000** — discrete VQ and continuous β-VAE alike, with test-time gradient refinement of the continuous code not helping. What transfers (0.933) is a *sequence* of discrete codes executed by a shared operator. On that evidence the load-bearing axis is **monolithic vs compositional**, not discrete vs continuous, and this row has been comparing two ways of building the same failure. It also settles the codebook-size hypothesis flagged above, in the compositional case and against the intuition: a 6× over-complete codebook does *not* degrade into one code per observed composition — it still allocates primitive-level codes, and scores slightly better. What keeps position B alive is that NEO's domains are synthetic and its encoder is frozen, so nothing yet shows a discrete compositional alphabet surviving the 2000M-frame regime where the continuous code won.
| T149 | **Must a vision encoder be trained with language supervision to align well with a language model?** | **Yes — the standing assumption of the whole multimodal-LLM stack.** Essentially every encoder used for video question answering (CLIP, SigLIP, Perception Encoder) is pretrained on image–caption pairs, chosen explicitly for the semantic alignment that supervision buys; the field treats language-supervised features as the ones an LLM's input embedding space can absorb. | **No.** In a controlled comparison — same Qwen2-7B backbone, same 18M alignment samples, frozen encoder, only the encoder swapped — a video encoder pretrained with *no* language supervision at all averages 52.3 against Perception Encoder's 49.1, SigLIP2's 48.1 and DINOv2's 45.7, winning every benchmark but PerceptionTest, with the largest margins on the temporal ones (MVP 31.1 vs 26.7, TemporalBench 33.3 vs 27.5). Scaled to 88.5M alignment samples it is state of the art at the 8B class on five benchmarks (Assran et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/v-jepa-2.md]]). | Whether language is a *source* of conceptual structure or only a *readout* of it — the same question [[wiki/concepts/compositionality.md]] asks about whether syntax supplies the binding format (T8). If a purely self-supervised visual objective produces features an LLM can consume, then alignment is a projection problem solved at fine-tuning time, and the case for language-supervised pretraining is about data efficiency rather than about representation. Limits: one architecture, alignment data held fixed but pretraining data not, and the losses (PerceptionTest, TVBench, MVBench) cluster on the *appearance*-biased benchmarks, which is exactly where this encoder is independently weakest. **A second, independent B-side data point, on a quantity rather than a benchmark** (Oquab et al. 2023, [[wiki/entities/dinov2.md]]): a 300M self-supervised iBOT beats a 1.8B language-supervised OpenCLIP ViT-G on all three monocular-depth benchmarks (NYUd linear RMSE 0.417 vs 0.541), and DINOv2 ViT-g reaches 0.344 — with the qualitative failure being OpenCLIP omitting objects entirely from its depth map. Depth is precisely the content [[wiki/concepts/cross-modal-grounding.md]]'s rate argument says a 77-token caption never carries, so the two sides may not disagree about supervision at all: language supervision looks like a *filter* on which quantities survive, winning where captions are informative (SUN397, Stanford Cars, ImageNet-R/Sketch) and losing where they are silent (depth, fine-grained species, temporal structure). | `LEANING` — toward B on temporal content, unsettled on appearance. **A third system suggests the question is mis-posed** (Chen et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/vl-jepa.md]]): it keeps a language-free video encoder *frozen* and puts language in as the space a downstream predictor predicts **into**, and beats the best language-supervised encoder on 8 zero-shot video classification sets (52.5 vs 44.7) and 8 retrieval sets (63.7 vs 58.1) at **26× fewer image–text pairs** (3.3B vs 86B). If the rate bound is a property of what the *encoder* was trained on, then language is free to be a target without ever being a bottleneck — and the appearance-side losses persist exactly as the frozen encoder predicts (K400 64.8 vs 76.4) |
| T150 | **Should a quantisation codebook be learned from data or installed by construction?** | **Learned.** The entire vector-quantisation lineage since VQ-VAE trains the codebook jointly with the encoder, on the argument that a codebook fitted to the data allocates its capacity where the data are; every discrete world model and tokeniser in the wiki inherits this, and [[wiki/entities/adaworld.md]] pushes it further by learning the action condition itself. Nothing in the wiki previously suggested a fixed codebook would be anything but a handicap accepted for interpretability. | **Installed.** [[wiki/entities/gcq.md]] fixes its codebook to the attractor states of a toroidal continuous attractor network and reports that making the same codewords learnable — adding the standard codebook loss `γ‖sg[s] − ŝ‖²`, everything else identical — makes the model *worse* on both metrics (FID_p 43.41 → 47.76, PSNR_p 27.77 → 24.48). The stated cause is structural rather than optimisational: scaffold codewords stand in dynamical relations to one another (`e_i + a` must remain a codeword), and a per-code gradient does not preserve them. Finite scalar quantisation (FSQ) is the independent precedent — a predefined codebook that matches VQ-VAE — and GCQ's static-setting check on ImageNet reports minimal degradation against VQ-VAE at sequence length 1. | Whether the discrete vocabulary a reasoning system runs over is something to be discovered or something to be supplied — G27's node set, G4's edge labels and G47's manifold are the same question at three levels. If B, the wiki's standing "learn the structure" programme is buying flexibility it does not need in exactly the places where the structure is a group. | `LIVE`. B's evidence is one ablation inside one architecture, on a codebook whose *whole point* is that its elements are related — it says nothing about unstructured codebooks, and it perturbs code content within a fixed topology while never testing a wrong topology (which is the case that would matter, [[wiki/empirical-tensions.md]] T77, G47). A's position has never been tested against a structured fixed codebook at scale. The decidable version: sweep codebook adaptability against domain–topology mismatch, and check whether learning helps exactly when the installed manifold is wrong. |
| T151 | **Where do a model's objects live — in its architecture, or in a read-out of its predictions?** | **In the architecture.** The object-centric world-model lineage routes information through a fixed number of competing latent slots with soft attention bottlenecks (MONet, Slot Attention, SAVi, C-SWM, FOCUS), trained on prediction or contrast; entities are a *factor of the latent*, which is what makes them available to a downstream reasoner as variables. [[wiki/concepts/compositionality.md]] and gap G21 both assume this shape | **In a read-out.** SpelkeNet has no object variable, no slot count `K` and no bottleneck: the network is an unconstrained 7B autoregressive flow model, and entities are recovered post hoc as clusters of the pairwise correlation between its counterfactual responses — which beats a supervised segmenter on point-prompted Spelke segmentation (AR 0.541 vs 0.482) on real internet imagery where the slot lineage is reported not to scale at all, since too many slots degenerate and too few make reconstruction ill-posed (Venkatesh et al. 2025) | Whether entity discovery is a training-time architectural commitment or an inference-time query. Position B costs `R × T` full rollouts per segment and delivers no variable a planner can bind to, so a downstream symbolic consumer would have to re-run the probe or distil it — and distilling deletes the query. Position A delivers variables and cannot get them from real scenes | `LIVE` |
| T152 | **Is a world model's conditioning interface a design requirement, or an implementation detail?** | **An implementation detail.** Every world model in the wiki is specified as architecture + objective + data (+ behaviour policy, after Zhang et al. 2026), and the conditioning path — text prompt, dense mask, action vector, goal embedding — is chosen for convenience. The evaluation follows: prediction quality, then planning success | **A requirement, and it gates which counterfactuals exist.** Diffusion's iterative *global* denoising has no place to put a one-pixel intervention, and text is a poor control surface for fine-grained spatial queries — so both are excluded from counterfactual probing however accurate they are. LRAS's `(pointer, content)` token pairing makes a localised intervention two appended tokens and arbitrary decoding order a posterior-exploration knob, which is the stated reason SpelkeNet is built on it rather than on a video diffusion model (Venkatesh et al. 2025) | Model selection for anything other than open-loop prediction. If B holds, a model's *query language* is a first-class property, an accurate model with a global-only interface answers no interventional question, and the wave's architectural comparisons (JEPA vs RSSM vs diffusion) are missing an axis | `LEANING` — toward B, on one demonstration: the interface argument is stated a priori and the diffusion alternative was never run as a baseline |
| T153 | **Is a straight latent trajectory a good representation or a partial collapse?** | **A good representation, and a target.** The temporal-straightening hypothesis (Hénaff et al.) holds that perceptual systems render temporally complex input as approximately straight paths precisely so that prediction becomes linear extrapolation; straightness measured on pretrained features discriminates real from generated video, and straighter latents have been reported to help planning. On this reading `S_straight = mean cos(v_t, v_{t+1})` is a property to regularise toward ([[wiki/concepts/population-geometry.md]]). | **A collapse of the axis nobody constrained.** In LeWM the anti-collapse term acts on the *per-timestep marginal* only, straightness rises monotonically over training with nothing asking for it, and the model that has an explicit temporal-smoothness regulariser ends up **less** straight. The authors name their own result *temporal collapse* — the encoder is free to make successive embeddings near-collinear because no term prices it (Maes et al. 2026, [[wiki/entities/lewm.md]]). | Whether the next JEPA-family objective should add a temporal regulariser or delete one. The limit settles the direction if not the set-point: a perfectly straight trajectory is one-dimensional and carries no information about the branching of futures, so the useful value is an interior optimum and nobody has located it or shown the effect is causal. | `LIVE` — no intervention run; the straightness/performance link is a correlation over one environment |
| T154 | **Does a control-usable visual world model need foundation-scale pretraining?** | **Yes, and freezing it is what makes JEPA training tractable.** DINO-WM and [[wiki/entities/v-jepa-2.md]] both avoid collapse by not training the encoder at all, and the physical-probing advantage tracks the corpus: DINOv2's ~124M images buy velocity and rotational quantities that the small end-to-end models do not recover. The wave's premise is that the encoder is the expensive part and should be amortised across tasks. | **No, at least below 3D visual complexity.** A 15M-parameter ViT-Tiny trained end-to-end from pixels on one GPU beats DINO-WM on Push-T *even when DINO-WM is given proprioception*, wins under matched FLOPs on both Push-T and OGBench-Cube, plans ~48× faster, and has lower seed variance (96.0 ± 2.83 vs 92.0 ± 1.63) — while losing on the visually richest 3D environment and on the *simplest* one (Maes et al. 2026, [[wiki/entities/lewm.md]]). **The classification-side version, and it is the cleanest evidence either column has** (Balestriero & LeCun 2025, [[wiki/entities/lejepa.md]]): a 21M ResNet-34 trained from random initialisation on Galaxy10's 11k images beats DINOv3 ViT-S/16 and DINOv2-S under frozen probing (78.2 / 71.4 / 67.6), under full finetuning (83.3 / 81.6 / 78.3) and at every shot count down to one sample per class, and beats a 630M I-JEPA pretrained on ImageNet-22k by 14 points — while *losing* to that same I-JEPA on flowers102, a 1020-image natural-image dataset. Only the encoder varies, so B generalises beyond control: transfer's advantage is a function of **distributional distance from the pretraining corpus**, not of corpus size, and it inverts once the target domain leaves it. | Where the wave should spend: on encoders reused across environments, or on per-environment models cheap enough to retrain. It also decides how to read every probing table in this literature, since the frozen-encoder systems differ from the end-to-end ones in corpus, parameter count and objective at once ([[wiki/concepts/representation-probing.md]]). **Side A's encoder now has its primary source, and it moves one term.** DINOv2's advantage over a same-size corpus is not size but *shaping*: 142M curated images beat 142M uncurated ones from the same crawl at matched compute by ~14 points on ImageNet-A and iNat2018, while the uncurated corpus wins on segmentation (Oquab et al. 2023, [[wiki/entities/dinov2.md]]). So "foundation-scale pretraining" is doing two things the comparison cannot separate — a large corpus and a curated distribution — and the curation targets were the evaluation benchmarks' own train splits. | `LIVE` — no controlled comparison exists; the two sides differ on architecture, data and scale simultaneously, and all LeWM results are in simulation |
| T155 | **Does a simplicity prior select structure, or destroy it?** | **Select.** The wiki's oldest and strongest inductive bias: weight hypotheses by `2^−l(π)` and the mixture dominates every computable alternative ([[wiki/concepts/universal-induction.md]]); [[wiki/concepts/prediction-compression-equivalence.md]] makes log-loss a measure of structure found; the two-level hierarchy is itself an MDL argument (`\|primitives\| + N·\|arrangement\|` beats `N·\|concept\|`, [[wiki/concepts/compositionality.md]]). Shorter is more compositional is the premise [[wiki/entities/neo-neural-theorizer.md]] states outright and builds on. | **Destroy, past a threshold, and the threshold is close.** The same system sweeps its simplicity weight and reports the reversal: `λ_MDL = 1.2` takes action primitiveness from **1.000 to 0.213** and compositional-OOD transfer from 0.933 to 0.160, with the code–primitive alignment matrix filling with *composite* codes. The mechanism is not subtle — one code for `Left-Down` is a shorter explanation than two codes, so a strong enough length penalty **buys brevity by entangling the vocabulary**, which is the precise opposite of what the bias was invoked for. `λ ∈ {0.8, 1.0}` tracks the ground-truth program length; 1.2 does not. | Gap **G26** in its operative form. If short-and-entangled and short-and-compositional are both minima of a length prior, then description length alone never picks the second, and what did the work in the successful runs is *length under enforced re-use* — one shared executor realising every symbol on every instance. That is a second term nobody is pricing, and nothing sets the balance except a sweep against ground truth the learner is not supposed to have. | `LIVE`. Evidence is one sweep of three values in one architecture on three synthetic domains, and `λ` interacts with codebook size (the over-complete run used `λ = 1.0`), so the band's width is unknown. The decidable version: hold the executor's sharing fixed and vary it — if compositional recovery survives strong length pressure only when the executor is shared across instances, then re-use, not brevity, is the bias worth naming |
| T156 | **When a compositional model fails out of distribution, is the deficit in the vocabulary or in the search over it?** | **The vocabulary.** Every representational reading in the wiki: a model that fails on unseen compositions lacks the parts, or holds them entangled, and the remedy is a better representation — the entire argument of [[wiki/concepts/compositionality.md]], [[wiki/concepts/program-induction.md]]'s cost 1, and the standing interpretation of a low benchmark score as evidence about what the model *has*. | **The search, and the two come apart by a factor of 20.** [[wiki/entities/neo-neural-theorizer.md]] scores its vocabulary independently of its task performance: at 6-step arithmetic programs, action primitiveness is **1.000** (every ground-truth primitive is reproducible by a single learned code) while transferability is **0.019–0.038**. Sampling 1024 programs from the same amortised proposer and majority-voting over those that reproduce the support pair recovers **0.696–0.707** — same weights, same codebook, ~180× compute. Nothing about the representation changed. | What a benchmark score licenses. Under B, "model X does not compose" is unfalsifiable without a separate coverage measurement, and a large fraction of the wiki's OOD-failure evidence is uninterpretable in the same way. It also relocates the test-time-compute lever: search pays **only** over a factored hypothesis space — the same paper's monolithic baselines are flat in sampling budget and its gradient-refined baseline reaches 0.994 self-fit with 0.000 transfer — so scaling inference is not a general remedy for an amortisation gap but a remedy for a *proposal* gap in a compositional model ([[wiki/concepts/amortized-inference.md]]). | `LIVE`, and the asymmetry is that only B has an instrument. Primitiveness needs a ground-truth primitive set to score against, which no real domain supplies, so outside synthetic benchmarks the two hypotheses are currently indistinguishable — the open instrument question is what plays the role of primitiveness when nobody knows the true vocabulary |
| T157 | **When a linear probe fails, is the representation non-linear or is the label basis wrong?** | **Non-linear.** The standard inference and the one the emergent-world-model literature made: a linear probe for Othello board squares {Black, White, Empty} saturates at ~75% across six layers while a 2-layer MLP probe reaches 98.7%, so the world model is encoded non-linearly and only a non-linear decoder can read it (Li et al. 2022) | **Neither — "linear" is a property of the pair (representation, feature vocabulary).** Relabelling the *same activations* relative to whoever moves — {Mine, Yours, Empty} — puts a **linear** probe at 99.6%, above the MLP at every layer; the MLP had been spending capacity re-deriving a parity transform. Corroborated at circuit level: first-layer heads specialise by timestep parity. And the point generalises — the source's own example is a physics model carrying `√distance`, where whether distance is "non-linearly represented" depends on whether `d²` is admitted as a feature (Nanda et al. 2023) | Decides what a null probe means, which is most of what an interpretability sweep produces. If B holds, every reported "non-linear representation" is unfalsified rather than established, and the missing procedure is a *search over label bases* — which no methodology in [[wiki/concepts/representation-probing.md]] contains. It also changes the practical advice: guess the frame the model's loss is invariant under (Othello legality is colour-symmetric), not the frame a human describing the domain would use | `LEANING B` for the Othello case, where B is decisive; `LIVE` in general, because B's own argument makes the question undecidable without a stated feature vocabulary, and nobody has a way to enumerate one ([[wiki/concepts/linear-representation-hypothesis.md]], [[wiki/entities/othellogpt.md]]) |
| T158 | **When a model is shown to use its world model, does it use it on every input?** | **Yes — that is what a causal intervention establishes.** Edit the probed board state and the model's legal-move predictions follow it, on 1,000 test cases and even on boards unreachable by legal play; the representation is therefore on the computational path from input to output (Li et al. 2022; Nanda et al. 2023) | **No — the intervention score is an average over cases, and at least two circuits are running.** In the same model, from roughly move 30 onward, the correct legal moves are increasingly often resolved at an *earlier layer* than the correct board state (`MoveFirst`), and board-probe accuracy degrades in end games — while the board intervention still works there (0.112 errors). Reading: late in the game most squares are filled and most empty squares are legal, so legality is predictable from cheap features (`Empty` ∧ local ones) with no board needed, and the cheap circuit finishes first | Decides what "emergent world model, causally verified" buys. If B holds, a passed intervention test licenses "the model *can* use it" and not "the model's answer came from it" — so a system can be certified to hold and use a latent graph and still route most queries around it, which is [[wiki/concepts/shortcut-learning.md]] surviving the strongest available internal test. It also makes per-input circuit attribution, not per-dataset probe accuracy, the quantity an architecture argument needs | `LEANING B` — B's evidence is internal to A's own model and A has no counter-measurement; what would settle it is ablating the candidate cheap features (`Is-Surrounded-By-Mine`, `Is-Border`) and showing end-game accuracy falls, which is named and not done ([[wiki/entities/othellogpt.md]], [[wiki/concepts/representation-probing.md]]) |
| T159 | **Is a geometric structure that appears only after dimensionality reduction a property of the representation or of the projection?** | **Of the representation.** PCA removes superposition interference and the prompt/format component that every activation carries; what survives is the feature subspace the network's own downstream reads select. The supporting evidence is causal: steering vectors built by projecting the fitted PCA directions *back* into the residual stream move the model's output on 74.3% of trials, so the subspace is at least partly read (Tehenan et al. 2025, [[wiki/concepts/linear-representation-hypothesis.md]]). | **Of the pair.** The same relation vectors compose additively at cos 0.993 / 6.0° inside the fitted 3-D subspace and at 0.395 / 66.7° in the raw residual stream they were extracted from; one inverse pair is antipodal projected (0.995) and near-*orthogonal* raw (0.113). Projection is linear, so it cannot create additivity among the projected vectors — it discards a remainder that is large and non-additive, and no measurement says whether that remainder is interference or content. A structure requiring a post-hoc 3-dimensional summary to become visible is a claim about the summary. | Every "linear / compositional / antipodal geometry" number in the emergent-world-model and steering literatures, most of which report the projected column only. It is T157 one step out: T157 says the *label basis* is a free parameter with more leverage than the decoder class; this says the *subspace* is another one, and unlike the label basis it is fitted from the data rather than guessed, which makes it look objective. Cheap partial resolution: report both columns always, and steer the composed direction (`v_above + v_right`) — if the composite steers as well as its parts, the subspace is the object; if it does not, the remainder was doing work. | `LIVE` |
| T160 | **Does the geometric quality of a representation predict whether the model uses it?** | **Yes — that is the working assumption of the whole find-a-direction-then-steer-it pipeline.** Clean antipodal structure, high probe accuracy and high variance-explained are what identify a direction as a feature; the Othello case is the existence proof, where the 99.6%-accurate direction is also the one that installs a counterfactual board at 0.10 errors ([[wiki/entities/othellogpt.md]]). | **No, and the ranking inverts.** In the same model, same site, same fitted subspace, same 100-trial protocol: `in front ↔ behind` is the *best*-aligned inverse pair at layer 24 (cos 0.9950) and steers at **5%**, while `above ↔ below` is the *worst* (0.9779) and steers at **100%**; `left` 100% vs `right` 79% splits a single axis. Geometry scores a fitted summary, steering scores a handle, and the two are different measurements (Tehenan et al. 2025). | Any interpretability pipeline that selects which features to intervene on by geometric quality — i.e. the default one. It also weakens aggregate steering rates as evidence: 74.3% overall is a mixture of three saturated relations and one that fails almost completely, so the aggregate describes no relation in the set. Competing explanations for the failure are untested and cheap to separate: multi-token surface form (the source's own), or an unstable reference frame on the only axis whose convention varies ([[wiki/concepts/distributed-reference-frames.md]]). | `LIVE` |
| T161 | **Does a stable signature failure profile identify an *installed* prior?** | **Yes — that is the identification criterion.** A core system is identified not by its content but by a quantitative failure profile held constant across ages, species, cultures and tasks: content can be mimicked by learning, a limit that does not move cannot. The wiki adopted this as G17's second instrument — certify a prior by a constant (capacity, Weber ratio, entry threshold) that survives scale, retraining and domain shift, plus a resource-stress probe (Spelke & Kinzler 2007, [[wiki/concepts/core-knowledge.md]]) | **No — a learned model with no prior reproduces the diagnostic.** V-JEPA has no installed abstraction of any kind and its intuitive-physics profile is sharp and *invariant*: object-intrinsic properties (permanence `g = 9.0`, continuity `g = 11.0`, shape `g = 8.1`) large, object-against-world properties (support, gravity, inertia) moderate, **object–object interaction (solidity, collision) and colour constancy at chance** — and the same partition holds across a 115M→ViT-H scaling range, three pretraining objectives, three corpora and a 100× change in unique-data volume, with its errors *correlated with human errors* in the occluded conditions. What actually pins the profile is architectural resource — a 3–4 s memory window and a framerate — which no scaling or retraining lever touches (Garrido et al. 2025, [[wiki/concepts/violation-of-expectation.md]]) | G17's second instrument, and every "architecture X has prior P" claim it was meant to adjudicate. If a capacity bottleneck produces the same invariance as an installed prior, constancy-under-perturbation is necessary and not sufficient, and the wiki's cheapest certification protocol certifies nothing on its own. It also cuts the other way, at biology: the same argument applies to infants, whose signature limits could likewise index a resource rather than a module | `LIVE` — and the discriminating experiment is one sweep, already runnable on the ingested model: vary the **resource** the failure could be attributed to (context length, framerate) while holding objective, data and scale fixed. If solidity and collision move, it was a bottleneck; if they do not, the profile survives as evidence of something structural. Nobody has run it on any model or, in the resource-parametric form, on any infant |
| T162 | **Does relational understanding require decoding back to the input space?** | **No — reconstruction is unnecessary and actively harmful.** The JEPA lineage's founding commitment: predict in representation space, never in pixels, because a decoder forces the model to spend capacity on unpredictable detail; the wiki carries this as the argument behind [[wiki/entities/h-jepa.md]], [[wiki/entities/v-jepa-2.md]] and [[wiki/entities/lewm.md]], and [[wiki/concepts/violation-of-expectation.md]] supplies its strongest evidence — at matched protocol a pixel-space predictor (VideoMAEv2) sits at the untrained null where a representation-space predictor scores 98% on IntPhys. **And it now has a controlled within-method measurement** ([[wiki/entities/i-jepa.md]]): identical architecture, masking and dataset, target computed by the EMA encoder versus target computed in pixels, with the pixel arm given 800 epochs against 500 — 66.9 vs **40.7** on ImageNet-1% linear evaluation. It is the only place in the wiki where the commitment is isolated from every co-varying design choice. | **Yes, on exactly the ability at issue.** Conditional generative models used as classifiers by Bayes' rule — `p_θ(c\|x) ∝ p(c)·p_θ(x\|c)`, no retraining — **far outperform** discriminative joint-embedding models like CLIP on Winoground, the benchmark built to test relation and word-order understanding; they also show more shape bias, better human alignment, and better effective robustness (Bordes et al. 2024, [[wiki/concepts/cross-modal-grounding.md]]). The two positions are not evaluated on the same axis: the JEPA evidence is physical plausibility over time, the generative evidence is compositional structure within a frame. **A third data point sits between them and favours B on a narrow axis:** on Clevr object *counting*, the pixel-reconstruction MAE beats the same-scale I-JEPA 90.5 to 86.7, while on depth the two tie at 72.4 — so what latent targets discard is not uniformly irrelevant, and the discarded material includes some the wiki would call structural. | Whether a reasoning architecture needs a decoder at all, or only for inspection. If B is right, the wiki's whole latent-prediction wave has an untested blind spot exactly where [[wiki/concepts/compositionality.md]] locates the deficit — the relation slot. | `LIVE` — and **half of the cheap test has now been run** (Chen et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/vl-jepa.md]]): a JEPA whose target is a *sentence embedding* beats CLIP, SigLIP2 and Perception Encoder on the text-only hard-negative triplets of SugarCrepe++ (63.9 vs 58.6) and VISLA (42.9 vs 40.4) from a 300M text encoder against their 537–708M, with the gain concentrated in the **swap** categories that perturb word order while holding the word bag fixed (Swap-Object 42.0 vs 13.5–30.6) and Replace-Relation at a tie. That is a non-generative model winning on the axis B owns — but it is a *text-only* protocol with no image in the loop, so it shows a better text encoder rather than better image↔relation binding, and Winoground and PUG (paired negative *image*) remain unrun by any JEPA. A generative classifier on IntPhys is still unrun. **(brainstorm)** A third reading dissolves it: what reconstruction supplies is not pixels but a *distortion term that prices arrangement* — moving two objects changes the pixels but barely changes a bag-of-objects code, and two arrangements of one scene are almost never each other's minibatch negative — in which case the repair is a paired negative *image*, not a decoder ([[wiki/concepts/energy-based-models.md]]). |
| T163 | **Can retrieval be amortised into a geometry, or is it irreducibly a joint computation?** | **Amortised.** Encode every stored structure once, offline, into a vector; answer a query by a coordinate comparison against the index. This is what makes schema retrieval tractable at all — median 95.5 AUROC at 0.03 s against 7.5–25.9 s for exact combinatorial search ([[wiki/concepts/subgraph-matching.md]]), and it is the shape of every content-addressed read in the wiki: attention over a cache, key–value memory, dense retrieval | **Irreducibly joint.** Factorising the compatibility as `⟨f(q), g(x)⟩` makes the score matrix rank-`d`, so realizing all `k`-subsets of `n` items with margin `γ` requires `d ≥ log C(n,k)/log(1+1/γ)` — and the empirical requirement is ~4.5× that even for freely optimised vectors with no encoder and no generalisation demand. On a task consisting of *who likes apples?* over 46 documents, every single-vector model from 32 to 4096 dimensions fails (best R@2 54.3, none solved at R@20) while a cross-encoder scoring query and document *together* gets 100% in one forward pass (Weller et al. 2026, [[wiki/concepts/retrieval-capacity.md]]) | G37's whole answer set, and the wiki's default assumption that a memory read is cheap.
