If B holds, the cheap geometric read is a *first-stage filter* with a ceiling fixed at architecture time, and any query that composes — *X and Y*, *X but not Z* — climbs `C(n,k)` faster than any affordable `d`. It also relocates the cost question: amortisation does not remove the computation, it caps which computations are expressible | `LIVE` — the two are compatible as a cascade (cheap index proposes, joint scorer disposes), which is what deployed retrieval already does and what no memory architecture in the wiki has. What is unresolved is the middle, and it now has two occupants rather than one: multi-vector late interaction beats single vectors by 20–60 recall points on the same task with no theory covering it, and [[wiki/entities/learningmatch.md]] keeps the factorised encoders while making only the *score* un-factorised — a 5-layer head over two cached embeddings, 20 µs per pair — which escapes the rank bound at `O(n)` cheap head calls instead of `O(n)` model calls. Nothing says which queries the first stage will silently drop |
| T164 | **Must a collapse-avoiding self-supervised learner descend a well-defined objective?** | **Yes — the anti-collapse provision is a term in the loss.** The wiki's whole treatment of gap G34 is a catalogue of terms: negatives (InfoNCE), variance + covariance (VICReg, Barlow Twins), distribution matching (SIGReg, [[wiki/entities/lewm.md]]). Each is certifiable — you can say what the objective's minimum is and check it is not collapsed — and the wiki's stated preference ordering between them is by *coefficient count*, which presupposes there is an objective to count coefficients in ([[wiki/concepts/energy-based-models.md]]). **And the position is weaker than the wiki has been stating it** (Bardes et al. 2022, [[wiki/entities/vicreg.md]]): VICReg's minimum is non-collapsed for *every* positive coefficient triple, and training collapses anyway at `λ,μ,ν = 1,1,1`, `10,1,1` and `1,10,1`, converging only on the ray `λ = μ ≳ 5, ν = 1`. A term certifies the *target*, not the trajectory — so A's advantage over B is that a designer can say what the system was supposed to reach, not that anyone can check it did. **Barlow Twins is this position at its cleanest and adds a fact that cuts at B directly**: its minimum is stated as a matrix identity (`C = I`), it needs one coefficient that is flat over a 10× range, and adding B's two components *on top of it* costs **10 points** (71.4 → 61.3; stop-gradient alone 70.5, predictor alone 70.2) — so the update-rule asymmetry is not a free extra safeguard but a competing design that pulls the dynamics off a minimum that already exists (Zbontar et al. 2021, [[wiki/entities/barlow-twins.md]]) **A's first measured payoff, and it arrives from the same lineage** (Balestriero & LeCun 2025, [[wiki/entities/lejepa.md]]): a certifiable loss whose *value* is monotone in downstream quality certifies the trajectory as well as the target — LeJEPA's training loss correlates with downstream linear-probe accuracy at `ρ_s ≈ 0.85` across architectures, datasets, learning rates and epoch budgets, and `≈0.99` under the rescaling `train_loss / λ^0.4`, giving label-free model selection where B's dynamical systems have no scalar whose value means anything. It also removes B's mechanism from the necessary list: with the embedding distribution constrained directly, the predictor whose removal collapses BYOL and I-JEPA is deletable at no cost and the EMA survives only as evaluation-time weight averaging. What A still cannot do is certify a read-out the loss does not measure (T168). | **No — an asymmetry in the update rule suffices, and then there is no objective at all.** BYOL's per-step loss is a regression whose exact global minimum *is* the collapsed constant encoder; it reaches 74.3% ImageNet linear-eval top-1 anyway, because the target branch is updated by an EMA rather than by a gradient. The authors hypothesise that **no** loss `L_{θ,ξ}` exists whose joint gradient descent is BYOL's dynamics, on the GAN analogy. Both components are necessary (removing either collapses at ~0.3%) and both are substitutable by a near-optimal predictor with no EMA at all (52.5% closed-form, 66.5% raised learning rate) — so what prevents collapse is a *rate ratio* between two networks, a quantity appearing in no objective (Grill et al. 2020, [[wiki/entities/byol.md]]) | Decides what an anti-collapse audit can check. Under A, checking the objective's minimum suffices; under B, a system can be off-collapse for reasons no inspection of the loss reveals, and every identifiability result the wiki relies on ([[wiki/concepts/objective-identifiability.md]], the `N(0,I)`-constrained recovery theorem) is a statement about a *global minimiser* that such a system has no reason to approach Home page for this row: [[wiki/concepts/representational-collapse.md]]. | `LIVE` — B is a demonstration and A is a research programme, so they do not directly conflict on facts, and B has since acquired a second instance in the lineage the wiki cares most about: an image JEPA runs the same zero-coefficient predictor + EMA skeleton to 81.1 ImageNet linear evaluation with the pairs supplied by masking rather than augmentation, so the uncertifiable mechanism is what the whole JEPA family is actually built on ([[wiki/entities/i-jepa.md]]). What is live is the *preference*: B is cheaper (zero anti-collapse coefficients) and A is certifiable, and no source in the wiki has measured what the certifiability buys. The discriminating experiment is cheap and unrun: take one JEPA and train it under both provisions on the same data, then check whether the objective-based one behaves as the recovery theorem predicts on a policy with vanishing conditional action excitation (`ρ_tr → 0`) where the dynamical one has no prediction at all. **B's "rate ratio" now has a literal dial and an interior optimum** (Chen et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/vl-jepa.md]]): with a genuinely separate target network in another modality, a scalar learning-rate multiplier on the target branch is swept, and both endpoints lose — full speed −3.6 classification, **frozen −7.3** — with the optimum at 0.05–0.10. The two regimes the JEPA lineage actually ships (jointly trained/EMA, and frozen) are the two ends of a continuum nobody has been tuning |
| T165 | **Is augmentation fragility a consequence of the *contrastive* objective?** | **Yes — the negatives author the shortcut.** Crops of one image share a colour histogram and histograms differ across images, so a discrimination task on crop-only augmentations is solved by histogram and the representation is never charged for more; SimCLR loses **27.6** points on that ablation where BYOL, whose target is a regression onto a richer representation rather than a discrimination, loses **13.1** and still reaches 59.4%. The wiki carries this as the generalisable statement that *an objective's shortcut is fixed by its noise distribution* (Grill et al. 2020, [[wiki/entities/byol.md]], [[wiki/concepts/shortcut-learning.md]]) | **No — a negative-free learner is just as fragile.** Barlow Twins has no negatives, no noise distribution and no discrimination anywhere in its loss, and degrades on the same progressive-removal sweep **like SimCLR** (≈ −25 points at crop-only, Fig. 3) rather than like BYOL. The authors read the same fact as a *feature*: the learned invariances are "better controlled by the specific set of distortions used", where BYOL's are "generic and intriguingly independent" of them (Zbontar et al. 2021, [[wiki/entities/barlow-twins.md]]) | Decides what the augmentation set is. Under A it is a shortcut-design surface specific to contrastive methods, and the repair is to drop the negatives. Under B the operative variable is whether the objective's *only* source of signal is the distortion — true of both SimCLR and Barlow Twins, false of BYOL, whose EMA target carries features no augmentation demanded — so augmentation design is unavoidable for anything whose loss is a function of two views alone, and BYOL is the outlier needing the explanation. It also strains [[wiki/concepts/divergence-objectives.md]]'s rate–distortion split: all three share `f(X)` and therefore the rate, so that framing predicts equal degradation and does not get it. **And the premise that augmentation design is unavoidable is false**: an image JEPA takes its two views from *masking one image* and needs no distortions at all, reaching 81.1 ImageNet linear evaluation ([[wiki/entities/i-jepa.md]]) — which does not settle A vs B but moves the design surface, since that method's mask sampler is then worth 34 points and is the same kind of unavoidable choice under a different name (T167) | `LIVE` — the three curves are from two papers at matched protocol (BYOL's Fig. 3b, reused by Zbontar et al.), so the facts are not in dispute; the mechanism is. The discriminating experiment is one run: Barlow Twins with an EMA target on one branch under crop-only. If it recovers toward −13 the operative variable is the target's richness (B); if it stays at −25 the operative variable is the loss family (A) |
| T166 | **Are anti-collapse mechanisms from different families composable?** | **No — they are alternative whole designs.** Adding BYOL's predictor and stop-gradient to Barlow Twins costs **10 points** (71.4 → 61.3; either alone ≈ −1), and the reverse control agrees — BYOL with Barlow Twins' 8192-wide projector drops 74.1 → 72.3. The reading the wiki adopted: an update-rule asymmetry inside a system that already has a well-defined minimum drags the dynamics off it, so a designer picks one design rather than layering safeguards (Zbontar et al. 2021, [[wiki/entities/barlow-twins.md]]) | **Yes, and the graft is mildly positive.** VICReg — same family, same off-diagonal penalty — takes a predictor and stop-gradient *neutrally* (73.2 → 73.2 at 1000 epochs; 68.6 → 67.4 at 100), and its **variance term added to BYOL buys +0.9** (69.3 → 70.2) and to SimSiam +0.2, by raising a representation-level standard deviation that BYOL's tricks leave under-spread. The two mechanisms drive the *same two statistics* — BYOL's embeddings already sit at `1/√d` on the sphere and its representations decorrelate with no term asking them to — so they are two implementations of one target, not rivals (Bardes et al. 2022, [[wiki/entities/vicreg.md]]) | Decides whether the JEPA lineage's inherited EMA + stop-gradient can be *supplemented* with a certifiable term (cheap: keeps the working system, adds an auditable target) or must be *replaced* (expensive: [[wiki/entities/lewm.md]]'s whole programme). Under B the wiki's certifiability preference costs almost nothing; under A it costs a redesign Home page for this row: [[wiki/concepts/representational-collapse.md]]. | `LEANING B, with the conflict localised` — the two papers differ in one component: Barlow Twins standardises the embeddings along the batch *inside the loss* and VICReg does not, replacing it with an explicit variance hinge. **(brainstorm)** Batch standardisation is a second implicit cross-branch coupling, and stacking it with an EMA/stop-gradient is what costs 10 points — a three-way interaction rather than a two-family incompatibility. The discriminating experiment is one run: Barlow Twins with cross-*covariance* (no standardisation) plus predictor and stop-gradient. Independently, both papers agree the **covariance** term is the piece that does not compose ("optimization with SG and CR is hard": BYOL 70.2 → 69.5, SimSiam 68.1 → 67.6) **A third data point, from a different pair of loci** (Akbar 2026, [[wiki/entities/lejepa.md]] §Part 8): Muon's orthogonalised *updates* and SIGReg's constrained *representation* stack cleanly on a collapsing ViT — 20.73 (AdamW) → 62.44 (Muon alone) → **74.56** (Muon + Weak SIGReg) — and SIGReg is additive again on top of the hand-tuned architecture heuristics (75.87 → 76.98). So the failures of composition in this row are both *update-rule into loss-with-a-minimum*, and provisions acting on genuinely different objects appear to compose. |
| T167 | **Is a self-supervised representation's character set by its objective or by its pair sampler?** | **By the objective.** Everything the wiki carries on gap G34 is a catalogue of *losses* — negatives, variance + covariance, distribution matching, EMA asymmetry — and the whole comparison literature ranks methods by which term they use, with a spread of a few points on ImageNet linear evaluation across the entire family. The pair-construction step is reported as an implementation detail: SimCLR's augmentation list, iBOT's mask ratio, V-JEPA's tubelet mask are stated and not ablated. | **By the pair sampler, by a wide margin.** Holding the loss, the architecture, the dataset and the epoch budget fixed and varying only which sub-images become context and target, an image JEPA moves **15.5 → 54.2** on ImageNet-1% linear evaluation — a factor of 3.5, where the entire anti-collapse literature spans a few points (Assran et al. 2023, [[wiki/entities/i-jepa.md]]). Two of the losing settings are the masked-image-modelling defaults. The mechanism is two opposite failures: targets predictable by local texture continuation make the objective cheap without content, and a context uninformative about its target makes the conditional mean nearly constant. | Where a designer spends effort, and what an emergence claim is a claim *about*. If B holds, the wiki's coefficient-count preference ordering over anti-collapse families ranks the smaller lever, and every "objective X produces representation Y" statement needs the pair distribution reported alongside — an eighth audit item for [[wiki/concepts/objective-identifiability.md]]. It also reopens G32 from the self-supervised side: the mask sampler *is* the experience stream, and nothing designs it. Home page for this row: [[wiki/concepts/representational-collapse.md]]. | `LEANING B` — one method, one modality, one metric, so the magnitude may not transfer; but the comparison is internally controlled in a way no cross-method loss comparison in the wiki is. The counter-consideration is that the winning setting was itself found by search, so B currently says the lever is large, not that anyone knows how to set it. |
| T168 | **Is collapse-avoidance a property a design *has*, or a state a training run must be *kept in*?** | **A property, certified once from the objective.** The wiki's entire treatment of gap G34 catalogues *provisions* — negatives, variance + covariance, distribution matching, an EMA asymmetry, teacher-output normalisation — and ranks them by coefficient count and by whether the loss's minimum can be stated and checked non-collapsed (T164). Nothing in that framing is time-dependent: a design either carries a provision or it does not, and the check is made against the objective, not against a checkpoint. | **A state, and the certification does not cover the read-outs the objective is silent about.** DINOv3's ViT-7B trains 1M iterations on one stationary distribution with `L_DINO + L_iBOT + 0.1·L_KoLeo`: no term diverges, no encoder goes constant, patch norms stay stable (the registers work), and the ImageNet linear probe rises **monotonically throughout** — while a linear probe on the *patch* tokens peaks at ~200k iterations and ends **below its own early value**, visible as a steadily rising CLS↔patch cosine similarity. The masked-patch term that DINOv2's ablation showed *buys* dense structure is present and being minimised the whole time and does not *hold* it, because it constrains each patch and not the relations among them. The same decline appears in the ViT-g and, worse, in an independent 7B DINO scaling effort (Siméoni et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/dinov3.md]]). **And a second, non-temporal counterexample to the same certification** ([[wiki/entities/hit-jepa.md]]): in a three-level trajectory JEPA carrying SmoothL1 in representation space *plus* VICReg variance and covariance terms at every level, changing only the channel between levels — upsampled embeddings concatenated downward instead of upsampled attention matrices — degrades retrieval by a factor of **465**, with the objective, the coefficients, the branches and the data untouched. The rank scales linearly with database size, so the true match sits at a fixed ~0.5 percentile: coarse neighbourhood structure survives, within-neighbourhood ordering does not. What the objective certifies is each branch's embedding; what collapsed is a representation *assembled across* branches, which no term scores (Li et al. 2025). **The monitor B implies now exists, and it has not met the failure** (Balestriero & LeCun 2025, [[wiki/entities/lejepa.md]]): a JEPA whose training-loss value is `ρ_s ≈ 0.85`–`0.99` correlated with downstream accuracy across architectures, datasets and budgets is exactly the label-free per-run signal B's monitoring requirement needs — but the correlation is measured against **global** linear probes, which is the read-out that rose monotonically through DINOv3's dense collapse, and its longest run is 100 ImageNet-1k epochs against DINOv3's 1M iterations. So B's requirement has an instrument and no evidence the instrument sees the failure that motivated it. | Whether an anti-collapse audit is a design review or a monitoring requirement, and what a training run's **stopping rule** is. If B holds: (i) certification must be stated *per read-out*, and the field's default read-out is the one that says train longer; (ii) training horizon becomes a design variable with no coefficient in any objective, alongside I-JEPA's pair sampler (T167) and now the inter-level wiring of a stack (HiT-JEPA) — three of the largest levers in the family are outside the loss; (iii) the fix generalises as a *shape* — a term on the **relations** between elements, anchored to the model's own earlier iterate, which repairs 800k iterations of erosion in 10k and is the wiki's nearest object to a trainable `g`-objective (G30, [[wiki/concepts/abstract-structural-codes.md]]). Home page for this row: [[wiki/concepts/representational-collapse.md]]. | `LEANING B` — the two supporting results share no model family, modality, objective or failure mode, which is what makes A's "certify once from the loss" untenable in general; but each is thin on its own. DINOv3: one model family, one modality, and the degradation is reported as training curves on two benchmarks rather than as a controlled ablation (`w_Gram` is never swept). But the observation is internally controlled — same run, same data, same loss, two probes moving in opposite directions — and it reproduces across two model sizes and one independent scaling effort, which is more support than most rows here carry. A also survives in its narrow reading: nothing *collapsed* in the sense the taxonomy defines, which is exactly the point — the taxonomy's predicate is too coarse to see this. **And the strongest case for B no longer needs a self-supervised objective to suspect** (Akbar 2026, [[wiki/entities/lejepa.md]] §Part 8): a ViT minimising plain supervised cross-entropy — an objective with *no* constant-encoder solution, since the label term prices it — collapses to 20.73% under AdamW with aggressive augmentation, and a covariance-isotropy term restores it to 72.02%. If a run can degenerate under an objective whose minimum is certifiably non-collapsed, collapse-avoidance cannot be a property read off the loss at all. `(tentative)` — collapse is inferred from accuracy, with no spectrum measurement reported. |
| T169 | **Is the isotropic Gaussian the right target for an embedding distribution, or only the right *shape* at a dimension nobody knows how to choose?** | **The right target, and it is proved rather than chosen.** At fixed total variance, an anisotropic embedding always admits a downstream task with higher ridge-probe bias and has strictly higher OLS estimator variance; and among all distributions under a scalar covariance constraint the isotropic Gaussian **uniquely** minimises the integrated squared bias of radius-k-NN and Nadaraya–Watson kernel probes. Isotropy from the linear analysis, Gaussianity from the nonlinear one. Enforcing it removes the predictor, the EMA teacher and register tokens, holds across ~50 architectures in 8 families and to 1.8B parameters, and yields a training loss that ranks checkpoints without labels (Balestriero & LeCun 2025, [[wiki/entities/lejepa.md]]) | **Only the shape. The target's *dimension* `K` is a free parameter with a measured cost the theorem is silent about.** The same regulariser in a world model loses to both baselines on the wiki's simplest environment — Two-Room, 2D navigation — because the data's intrinsic dimension is far below the 192 the isotropic Gaussian is asked to fill, and its performance band is bounded on both ends (collapse below ≈184, saturation above) with neither bound predicted by anything (Maes et al. 2026, [[wiki/entities/lewm.md]]). The price is charged even where `K` is *not* mismatched: re-running VICReg with the embedding `ℓ₂`-normalised — which fixes each dimension's variance the way `N(0,I)` does — costs **3.5 points** on ImageNet (Bardes et al. 2022, [[wiki/entities/vicreg.md]]). And the theorem's premise is the escape hatch: it is worst-case over *unknown* tasks, so any known structure in the downstream family makes an anisotropy strictly better by the same algebra | Whether distribution matching is a general anti-collapse provision or one that must be sized per dataset. Under A a practitioner sets `λ` and stops; under B `K` joins the tuning surface the family's whole selling point was eliminating, and the coefficient-count argument for it over VICReg (G34) partly unwinds. It also decides how to read every biological anisotropy the wiki records: if isotropy is optimal only when the read-out is unknown, a power-law cortical spectrum is evidence about which structures read the population ([[wiki/concepts/population-geometry.md]]) Home page for this row: [[wiki/concepts/representational-collapse.md]]. | `LIVE` — the two sides are not in contradiction so much as in different scopes, and no one has run the experiment that would join them: sweep `K` against a dataset's estimated intrinsic dimension under a fixed LeJEPA recipe and see whether the loss↔accuracy correlation that makes `λ` self-tuning also picks `K`. If it does, B dissolves into the same instrument; if it does not, the derivation covers less of the design than its framing suggests |
| T170 | **When several targets are equally valid, should the predictor represent the modes or should the target space quotient them away?** | **Represent them.** [[wiki/entities/h-jepa.md]] introduces the latent variable `z` for exactly this purpose — the world is multi-modal, a deterministic predictor must average, and averaging is what makes a predictor useless for planning; [[wiki/entities/v-jepa-2.md]] lists "no latent variable, so no multi-modality" as a first-class architectural limitation of its own system, and the pixel-generative branch (Cosmos, [[wiki/entities/adaworld.md]]) buys multi-modality with diffusion. | **Quotient them away, in the *target encoder*.** For the same input there are usually several correct answers, and in one-hot token space they are nearly orthogonal (no shared tokens), so a generative model must fit several disjoint high-density regions; a trained text encoder maps them to nearby points, turning the task into one coherent mode in a continuous space. The claim is that most target multi-modality is **surface variability** (word choice, style, paraphrase) rather than genuine alternatives, and that the right place to remove it is the space, not a latent. Measured at matched encoder, data, batch and iterations: 14.8 vs 7.1 CIDEr and 41.0 vs 27.2 top-5 against a token-generative baseline with **twice** the trainable parameters (Chen et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/vl-jepa.md]]). | Whether a JEPA predictor needs a stochastic latent at all. Under A the multi-modality is handled downstream of the encoder and costs sampling machinery at planning time; under B it is handled *upstream*, by choosing a target encoder whose equivalence classes match the ones the task should ignore — which relocates the decision from the architecture into a component nobody currently derives (VL-JEPA's own ablation (e) shows swapping the Y-Encoder moves read-outs by up to 14 points, so the amount of quotienting is a free parameter). | `LIVE` — **and the two may be about different multi-modality.** *"The lamp is off"* / *"the room goes dark"* are one event under two descriptions; *"the person went through the left door"* / *"…the right door"* are two events. B's evidence is entirely from the first kind, and nothing in the source shows the Y-Encoder separates them. **(brainstorm)** The testable version: measure the Y-Encoder's distance between paraphrases and between genuine alternatives on the same prompt — if the ratio is near 1 the model is averaging real futures and its own metric cannot see it |
| T171 | **Does climbing the moment ladder pay?** | **Yes — pin the whole distribution.** [[wiki/entities/lejepa.md]] §5.2 shows the anti-collapse loss families are one ladder indexed by how many moments of `N(0,I)` the test constrains, and argues for the top rung on **identifiability**: finitely many moments do not determine a distribution (Carleman), so a family of non-Gaussian solutions satisfies a second-moment objective exactly and the encoder may sit anywhere in it — "a phenomenon already observed in VICReg". The Epps–Pulley characteristic-function test leaves no such family, at the same coefficient count and `O(N)` cost (Balestriero & LeCun 2025). | **Not measurably.** Head to head on CIFAR-100, a sketched **second-moment** test `‖Cov(ZS^⊤) − I‖_F` matches or beats the full Epps–Pulley test in 5 of 8 settings — +1.8 on the collapsed ViT, **+6.2** on the vanilla 6-layer MLP, +4.4 on ViT+Muon — and its three losses are all on already-stabilised baselines and ≤1.1 points. Whatever the extra moments buy, it is not stability and not accuracy in these runs (Akbar 2026, [[wiki/entities/lejepa.md]] §Part 8). | Whether the wiki's recommended anti-collapse provision is the characteristic-function test or [[wiki/entities/vicreg.md]]'s covariance term with a random sketch bolted on — a real cost difference (17-knot quadrature and complex exponentials vs one Frobenius norm) and a real conceptual one, since only the top rung makes the trained object *identifiable*. Home page for this row: [[wiki/concepts/representational-collapse.md]]. | `LIVE` — B is one dataset, one author, no seed variance, no measurement of the spectrum either test is supposedly fixing, and its setting is **supervised**, where a label term already removes the degenerate solutions identifiability is meant to exclude. A's argument is a theorem about the solution *set*; B's evidence is about the *trajectory*, so they may not be about the same thing (cf. T164, T168) |
| T172 | **Is an action-grounded vocabulary necessarily agent-relative?** | **Yes, by construction.** [[wiki/concepts/affordance-grounded-symbols.md]] defines a symbol as an equivalence class under *this* agent's repertoire — `b(x₁) = b(x₂) ⟺ ∀a ∈ A: effect(x₁,a) ≈ effect(x₂,a)` — so the same stone is one symbol to an agent that can lift it and another to one that cannot, and a distinction no action in `A` makes can never be represented. The *Umwelt* claim in a training objective | **No, in practice.** One interface fitted above **22 robot types** and reported to beat single-embodiment training on each (Open X-Embodiment, 1.4M episodes); one shared discrete codebook above manipulators, bimanual arms, navigation and quadrupeds with only per-body decoders (UniAct, CrossFormer); optical flow and point tracks used as embodiment-agnostic intermediates; and latent action alphabets extracted from *human* video and used to pretrain robot policies (LAPA → GR00T N1) — all as reported in Kawaharazuka et al. 2025, [[wiki/concepts/cross-embodiment-transfer.md]] | Whether the wiki's answers to G4 and G27 must be re-derived per body or pretrained once across many. If B, an agent's vocabulary is not bounded by its own repertoire and human data is directly usable; if A, every cross-embodiment interface is exporting only the perception-and-intent level and the field is measuring data scale, not body invariance | `LIVE` — and B's own literature concedes the weak form: a body-specific decoder survives in every design, no method uniformly handles manipulators, mobile bases and legs, and nothing reports which distinctions a shared interface *cannot* make for a member body |
| T173 | **Should a learned similarity live in the geometry of the embedding, or in a comparator on top of it?** | **In the geometry.** Encode each item once, compare by a fixed form, and the read-out is free. [[wiki/entities/neuromatch.md]] ablates this directly: the order-embedding hinge `‖max{0, z_q − z_u}‖²` beats an MLP comparator, a neural tensor network and box embeddings on accuracy *and* runs 10× faster (0.03 s vs 0.44 s at query size ≤ 50) — "isolates the geometry as the source of both accuracy and speed". [[wiki/entities/lejepa.md]] gives the normative version: at fixed variance the isotropic Gaussian uniquely minimises linear-, k-NN- and kernel-probe risk, so the prescription is to shape the *space* until the simplest probe is optimal. Every JEPA, VICReg and BYOL read-out in the wiki is a fixed cosine or `ℓ₂` | **In the comparator.** [[wiki/entities/learningmatch.md]] sweeps both and finds the opposite: encoder depth "did not have a significant impact on the accuracy", comparator depth did, up to a knee at 5×1024. The head is also given the pair's **raw mean**, not only its difference — because the target match varies drastically with mass in the low-chirp-mass region and negligibly in the high-mass one, and *every fixed score form in the wiki (inner product, cosine, `ℓ₂`, order hinge) is translation-invariant in the embedding*, so none can express a similarity whose scale depends on where the pair sits. [[wiki/concepts/retrieval-capacity.md]]'s cross-encoder result is the same sign: 100% on a task no single-vector model solves at any dimension | Where the parameters of every memory read in the wiki should go, and what a cheap index can be trusted to answer. Under A a store is built once and queried by coordinate test, and G37's retrieval slot is closed; under B the read is `O(n)` head evaluations however good the encoder, and the rank-`d` bound is escaped by moving expressivity into the comparator rather than by raising `d` | `LIVE` — the candidate discriminator is the *algebraic structure of the target relation*, and it is untested: subgraph containment is transitive and reflexive, which is exactly what an order embedding encodes exactly and for free, while a metric with varying curvature has no such property for a geometry to exploit. Nobody has run both read-out families on both relation types, and LearningMatch's own evidence is a depth sweep with no seed variance on one domain `(tentative)`. Distinct from T163, which asks whether the *score* may be factorised at all; this asks what form it should take once it is |
| T174 | **Does the recurrent connectivity of a fixed-dynamics sequence model carry expressive power?** | **Yes — a random fixed reservoir is "the antithesis of the optimal", and growing the right graph is what pays.** [[wiki/entities/hag-reservoir.md]] starts from `W = 0` and grows edges by long-window correlation; the grown reservoirs beat every static and locally-tuned baseline across five classification benchmarks, drive mean pairwise correlation from 0.994 to 0.476 on the hardest task, and lift effective dimensionality from 1.0 to 13.0. The stated premise of the whole plasticity-in-reservoirs literature (IP, Oja, Anti-Oja, BCM) is that *which units connect to which* is a lever worth pulling. | **No — not within the fading-memory class.** Li, Fong & Tiňo 2024 prove constructively that a **single ring with one shared weight `λ` and `±1` (or `±1, ±i`) input signs** approximates any linear reservoir system, hence any time-invariant fading-memory filter over uniformly bounded inputs, to arbitrary `ε` ([[wiki/entities/simple-cycle-reservoir.md]], Thm 22). Three visibly different constrained families — C-SCR, SMCR, Twin SCR — are *all* universal with one tunable recurrent parameter each. Any topology reachable is reachable from the ring, paid for in state dimension `n' ≈ (N+1)·n·k`, `k > nm`. | Every argument in the wiki of the form "this recurrent topology is the right one because it can express X". If B, that argument is empty for every `X` inside the fading-memory class, and architecture must be defended on conditioning, dimension-at-fixed-`ε`, sample efficiency or class membership instead — which are exactly the quantities neither paper measures. **The two are not yet in formal contradiction, for three reasons.** (i) A's dynamics are `tanh`-nonlinear, B's theorems are for linear state updates only. (ii) B is existential in `n'` with no lower bound and an admittedly loose `k > nm`, so "expressively equal" is compatible with "wildly different at the `n ≈ 500` A actually runs". (iii) A's best variant reaches `ρ(W) = 2.89`, i.e. **outside** the contractive class B's theorem covers — so A's largest gains may come from leaving the class rather than from rewiring within it. The discriminating experiment: construct B's C-SCR approximant of a fitted reservoir and compare it to A's grown network at matched `n` and matched `ρ`. Unrun. **Update (Gu, Goel & Ré 2022, [[wiki/entities/s4.md]]) — the missing quantity now has a name and a measurement, and it is neither topology nor width.** S4 is B's object exactly (linear contractive state update, all nonlinearity outside the recurrence) and it reports that *which* matrix, at fixed topology and fixed `n`, is worth **more than 15 points of validation accuracy** and the difference between 50% and 96.35% on a 16,384-step task — while every variant reaches 100% training accuracy. So B's "topology is free" and A's "connectivity is a lever" are both compatible with the decisive variable being the **spectrum of `A` and its conditioning**: the matrices that work (HiPPO) are maximally *non-normal*, with a diagonaliser whose entries reach `2^{4N/3}`, i.e. exactly the ones B's unitary-dilation route conjugates away. Neither A nor B measures conditioning; S4's Lemma 3.2 is the wiki's only number on it. | `LIVE` |
| T175 | **Once every candidate is a universal approximator, is there any expressivity difference left to measure?** | **No — not by any measure that survives the class.** Li, Fong & Tiňo 2024 make three visibly different constrained architectures universal *simultaneously* over time-invariant fading-memory filters, each with one tunable recurrent parameter, and the constructions are explicit ([[wiki/entities/simple-cycle-reservoir.md]], Thms 15/20/21/22). What is left to argue about is sample efficiency, conditioning, the state dimension needed at a given `ε`, and whether the target is inside the class at all — none of which is expressivity. | **Yes, and the field already knows universality is the wrong instrument.** Hasani et al. 2021 prove LTC universality (Thm 3) and then write that the theorem "does not yield a concrete measure on where the separation is between different neural network architectures", replacing it with **trajectory length** — the arc length of the PCA-projected hidden trajectory under a circular input — and lower-bounding its growth separately for Neural ODEs, CT-RNNs and LTCs (Thms 4–5). The measure does separate them, by orders of magnitude in places (hard-tanh, width 200: `138 / 121 / 5.4×10⁴`), and it is sensitive to width, weight variance, activation and solver steps ([[wiki/entities/ltc.md]]). | Every architectural argument in the wiki. If A, the recurrent-structure lever is a no-op inside a stated class and design effort belongs elsewhere (G29). If B, universality is merely a low bar and finer geometric measures are the right tool — which is what most of the wiki's architecture pages implicitly assume without ever naming a measure. | `LIVE` — and B's instrument has a defect that A does not: LTC's ODE is **stiff**, so a variable-step solver spends up to **133× more integration steps** on it than on a Neural ODE (81.01 vs 0.61 for hard-tanh), while trajectory length is *exponential in step count* for every family. A large fraction of B's separation is bought compute, and the compute-matched comparison is never run. The resolution is cheap and nobody has done it: fix `L` for all families with a fixed-step solver and re-measure. Until then the wiki has **no per-FLOP expressivity measure at all**, which is the actual gap both positions expose. **Update (Hasani et al. 2022, [[wiki/entities/cfc.md]]):** the same group then removed the solver entirely — a CfC evaluates `t` explicitly and costs what a discrete RNN costs — so the `L`-free member of the family now exists and the compute-matched measurement needs no new architecture, only the measure run on it. It has not been run; what *is* known is that the solver-free model matches or beats the stiff one on task accuracy, which is what one would expect if the trajectory-length gap was bought rather than architectural. **A third source declines the question entirely, and that is itself evidence for A**: Gu, Goel & Ré 2022 ([[wiki/entities/s4.md]]) make *no* expressivity claim — no universality theorem, no geometric measure — and instead report an algorithm (`Õ(N+L)` via normal-plus-low-rank + Woodbury + Cauchy kernels) plus benchmarks, then ablate and find the result is carried by an **initialisation**. If the strongest long-range sequence model in the wiki was obtained without any expressivity argument being made, the instrument A says is unavailable was also not needed. |
| T176 | **Should a continuous-time model keep its differential equation, or compile it away?** | **Keep it.** The ODE *is* the semantics, and three properties follow from it and from nothing else: it is invertible under uniqueness conditions, so it can be run backwards in time — the basis of continuous normalizing flows; it has a vector field that can be read as an equation ([[wiki/entities/kan-ode.md]] recovers a PDE source term to 0.5% by symbolic regression on it); and it is a differential operator of the bilinear DCM form that neuroimaging fits to infer effective connectivity ([[wiki/concepts/causal-model-building.md]]), which is the stated motivation for [[wiki/entities/ltc.md]]'s equation. Its state-boundedness theorems are proved *about the ODE's compartments*. Implicit ODE/PDE models also remain the right choice for continuously defined physics and control. | **Compile it away.** [[wiki/entities/cfc.md]] replaces the one remaining integral with a closed form and gets 1–5 orders of magnitude in training/inference (8,752% over Latent-ODE-ODE on human activity; 160–220× on PhysioNet; >150-fold accuracy-per-compute-time) with no accuracy loss, SOTA 98.09% on event-based sequential MNIST and +18% over transformers on Walker2D. Approximation error from a `p`-th order integrator stops existing as a concept, the hidden hyperparameter that is the solver disappears, and the pathology ODE-RNNs show on irregularly sampled data — which they fail *for every solver* — is fixed by a gate `σ(−f·t)`, showing it was never an integration problem. | Whether "continuous-time" in this wiki names a *computational commitment* or a *derivational origin*. Under B, every continuous-time architecture here should be compiled to closed form and the ODE retained only as the thing the closed form was derived from — which costs the wiki its one readable transition function and its one causal-inference-shaped architecture. Under A, the 1–5 orders of magnitude are the price of keeping properties that no benchmark in either paper measures. | `LIVE` — **and the two positions have never been evaluated on a task that needs A's properties.** Every experiment cited by B is time-series regression or classification, where invertibility, symbolic readability and interventional causal identification are all irrelevant; B's own authors concede all three losses in their limitations section rather than contesting them. The discriminating experiment is a task requiring backward-in-time rollout or an intervention on a fitted connectivity matrix, run on an LTC and its compiled CfC at matched accuracy. Note also that B's compilation is doubly approximate — Lemma 1's bound is *sharp*, i.e. worst-case relative error is total (saved only by a shared `e^{−w_τ t}` envelope), and Algorithm 1's per-synapse superposition of a scalar, self-connection-free theorem across an arbitrary graph is not bounded at all. |
| T177 | **Does long-range capability come from the architecture or from the initialisation?** | **Architecture.** The wiki's default and the premise of nearly every entity page here: memory horizon is a structural property. [[wiki/entities/ltc.md]] builds an input-dependent rate into the ODE and lower-bounds trajectory length per architecture family; [[wiki/entities/hag-reservoir.md]] grows the recurrent graph and beats every static baseline; [[wiki/entities/simple-cycle-reservoir.md]] proves what a topology can and cannot express; [[wiki/architectural-gaps.md]] G16 puts the heaviest design lever in the architecture slot. | **The initialisation, and it is a generalisation effect rather than a capacity one.** Gu, Goel & Ré 2022 hold architecture, learning rule and objective fixed and vary only the *initial value* of the state matrix `A` on sequential CIFAR-10 at ≤100K parameters: random Gaussian, random diagonal Gaussian, random normal-plus-low-rank, and HiPPO. **With `A` trained, every arm reaches perfect training accuracy and validation accuracies remain separated by over 15%.** Random NPLR — S4's own parameterisation minus HiPPO — "still do not perform well", and the authors state the effectiveness "primarily comes from the HiPPO initialization, not the NPLR parameterization". The predecessor LSSL reports the same swap as 60% → 98% on sequential MNIST ([[wiki/entities/s4.md]]). | Everything. If B, then (i) architectural arguments about recurrent structure are arguments about a *starting point* that gradient descent neither recovers nor destroys, (ii) the [[wiki/concepts/three-component-framework.md]]'s three slots do not contain the decisive variable, and (iii) the right object of study is the prior on `A` — which for HiPPO is a projection of input history onto an orthogonal polynomial basis, i.e. a **choice of what to compress the past into**, made before any data arrive. It also reframes G29: architecture search would be searching the wrong space. | `LIVE` — the two positions are not symmetric and the asymmetry is the interesting part. A's evidence is *comparative across architectures*; B's is *within one architecture, across inits*, and no source in the wiki has run both axes at once. The discriminating experiment is cheap and unrun: HiPPO-initialise a CT-RNN, an LTC and a grown reservoir at matched `n`, and see whether the architectural separations A reports survive. If they collapse, B is general; if they persist, the two effects are additive and the wiki needs both. A second caveat on B: the ablation is one task (sequential CIFAR-10) at one scale (100K parameters), with the supporting figures dropped in extraction, so the >15% figure is held from prose. **Update (Karami et al. 2025, [[wiki/entities/ms-ssm.md]]) — B is replicated on a second architecture and the variable is refined.** Holding the MS-SSM architecture, learning rule and objective fixed and changing only the *distribution the initial eigenvalues of `A` are drawn from* — Mamba's initialisation vs a scale-stratified draw `diag(A^s) ~ U(−N(S+1−s), −N(S−s)]` that assigns each band a disjoint interval — moves ListOps **57.49 → 63.04** and PTB-XL AUROC **0.928 → 0.939**. Two things follow. (i) B is no longer a single-paper, single-task result: two independent groups, two architectures, two initialisation families, same conclusion. (ii) The object in the fourth slot is not "an initialisation" but a **measure**: HiPPO is one point, and what MS-SSM varies is the *spread* of the draw rather than its identity. The companion number makes the point without any initialisation argument at all — at fixed total state, redistributing it across bands is worth 25.0 ListOps points where doubling the state is worth 4.1 (Mamba 2× state 42.14 vs MS-SSM 63.04 vs Mamba 38.02). So the residual A-position is narrower than it was: capacity is measurably *not* the lever, and what is left for architecture to explain is whatever the wavelet front end contributes beyond the stratified spectrum — which MS-SSM's Table 5 claims is the largest single contribution and reports in a dropped image, so it is unquantified. |
| T178 | **Should a multiscale temporal decomposition be imposed on a sequence model, or left for a deep stack to discover?** | **Discover it.** [[wiki/entities/s4.md]] puts one initialisation interval on one linear time-invariant recurrence, stacks the layer, and the decomposition appears anyway: first-layer kernels cover a few steps of local context while last-layer kernels span all 16,384 positions at varying spatial frequencies, with no hierarchy anywhere in the architecture. That stack is state of the art on all six Long Range Arena tasks and the only model above chance on Path-X. Nothing was designed in, and the same weights re-tune to a new temporal resolution from one scalar `Δ`. | **Impose it.** [[wiki/entities/ms-ssm.md]] installs the hierarchy inside a *single* layer — a learned undecimated wavelet cascade into `S+2` bands, one SSM per band, eigenvalues initialised into disjoint intervals, an input-dependent mixer — at matched parameters and matched total state, and gains **3.4 points on ListOps** over S4 (63.04 vs 59.60) plus the best sCIFAR, ImageNet-1K and PTB-XL numbers in its tables. Its own ablation attributes the largest share of the gain to the multi-resolution convolution rather than to the recurrence. | How a builder spends depth. If A, multiscale structure is free at sufficient depth and a front end is redundant machinery; if B, depth discovers it *inefficiently* and one layer of imposed structure substitutes for many layers of search. The split in the evidence is the interesting part and neither paper names it: **the imposed hierarchy wins where the task's structure is explicitly nested** (ListOps, pixel-sequence sCIFAR, ECG rhythm-vs-form) **and loses where it is flat and long** (Retrieval, Path-X — MS-SSM's headline S6 variant averages 86.73 on the five non-ListOps LRA tasks against S4's 91.38, S5's 92.52 and Mega's 93.22). | `LIVE` — and the discriminating experiment is cheaper than either paper's, because the two mechanisms MS-SSM ships are separable and it never separates them: run plain S4 with a **band-stratified** `A` and *no* wavelet front end. If it recovers the ListOps gain, the front end is redundant and the whole effect is T177's, i.e. an initialisation. If it does not, imposed band-limiting is doing work that no initialisation can do, and A is refuted for nested tasks. A second confound to clear first: MS-SSM's LRA baselines are quoted from a third source (Qin et al. 2024) and its two variants disagree by 5.2 points in opposite directions across the two task families. |
| T179 | **Is short path length between positions why long-range dependencies become learnable?** | **Yes — it is the founding argument for attention.** [[wiki/entities/transformer.md]] Table 1 makes the whole case on the third column: self-attention connects any two positions in `O(1)` sequential hops where recurrence needs `O(n)` and convolution `O(log_k n)`, on the cited premise (Hochreiter et al. 2001) that the shorter the forward and backward signal paths, the easier the dependency is to learn. Parallelism and the `n < d` cost regime are presented as secondary. The architecture then took machine translation, structured parsing at 40K examples, and the decade. | **No.** [[wiki/entities/s4.md]] has `O(L)`-deep information flow through a contractive linear recurrence and a kernel that cannot depend on content, and it carries a 16,384-step dependency at 96.35% (Path-X) where all 11 Transformer variants — every one of them with `O(1)` path length — are at chance, winning every Long Range Arena task by >20 average points. Its own ablation attributes the result to the **initialisation** of `A` (T177), a variable path length does not name. Conversely the `O(1)`-path model fails at 16K. | Which property to buy when the two are separable. If A, the design target is hop count and quadratic cost is the honest price; if B, the target is the spectrum of the state operator and attention's `O(n²)` buys something else entirely — a read whose *target* is chosen by the current content, which is what an LTI kernel cannot express and what the founding paper never argues for. The wiki's four-cell brainstorm (short paths ± content-dependent reads) exists because 2017 bundled them against RNNs, where they arrive together. | `LEANING` toward B on the stated claim: short paths are demonstrably neither necessary (S4) nor sufficient (all Transformer variants at chance on Path-X) for a 16,384-step dependency. What is *not* in question is the second, unargued property — no attention-free model in the wiki performs a content-addressed read (Vaswani et al. 2017; Gu et al. 2022) |
| T180 | **Does verifier-driven test-time search buy reasoning, or only selection?** | **Selection.** The generator's coverage is already there and the verifier merely picks: o1 on AIME 2024 reads 74% greedy → 83% majority@64 → 93% best-of-1000 with a learned scorer, DeepSeek-Prover-V2 on miniF2F reads ~60% pass@1 → 88.9% pass@8192, and the wiki's own [[wiki/entities/neo-neural-theorizer.md]] goes 0.019–0.038 → ~0.70 at 1024 samples with a *complete* primitive set in hand. On this reading pass@1 measures the policy and everything above it measures the selector, so a reported gain can be bought entirely with inference budget (Raiyan et al. 2026). | **Reasoning.** Reinforcement learning from verifiable rewards distils the search back into the policy: the same o1 goes from GPT-4o's 12% to **74% at greedy decoding**, and DeepSeek-R1 elicits self-correction in an open-weights base model from reward signal alone with no supervised traces — so the verifier is training-time scaffolding whose product is a better single forward pass, not a permanent inference-time appendage. | Whether a brain-inspired reasoner needs a rejector *at run time* or only during learning — i.e. whether [[wiki/concepts/external-verification.md]]'s ladder is an architecture or a curriculum. Also whether the wiki may read a best-of-`k` number as evidence about a representation at all ([[wiki/empirical-tensions.md]] T156 is the same question asked of NEO). | `LIVE`. The decisive experiment is not in the survey: train with a verifier, then measure the resulting policy alone against a matched policy trained without one, at a *fixed* sample budget. Every reported comparison varies the budget and the training regime together. |
| T181 | **Is a more accurate verifier always a better verifier?** | **Yes — accuracy is the binding constraint.** The whole ladder is ordered by it: process reward models beat outcome models because they localise the error, kernel-checked proofs beat both because they cannot be wrong, and rule-based graders are indicted precisely for inaccuracy (up to 38% of responses flagged incorrect were correct, denying the model informative gradient). | **No — accuracy and exploitability are separate axes.** The survey reports that higher-accuracy *learned* verifiers are sometimes **more** susceptible to hacking during training, because a richer signal is a richer surface for the policy to Goodhart against; the three conditions a verifier must meet (accurate, adversarially resistant, informative on partial progress) are stated as independent, and a kernel's advantage is that it meets all three rather than that it scores highest. | What to spend on when a verifier must be built: a better scorer, or an *independence* property. It also decides whether the wiki's internal-verifier candidates ([[wiki/concepts/simulation-based-planning.md]], [[wiki/concepts/precision-weighting.md]]) can be improved into the role or are excluded from it by construction, since both are computed by the model they check. | `LEANING` toward B, on the grounds that the one verifier meeting all three conditions is the one that is not learned — but the survey's hacking claim is reported without a controlled sweep of verifier accuracy against hacking rate, so the coefficient is unknown. |
| T182 | **Does compression build the library or destroy it?** | **Builds it.** Library learning abstracts program components *shared across previously solved tasks* into new primitives, seeking the most explanatory and compact theory of a domain — a heuristic hierarchical Bayesian inference of the prior itself, reported to dramatically expand the repertoire of learnable concepts at fixed compute (DreamCoder, Ellis et al. 2021; LAPS adds language as a second channel on the same prior, Wong et al. 2022; via Goodman, Gerstenberg & Tenenbaum 2024) | **Destroys it.** The wiki's only direct measurement of compression pressure on a learned vocabulary runs the other way: sweeping the MDL weight in `λ_MDL^k · ℓ(y, ŷ_k)` from a working band to `λ = 1.2` drives primitiveness from **1.000 to 0.213**, because one code for `Left-Down` is a cheaper explanation than two codes. Shorter descriptions are bought with *entangled* composites ([[wiki/entities/neo-neural-theorizer.md]], T155) | Whether gap G26 — a *learned* prior over programs — can be obtained by compression at all, which is the only concrete proposal anyone has made for it. The candidate discriminating variable: **across-task shared-subcomponent abstraction** (A, which can only propose a primitive that recurs in several solutions) versus **within-instance description length** (B, which is free to fuse two primitives that always co-occur in *one* solution). If that is the whole difference, both results stand and the rule is that compression must be scored over a corpus, never over an instance | `LIVE` — no source runs both regimes on one domain; A is second-hand here and reports no primitiveness-style metric at all |
| T183 | **Is theory of mind recursive inference, or a fixed point?** | **Recursive inference.** Because `query` is definable inside the stochastic λ-calculus, a planning query nests inside a goal-inference query with well-defined semantics — inference about inference, arbitrarily deep, and the same construction covers an agent reasoning about another agent's beliefs about a third (Goodman, Gerstenberg & Tenenbaum 2024; Stuhlmüller & Goodman 2014). Being *definable* is the whole argument: no separate mechanism is needed | **A fixed point, or not recursive at all.** Two coupled spacetime attractors, each taking the other's inferred plan as its reward input, relax to a mutual best response with no explicit posterior anywhere ([[wiki/entities/spacetime-attractor.md]]). And the wiki's one *measured* inverse-planning model scores 96.0–99.7% on the Baby Intuitions Benchmark at recursion depth **one**, using conjugacy, a 5-point rationality grid and A\* — no nesting, and the tractability comes precisely from refusing it ([[wiki/entities/hbtom.md]]) | Whether nesting depth is a free architectural parameter or an artefact of a formalism that never has to pay for it. **The cost is now measured, and the answer is neither side's** ([[wiki/entities/autotom.md]], Zhang et al. 2025): recursion is run to **order 4** by replacing the nested posterior with a *single sampled world per level* — draw one state from `b(s)` at level `l` to stand in for level `l−1`, recurse to 0, then run ordinary inverse planning there. That makes depth linear rather than multiplicative, and the price is visible in accuracy rather than in compute: 95.0 → 75.0 → 70.0 → 67.5 → 55.0 across orders 0–4, at ~12K tokens per question against ~6.5K at depth 1. So A's recursion is runnable, B's tractability-by-refusal is not forced — and the amortisation objection lands exactly as stated, since what is computed at depth 4 is a one-sample estimate of the nested query and not the query | `LIVE` — but the cost question inside it is settled: sampled nesting is cheap and lossy, and nobody has run *exact* nesting past depth 1 |
| T184 | **Should structural similarity live in the code or in the comparator?** | **In the comparator.** Keep item vectors a plain description and make the *comparison* do the structural work: SME searches for a consistent one-to-one mapping, [[wiki/entities/neuromatch.md]] embeds graphs so containment is a coordinate order and scores anchored neighbourhoods with a violation energy, and [[wiki/concepts/retrieval-capacity.md]]'s theorem says a plain dot product over `d`-dimensional keys can address only `(1+1/γ)^d` retrieval sets, so structure-sensitive queries *must* escape the bilinear form (cross-encoder, MaxSim, learned head) | **In the code.** Structural properties can be *pre-computed into the vectors* until they are surface features a dot product can read. Contextualized HRRs — each filler blended with the typical fillers of the other roles it occupies — reproduce the full MAC/FAC ordering `LS > AN^cm > AN > SS > FA` in 100/100 runs from a single dot product at `n`=2048, with no alignment search anywhere, and the salience of role alignment re-tunable by re-weighting the probe alone (Plate, NIPS 7) | Decides where retrieval cost is paid, and whether a precomputable index can be structure-sensitive at all. The two positions are not contradictory but they price differently: the comparator route un-factorises the score and forfeits `O(1)` per-item retrieval; the code route keeps the index and pays in **dot-product variance** instead — ordering violations fall 6/100 → 1/100 when `n` goes 2048 → 4096, i.e. the bound is paid in signal-to-noise, not evaded. Neither side has a certificate, and the code route has a hard failure the comparator route does not: two entities filling the same role set are indistinguishable | `LIVE` |
| T185 | **Does structural similarity influence memory *access*, or only evaluation after access?** | **Access is surface-driven.** Gentner & Forbus 1991 ([[wiki/entities/macfac.md]], now in the wiki as a primary source) and Gentner, Rattermann & Forbus 1993 find little effect of analogical similarity on reminding — the 1991 numbers are LS .56, SF .53, AN .12, FOR .09 with rated soundness running the *other* way (AN, LS > SF, FOR) and the dissociation holding **within subject**, i.e. people judge the matches that came to mind most easily to be unsound; the 1991 paper is careful that the finding is not that higher-order relations fail to contribute (one of three studies found a small significant benefit) but that their effect on retrieval is far smaller than on mapping; this is the premise of the two-stage MAC/FAC architecture, whose first stage inspects only object features and relations and is *designed* to be structure-blind so it scales to a whole memory | **Access is partly structure-sensitive.** Wharton et al. 1994 find an effect; Thagard et al. 1990 and the 1993 study agree the effect exists but appears only, or is greater, in the presence of surface similarity. Plate's contribution is that the debate need not be settled architecturally — a structure-sensitive first stage costs exactly one dot product, so structure-blindness at access is not forced by tractability | Decides whether a reasoning model's retrieval stage should pay for structure at all. If access is genuinely surface-driven in humans, the wiki's schema-retrieval mechanisms ([[wiki/concepts/subgraph-matching.md]], G37) are modelling the *second* stage and have no biological first stage to match; if it is structure-sensitive, the cheap-first-stage assumption that motivates order embeddings and every amortised matcher here is a tractability artefact rather than a design constraint | `LIVE` — the psychological evidence is mixed and all of it predates the mechanism that makes the question cheap to test. **Two 1995 additions narrow the question without settling it** (Forbus, Gentner & Law, [[wiki/entities/macfac.md]]). (i) *Both* kinds of content are required in the cheap statistic: sweeping the entire design space with relations dropped from the content vector leaves no viable setting anywhere, so structure-blindness at access cannot mean structure-*free* — the argument is about relational **arrangement**, not relational vocabulary. (ii) Structure-sensitivity at access is real but is a property of the *encoding*, not of the matcher: relational retrieval rises with expertise and intensive encoding (T199), which position A absorbs without amendment. What A concedes is the direction of the remaining disagreement — Wharton et al. 1994's competition effect, where competition between memory items heightens structural similarity's influence, is an inter-item effect this architecture cannot express at all, because each item is scored independently |
| T186 | **To make role structure readable by a dot product, do you need the *content* of the neighbouring roles or only their *labels*?** | **Content.** Plate's contextualization replaces each filler by a blend of the filler and the **typical fillers** of the other roles that same object occupies, obtained by *unbinding* (`typ^R_r = R ⊛ r†`) — the whole point being that "which other roles an object fills" is invisible to comparison and must be computed and folded in. It is what takes AN off its tie with FA and yields `LS > AN^cm > AN > SS > FA` in 100/100 runs (Plate, NIPS 7, [[wiki/concepts/vector-symbolic-binding.md]]) | **Labels suffice.** The re-representation `C*_x = C_x^lower ∨ ⋁_r C_r` superposes the *role code-vectors themselves* — no fillers, no unbinding, no content — and that is enough to map all five similarity types of the same probe set correctly, including the cross-mapped and first-order-relations cases that the plain codes fail, plus three classic complex analogies (Slipchenko & Rachkovskij 2009, [[wiki/concepts/analogical-mapping.md]]) | Decides the cost and the operator requirements of every structural index the wiki might build: a label-set context is one OR over already-held vectors, a typical-filler context needs an inverse and a pass over the episode per role. The two are not measured against each other — different tasks (ranking vs correspondence), different codes (dense real vs sparse binary) — and B's own stated hole is exactly what A's extra content would fix: a *set* of role labels cannot distinguish two episodes with identical relations in different arrangements. | `LIVE` |
| T187 | **Must a binding operator be invertible?** | **Yes — the decode direction is the more valuable half.** The wiki's reading of HRRs is that `†` is what makes a bound structure *readable* rather than merely comparable: "who was the agent?" is `P ⊛ role†` plus an attractor cleanup, and that is the property that would make one vector-symbolic store serve as a fast `M` supporting both similarity ranking and slot-level query. Contextualization itself is built out of unbinding ([[wiki/concepts/vector-symbolic-binding.md]]) | **No, for everything analogy actually needs.** Context-dependent thinning is `⟨Z⟩ = Z ∧ ⋁_k Π_k(Z)` — lossy, with no inverse — and it supports retrieval, mapping *and* inference by similarity alone: candidate inferences are produced by copying base **code-vectors** with substitution and validated by comparing them to the target episode, so nothing is ever decoded. Its non-invertibility is the same property that makes it work: because thinning keeps a subset of the constituents' bits, a composite stays similar to its parts and nested structural similarity propagates upward for free — which convolution's randomizing property destroys (Slipchenko & Rachkovskij 2009, [[wiki/concepts/analogical-mapping.md]]) | Whether a brain-inspired fast store must support slot-level interrogation or only comparison. If B, the cleanup-memory requirement ([[wiki/entities/hopfield-network.md]]) disappears and the substrate can be sparse binary; if A, everything downstream of retrieval — reading off *which* object filled a role, instantiating a schema with named arguments, answering a query about a stored episode — needs the inverse, and B's systems can only ever return a ranking. | `LIVE` |
| T188 | **Must an induced program be consistent with *all* the demonstrations?** | **Yes — consistency with the full data is the criterion.** Every inducer in the wiki scores a hypothesis by its fit to the whole training set: the posterior `p(π\|D) ∝ p(D\|π)·p(π)` is over the complete `D` ([[wiki/concepts/program-induction.md]]), ARC's own scoring assumes the ~3 demonstration pairs share one program ([[wiki/entities/arc-agi.md]]), and length is used only to break ties *among* fully consistent programs | **No — require ≥2, and stop.** [[wiki/entities/ilp-arc-synthesizer.md]] constrains ILP to clauses unifying across two or more training examples and deliberately not all, on the evidence of ARC task `0a938d79`: two pairs demand vertical lines, two horizontal, and the program covering all four is strictly more complex than the one covering the first two — which solves the test. Full coverage bought complexity the test did not need | Decides what the demonstration set *is*. Under A it is one constraint system and every example is evidence about the same program; under B it is a set of possibly-heterogeneous cases and part of the induction is choosing which of them to explain — a model-selection problem nobody in the wiki has posed. It also changes what a length prior ranks: over-general-but-short and specific-but-long are only comparable once the coverage requirement is fixed | `LIVE, and A now has a price too` — [[wiki/entities/arc-vsa-solver.md]] reports demonstration and query performance separately for a solver that enforces A in full (its action set must hit every output object of every demonstration): **48.8%** of ARC-AGI-1-Train tasks get a program reproducing every demonstration exactly, **10.8%** solve the query; on Eval, 33.5% → 3.0%. So full consistency with all demonstrations is passed four to eleven times more often than it is right — it is a weak filter, not the criterion A takes it for, and B's objection is no longer only about complexity but about how little the requirement discriminates. B's instance remains unpriced: B's system offers no rule for *which* two examples; the horizontal pair yields an equally valid, equally short program that fails the same test, so the reported success is a 50% draw on a 4-example task and gets worse as the example count grows. What survives regardless is the observation itself: **a fully consistent program can be more complex than the test instance requires**, which no simplicity prior over fully-consistent programs can express, and which is the same complaint as G26 arriving from the data side rather than the prior side |
| T189 | **Should a model be selected for how confidently it answers, or for how well it answers?** | **Confidence, and it is the only signal available.** [[wiki/entities/autotom.md]] grows an agent model by `U(M,q) = −H(P(q\|X^{ts:t})) − α\|M\|`, stopping when the query posterior drops below one bit — no label is consulted anywhere, which is what lets the search run at inference time on an unseen question. It works: 82.43 average over five theory-of-mind benchmarks, and the ablations show the criterion is doing the work rather than the scaffold (a fixed POMDP loses 9.8 points, the initial proposal alone loses 4.9, all-timesteps loses 4.5 while doubling the tokens). The paper's own worked example is the mechanism running correctly — adding a goal node moves a 0.5/0.5 belief posterior to 0.1/0.9 *on the correct hypothesis* | **Confidence is what an insufficient model maximises.** Nothing in `U` distinguishes "the model now explains the evidence" from "the model no longer represents the thing that would create doubt": omitting the belief node in a false-belief story yields a sharp and wrong posterior, and the authors name exactly this as their failure mode ("model adjustments may sometimes fail to recognize the relevance of certain mental variables, resulting in an insufficient model"). The wiki's other confidence-as-objective is [[wiki/concepts/precision-weighting.md]] / [[wiki/concepts/expected-free-energy.md]], where an entropy term is defended only because it sits *beside* a likelihood term that ties the model to observations — `U` has no such term. And the confidence being minimised is an entropy over LLM-invented conditionals that are never calibrated, so the quantity is a report of the backend's certainty, not of the model's | Every unsupervised structure-selection criterion: this is the wiki's only candidate that can run per-query at inference time (G26, G29), and if it is only a confidence maximiser then structure discovery without labels has no objective at all. The discriminating experiment is cheap and unrun: plot final model utility against correctness on the ~17% of questions AutoToM gets wrong, and sweep `α` and `U_min`, neither of which has a reported sensitivity analysis | `LIVE` — A is 100% of the reported evidence and B is the authors' own stated limitation; nobody has separated them |
| T190 | **Is cross-example consistency a symbolic intersection, or a vote over hypothesis labels?** | **Intersection.** `Π* = ⋂_i Π_i`, where a program is retained for example `i` only if `render(π(S(I_i))) = O_i` — deterministic, generator-independent, and the explicit reason the source gives for preferring its architecture to LLM solvers that "approximate consistency via stochastic voting rather than enforcing deterministic symbolic agreement" ([[wiki/entities/corethink-compositional-reasoner.md]], stated method; the same criterion is run for real by [[wiki/entities/ilp-arc-synthesizer.md]], whose clauses do execute) | **A vote.** The same source's deployed pipeline never executes a program: pattern detection is repeated 5× per example at temperature 0, patterns are ranked by **detection count**, the top-3 are forwarded to the solver as a natural-language hint, and the composition operator `∘` is defined and never invoked. The authors concede it — "operationally, we approximate it by repeating structured pattern detection, retaining recurrent Unit Patterns … and forwarding only the resulting consensus hints" | Decides which rung of [[wiki/concepts/external-verification.md]]'s ladder the wiki's best ARC-AGI-2 result actually stands on, and therefore what its +6.9-point hint ablation is evidence *for*: symbolic execution, or a strong structural prior delivered as text. It also decides whether the system is an instance of [[wiki/concepts/program-induction.md]] at all | `LEANING B` — B is the source's own account of what runs, and A appears only as the "idealized consistency objective". Note the ablation survives either reading, and under B it becomes a *sharper* result: voting over hypotheses beats voting over answers by 2.2× at additive rather than multiplicative cost, which is a claim about where to spend a vote, not about symbols |
| T191 | **Should a solver commit to one parse of the input, or carry several?** | **Commit — but choose which, by a computed score.** [[wiki/entities/arc-vsa-solver.md]] holds six object hypotheses, ranks them by one number (mean over output objects of the max softmax of their similarities to candidate input objects — penalising both too many objects and ambiguous correspondence), and tries them in order, keeping the first whose implied action set is cheap enough. One parse is live at a time; the alternatives are never composed. The argument is cognitive and computational at once: the parse space is smaller than the action space, so search it first, and System 1 proposes a parse before System 2 examines any transformation | **Do not commit.** [[wiki/entities/ilp-arc-synthesizer.md]] represents a grid as a list of *possibly-overlapping* objects — a rectangle is also lines is also points — and carries all readings forward until the relation that explains the transformation selects its own endpoints; the parse is chosen after the fact by downstream explanatory success. [[wiki/entities/corethink-compositional-reasoner.md]] takes the third position by not asking: one fixed 8-connected BFS parse, with a frozen LLM patching ambiguous cases | Decides where the object-hypothesis cost is paid and what a wrong parse costs. Under A a parse error is fatal and unrecoverable (the ranking is computed on demonstrations and cannot be checked against the query at all), but each candidate is cheap and the criterion is inspectable; under B nothing is ever wrong but the hypothesis space is multiplied by the parse ambiguity and typing is doing the pruning. It is G27 in its practical form: the wiki has been asking where a discretisation comes from and has never asked whether exactly one is needed | `LIVE` — no experiment compares them: A's six-hypothesis menu and B's typed superset are evaluated on different task sets (400+400 vs 5 hand-picked), and neither reports an ablation in which the parse policy is the only thing varied. **A quantity that would decide it exists and is unreported**: how often A's top-ranked parse is the one its own eventual solution needed |
| T192 | **What licenses a candidate analogical inference — its coherence with the target, or a causal model carried over from the source?** | **Coherence.** [[wiki/concepts/analogical-mapping.md]]'s acceptance test accepts a copy-with-substitution hypothesis `h` iff `sim(C_h, C_target-episode) > sim(C_h, C_s)` for every mapped sub-element `s` — the hypothesis must fit the whole target better than any of its parts. Computed from the same codes as the mapping, needs no external checker, no causal annotation and no domain knowledge, and it removed every distractor in the Water-Flow/Heat-Flow case for free (Slipchenko & Rachkovskij 2009) | **A causal model, and similarity is actively misleading.** Analogical inference strength tracks the source's cause–effect structure, not overall resemblance: a stated *causal* link between two source properties strengthens the transferred inference over a mere conjunction, and weakening the link to "develops before" shrinks the boost (Lassaline 1996); most sharply, when the source shows an effect *despite* a preventive cause, people judge the effect **more** likely in a target that **lacks** the preventer — the inference strengthens while source–target similarity falls (Lee & Holyoak 2008). Holyoak, Lee & Lu 2010 formalise it: the source's causal network plus the mapping is the input to copy-with-substitution, and the output is an elaborated *causal model* of the target, queried afterwards for arbitrary inferences | Decides what analogical transfer moves and what a model must store per episode. Under A an episode is a bag of relations and inference is a coherence filter — cheap, self-contained, and it will accept a plausible-looking transfer that no causal structure supports while rejecting the preventer case outright. Under B every episode must carry an annotated causal graph, which no wiki mechanism discovers ([[wiki/concepts/causal-model-building.md]], G16) — so B is right about the phenomenon and has no story about where its input comes from, while A runs today on representations that are themselves hand-authored | `LIVE` |
| T193 | **Is the developmental relational shift caused by accumulating relational knowledge, or by maturing executive capacity?** | **Knowledge.** Analogical competence is present from infancy — some analogical ability at 1 year — and what grows is knowledge of the relations being mapped (Goswami 1992, 2001). Adults show the same dependence: domain expertise predicts analogical performance *within* that domain, and experts are more likely than novices to retrieve remote analogs on relational grounds (Novick & Holyoak 1991) | **Executive maturation.** Preschoolers' relational responding falls under exactly the two manipulations that load the prefrontal cortex — the presence of a perceptually similar distractor (inhibition) and the requirement to integrate two relations (binding capacity) — and only by 13–14 years, roughly when prefrontal maturation is substantial, is the relational response reliable with both present (Richland, Morrison & Holyoak 2006). Children with autism matched to controls on executive function show comparable trends (Dawson et al. 2007; Morsanyi & Holyoak 2010), and the adult analogues of both manipulations (dual task, anxiety, frontal damage) reproduce the child pattern in healthy adults | Decides which parameter to scale in a model that reasons better with age or training. Under A the fix is vocabulary — more and better relation types ([[wiki/concepts/latent-graph-discovery.md]] G4) — and capacity is a constant. Under B the fix is the binding budget and the inhibition strength, which is a *capacity* parameter no wiki architecture exposes at all ([[wiki/entities/lisa.md]] is the only model that derives one). Holyoak's own reading is that both are real and neither is sufficient, which makes this the rare tension where the discriminating experiment exists — hold relational knowledge fixed and vary load — and has been run only in the adult direction | `LIVE` |
| T194 | **Is approaching a memory's capacity a failure mode or the mechanism that produces generalisation?** | **Failure.** Every capacity account in the wiki treats the load limit as the point where the store stops working: Hopfield-class recall collapses catastrophically past `p_max ≈ k·C/(a ln(1/a))` ([[wiki/entities/rolls-treves-hippocampal-model.md]]), scaffold stores degrade resolution ([[wiki/entities/vector-hash.md]]), and the design goal in each case is to stay below the number ([[wiki/architectural-gaps.md]] G42) | **The mechanism.** Jaeger's **class learning effect**: a 200-unit reservoir loaded with `k` patterns drawn from one 2-parameter family and then cued with patterns *never loaded* from the same family recalls the novel ones worse than the loaded ones only while `k` is small; **above a threshold the two accuracies become equal**, which the author reads as the network abandoning rote storage of instances and representing the parametric class instead. Loading pressure is what forces the abstraction ([[wiki/entities/conceptor.md]]) | Sets whether a fast store should be sized *below* the item count it must hold or deliberately *above* it. If B holds, a store operated near saturation is a schema learner and a store operated well below it is a lookup table — which makes capacity a curriculum parameter (G32) rather than a safety margin, and makes the quota a control signal rather than an alarm | `LIVE` — B is one demonstration, on one 2-parameter synthetic family, at `N = 200`, with no independent measure of what was abstracted and no comparison against a store trained on the family directly. But the two positions are not incompatible in the way they look: A measures recall of *stored items* and B measures recall of *unstored members of the same family*, so the same overload can be both a loss of item fidelity and a gain in class coverage, and no experiment here reports both curves for one store |
