The best *spike-native* end-to-end number is 98.88% on MNIST via surrogate gradients, which is offline BPTT wearing a membrane potential | Decides what the SNN literature's headline numbers license. If B, then every conversion result is an argument that a rate specification is *sufficient* for those tasks — a datum for T1 position A supplied by the neuromorphic literature itself — and the case for spiking substrates has to be made on energy, on delayed-error learning, or on representations rate models cannot express ([[wiki/entities/spiking-tem.md]]), never on benchmark accuracy | `LEANING B` — the split is the review's own finding, not an outside criticism; A is a summary that averages over the split |
| T232 | **Within the timing camp, which temporal code actually carries the information — and can the question be settled empirically?** | **Order, not latency: rank-order coding.** Its capacity is combinatorially larger — `log₂(N_spikes!)` bits against a count rate's `log₂(N_spikes + 1)`, six-fold at ten spikes and unbounded thereafter — and it is the only globally-referenced scheme that needs no global reference, because the brain has no access to stimulus onset (Rullen & Thorpe, in Auge et al. 2021). Time-to-first-spike is the engineering favourite and inherits that defect | **Neither — count rate wins in the actual measurement.** Rolls, Franco, Aggelopoulos & Jerez analysed spikes in inferior temporal visual cortex and report that count rate in a short window is *fast* and carries **more** information than either time-to-first-spike or rank order, because spontaneous firing corrupts both order and latency. Li & Tsien push a third position: that same spontaneous activity is not noise but inter-spike-interval structure, and rare long silences carry more information than high-activity periods. **And the survey's methodological verdict undercuts all three** — task accuracy cannot arbitrate, since published comparisons vary architecture and learning rule together with the code, while bits-per-spike cannot arbitrate either, because a capacity is only realisable by a reader that can decode it | Directly under [[wiki/concepts/temporal-coding.md]] and [[wiki/entities/spiking-neural-networks.md]], which argue *that* timing carries content and never say *which* timing variable. It decides what a spiking reasoning architecture should put on its wires, and it sets the terms for gap G77: A's argument for order is an argument from the missing time origin, B's argument against it is an argument from noise. The one clean within-pipeline comparison available goes to latency — same sensor, same network, same task: rank order 95.2%, time-to-first-spike 100%, the loss attributed to jitter permuting near-simultaneous spikes | `LIVE` — and structurally so: no instrument in the wiki scores a code independently of its reader |
| T233 | **Is an adaptation variable a memory or the thing that prevents one?** | **It is the store.** In the LSNN/DEXAT line a per-neuron adaptive threshold *is* the working memory: a 10-LIF + 10-DEXAT network holds a 1200 ms STORE-RECALL delay at 95% in 50 training iterations, memory span scales with the adaptation time constant and with baseline excitability, and the item is read with no retrieval operation at all — the memory is the depressed firing rate the next input meets (Ganguly et al. 2024 reviewing Bellec et al. 2018/2020, Shaban et al. 2021, Fitz et al.; [[wiki/concepts/spike-frequency-adaptation.md]]) | **It is the destabiliser.** The wiki's two prior users of the identical equation subtract it from the drive precisely to *stop* anything persisting: in [[wiki/entities/trnn.md]] `τ_v dV/dt = −V + mr` is what forces working memory out of a fixed point and into a moving trajectory of sequentially peaking units, and in [[wiki/entities/adaptive-cann.md]] the same `τ_v dV/dt = −V + mU` destroys the stationary bump into a travelling wave once `m > τ/τ_v` | Whether a builder adds adaptation to *get* memory or to *break* it. The reconciliation is visible and nobody states it: A-CANN supplies a threshold, so the two readings are **parameter regimes of one mechanism** — below it the variable is a leaky trace that outlasts its input (a store), above it a positive-feedback destabiliser (a sweep generator). What is missing is the same threshold for the discrete case: no one has written down when a DEXAT network stops holding and starts sweeping, and the interference that long adaptation causes between stored items (Fitz et al.) is plausibly that boundary seen from below | `LIVE` — but reducible: the disagreement is about `m` and `τ_v/τ`, not about what adaptation does |
